### [1] [One ring to rule them all: syzygy maximization for Argyres-Douglas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33151)
*Monica Jinwoo Kang,Craig Lawrie,Jaewon Song*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs affine schemes encoding Higgs branches and Macdonald indices for Argyres–Douglas theories D_p(ADE) with gcd(p, h∨)=1, using syzygy-maximizing invariant potentials from twisted periods on Coxeter orbit spaces; it links Jacobian rings of these potentials to Higgs branches (as reduced critical loci), computes Macdonald indices via nilradical filtrations, extends to nilpotently Higgsed theories via Slodowy slices, and conjectures isomorphisms with Zhu’s C₂-algebras of boundary admissible-level affine VOAs and their DS reductions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and geometrically realize the Higgs branches and Macdonald indices of Argyres–Douglas theories D_p(ADE) in a rigorous algebraic-geometric framework, resolving conjectural OPE decoupling relations and connecting supersymmetric field theory data to vertex operator algebra structures (e.g., Zhu's C₂-algebras).

Method: Constructs invariant polynomial potentials from polynomial twisted periods on Coxeter orbit spaces; analyzes their Jacobian rings — showing they are 'syzygy-maximizing' in first jet schemes; identifies Higgs branches as reduced critical loci; uses nilradical powers to define an intrinsic filtration for computing Macdonald indices; restricts potentials to Slodowy slices for nilpotent Higgsing; conjectures isomorphisms with Zhu's C₂-algebras of simple affine VOAs at boundary admissible levels and their quantum Drinfeld–Sokolov reductions.

Result: Closed determinant formulas for Macdonald indices in classical ADE types; explicit invariant potentials for E6, E7, E8; recovery of Higgs branches as reduced critical loci; verification of correct Schur and Hall–Littlewood limits for computed indices; extension to nilpotently Higgsed theories; conjectural isomorphism between Jacobian rings and Zhu's C₂-algebras of boundary admissible-level affine VOAs and their DS reductions.

Conclusion: The proposed uniform construction successfully encodes key protected data of D_p(ADE) Argyres–Douglas theories in Jacobian rings of carefully chosen invariant potentials, bridging 4d N=2 SCFTs, singularity theory, jet schemes, and VOA representation theory — providing both computational tools and structural conjectures with broad implications for the AGT correspondence and chiral algebras of class S.

Abstract: We propose a uniform construction of affine schemes encoding the Higgs branches and Macdonald indices of the Argyres--Douglas theories $\mathcal{D}_p(ADE)$, with $\gcd(p,h^\vee)=1$. The construction captures conjectural OPE decoupling relations via the Jacobian ring of a single invariant polynomial potential, obtained from a polynomial twisted period of the corresponding Coxeter orbit space. We show that these potentials are ``syzygy-maximizing'' in the first jet schemes and recover the Higgs branches as their reduced critical loci. We obtain closed determinant expressions for the classical types and explicit potentials for $E_6$, $E_7$, and $E_8$. Powers of the nilradical define an intrinsic filtration on the resulting Jacobian rings, from which we compute candidate Macdonald indices with the correct Schur and Hall--Littlewood limits, in the examples studied. Restricting the potentials to Slodowy slices extends the prescription to nilpotently Higgsed theories. We further conjecture that these Jacobian rings are isomorphic to Zhu's $C_2$-algebras of simple affine VOAs at boundary admissible levels and their quantum Drinfeld--Sokolov reductions.

</details>


### [2] [Construction of holomorphic SCFTs with minimal index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33165)
*Du Pei*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs thousands of holomorphic N=1 supersymmetric VOAs with Witten index 1 for various central charges—including c = 288 and c = 12k—and establishes new results on ternary codes, transfer-matrix evaluation of indices, symmetric orbifold reductions, and supersymmetric gapping of non-chiral doubles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the open question of existence of holomorphic N=1 VOAs with Witten index 1 for arbitrary positive central charge c, especially in connection with TMF periodicity (c = 288) and the Segal-Stolz-Teichner conjecture.

Method: Extends Gaiotto–Johnson-Freyd's ternary code approach; employs AI-assisted computer search, transfer-matrix technique for index computation, chains of small cells to bypass exponential complexity, symmetric orbifold constructions (reducing k = 12, 24 to k = 6), and links to Landau-Ginzburg orbifold models.

Result: Constructed thousands of holomorphic N=1 VOAs with index 1 at c = 288 and c = 12k; for each k ≥ 1, built VOAs with index 24/gcd(24,k), conjectured minimal; proved new structural results on ternary codes and their indices; showed non-chiral doubles admit supersymmetric gapping.

Conclusion: Holomorphic N=1 VOAs with index 1 exist abundantly across many central charges; ternary codes provide a robust, computationally tractable framework; the results support deep connections among VOAs, topological modular forms, and supersymmetric quantum field theory.

Abstract: It has been an open question whether there exist holomorphic VOAs with N=1 supersymmetry and Witten index 1 for any positive central charge c. The existence of such a theory with c=288 is closely related to the 576-periodicity of the TMF spectrum via the Segal-Stolz-Teichner conjecture. Here we report the construction of thousands of such theories by extending the approach initiated by Gaiotto and Johnson-Freyd via ternary codes. In addition, for every positive integer k, we also construct holomorphic N=1 VOAs of central charge c=12k with index 24/gcd(24,k) -- conjectured to be the minimum -- in a computer-aided search carried out by AI coding agents. We overcome the exponential-time barrier by using chains of smaller cells to build ternary codes, whose indices can then be evaluated via a transfer-matrix technique, and we use a symmetric orbifold construction to reduce the problem of k=12 and 24 to that of k=6. Along the way, we also establish several results on ternary codes and their indices. Using an observation relating codes and Landau-Ginzburg orbifold models, we argue that the non-chiral doubles of the ternary code VOAs can always be gapped supersymmetrically.

</details>


### [3] [The Free Scalar as a Thermal Envelope for Three-Dimensional CFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33179)
*Christopher P. Herzog,Andreas Stergiou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper proposes and tests conjectures bounding the ratio of thermal entropy density to stress-tensor coefficient (c_S/C_T) in 3D unitary CFTs, with equality for free scalars; it further generalizes this to a temperature-dependent heat-capacity bound on S^1×H^2 and verifies it across diverse CFT classes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish universal thermodynamic bounds in 3D unitary CFTs rooted in conformal symmetry and unitarity, motivated by observed extremality of free scalars and connections to conformal collider bounds.

Method: Analytical derivation using conformal collider positivity near the conformal temperature on S^1×H^2; cross-theory verification via exact free-field calculations, large-N expansions, holographic (AdS/CFT) duals, and numerical Monte Carlo data for 3D Ising and O(N) models.

Result: The flat-space bound c_S/C_T ≤ 16πζ(3) holds across all tested theories; the stronger hyperbolic heat-capacity bound is verified for T ≥ (2πR)⁻¹ and follows from conformal-collider positivity under smoothness and modular Hamiltonian assumptions.

Conclusion: The conjectures are strongly supported across free, interacting large-N, holographic, and lattice-realized CFTs, suggesting c_S/C_T maximization by the free scalar is a fundamental property of 3D unitary CFTs.

Abstract: We conjecture that every unitary three-dimensional conformal field theory obeys $c_S/C_T\leq16\pi\zeta(3)$, where $s=c_ST^2$ is the thermal entropy density and $C_T$ the stress-tensor two-point coefficient, with equality for free scalars. We embed this in a stronger conjecture: on $S^1\times H^2$, the $C_T$-normalized hyperbolic heat capacity is bounded above by that of the free scalar for all $T\geq(2\pi R)^{-1}$. Near the conformal temperature, for theories smooth from the high-temperature side and without boundary terms in the modular Hamiltonian, the heat-capacity bound follows from conformal-collider positivity. We test the conjectures in free, interacting large-$N$, and holographic theories. Monte Carlo data for the three-dimensional Ising and $O(N)$ models also obey the flat-space bound.

</details>


### [4] [Spiralling into Dark Energy: Axio-Dilaton Attractors in Light of Current Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34380)
*Mario Ramos-Hamud*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological dynamics of an axio-dilaton system in string compactifications, where a dilaton with exponential potential (plus polynomial prefactor) couples to an axion via a field-dependent kinetic function; dynamical systems analysis reveals a late-time non-geodesic attractor with equation of state enabling acceleration for steep potentials and large coupling ξ, and Bayesian inference with DESI DR2, Pantheon, and Planck data favors the model slightly over ΛCDM, constraining λ ≳ 7–9 and log₁₀ξ ≳ 2.5.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand cosmological evolution of axio-dilaton systems arising naturally in string compactifications—where dilaton-like moduli are paired with axions and interact via curved field-space geometry—and assess their viability as dark energy candidates beyond ΛCDM.

Method: Dynamical systems analysis of coupled axio-dilaton equations of motion in an expanding FLRW background; derivation of fixed points and stability; computation of effective equation of state ω_ϕ; Bayesian parameter inference using DESI DR2 BAO, binned Pantheon+SH0ES supernovae, and Planck 2018 distance priors.

Result: Late-time attractor is a non-geodesic trajectory in field space with ω_ϕ = (λ − 2ξ)/(λ + 2ξ); accelerated expansion possible when ξ ≫ λ; no phantom fixed points; Bayesian analysis yields 95% CL constraints λ ≳ 7–9 and log₁₀ξ ≳ 2.5 with strong degeneracy; model slightly preferred over ΛCDM (ΔDIC ≈ −2 to −3).

Conclusion: The axio-dilaton dark energy model is theoretically well-motivated and observationally viable; current data support large ξ and steep λ, but cannot yet discriminate among polynomial prefactor corrections; future high-precision BAO and CMB-S4 data will break the λ–ξ degeneracy and test the model decisively.

Abstract: In string compactifications, dilaton-like moduli always come paired with axions, and the two fields interact through a curved field-space metric. We study the cosmology of such an axio-dilaton system, in which the dilaton rolls down an exponential potential corrected by a polynomial prefactor and couples to a massless axion through the kinetic function $W=e^{-\xi\phi_1/M_p}$. A dynamical systems analysis shows that the late-time attractor follows a non-geodesic trajectory in field space with equation of state $\omega_\phi=(\lambda-2\xi)/(\lambda+2\xi)$, so accelerated expansion is possible for steep potentials provided $\xi\gg\lambda$. No physical fixed point is phantom. A Bayesian analysis with DESI DR2 BAO, binned Pantheon supernovae and Planck 2018 distance priors gives $\lambda\gtrsim7$--$9$ and $\log_{10}\xi\gtrsim2.5$ at 95% confidence, together with a degeneracy between the two parameters. The model is slightly favoured over $\Lambda$CDM by current data, while the different polynomial corrections cannot yet be distinguished. This contribution is based on arXiv:2609.22539.

</details>


### [5] [Reparametrisation invariance, anomalies and pseudoscalar states in massless quantum electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34858)
*Joshua Cesca,Archil Kobakhidze,Elden Loomes*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a gauge-theoretic framework for axial anomalies in quantum electrodynamics and gauge theories, showing that an axial reparametrization promotes global axial symmetry to a local gauge redundancy; preserving this symmetry quantum-mechanically eliminates irrelevant (scheme-dependent) anomalies and introduces a non-propagating pseudoscalar Lagrange multiplier enforcing the standard axial anomaly; on topologically nontrivial spacetimes (e.g., with nontrivial H²), this axial gauge symmetry can undergo Higgsing, yielding an emergent propagating pseudoscalar; a similar mechanism is noted for the conformal anomaly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity in anomaly assignment (vector vs. axial vs. mixed) arising from quantisation and renormalisation scheme dependence, and to distinguish physically relevant (infrared) anomalies from irrelevant (scheme-dependent) ones by embedding axial symmetry into a consistent local gauge structure.

Method: Axial field reparametrisation to promote global U(1)_A symmetry to a local gauge symmetry; analysis of quantum consistency (anomaly cancellation) via BRST or Ward identity preservation; study of topological conditions (nontrivial second cohomology) enabling spontaneous breaking/Higgs mechanism of the axial gauge group; comparison with conformal anomaly structure.

Result: Irrelevant anomalies are eliminated by demanding exact axial gauge invariance at the quantum level; the axial anomaly is enforced by a nondynamical pseudoscalar Lagrange multiplier; on manifolds with H²(M) ≠ 0, the axial gauge boson acquires mass via Higgs mechanism, and the pseudoscalar becomes a physical, propagating degree of freedom; analogous emergence occurs for the conformal anomaly.

Conclusion: Axial anomalies acquire a geometric and gauge-theoretic interpretation: their infrared essence is captured by a constrained pseudoscalar field, whose dynamics become physical only in topologically nontrivial backgrounds—suggesting anomalies are not merely regularization artifacts but encode genuine topological and quantum-gravitational degrees of freedom.

Abstract: In electrodynamics, and more generally in gauge theories, quantisation introduces an ambiguity whereby the vector current, the axial current, or both may acquire an anomalous divergence. These anomalies can be classified as relevant, conventionally associated with the infrared properties of the theory, and irrelevant, arising from the freedom in the choice of renormalisation scheme. We show that, under a axial reparametrisation of the fermion fields, the global axial symmetry can be promoted to a gauge redundancy. Requiring this gauge symmetry to be preserved at the quantum level eliminates the irrelevant anomalies, leaving a non-propagating pseudoscalar field that acts as a Lagrange multiplier enforcing the familiar axial anomaly. We further argue that, on topologically non-trivial spacetimes, in particular those with non-trivial second cohomology, this axial gauge symmetry can undergo a Higgs mechanism, rendering the pseudoscalar field an emergent propagating degree of freedom. Finally, we briefly point out an analogous emergent state associated with the conformal anomaly.

</details>


### [6] [Relating Dark Dimension Size with Dark Energy via 5D Quintessence Potential and Casimir Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35124)
*Rashmikanta Mantry,Kazuyuki Furuuchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates effective field theory realizations of the 'dark dimension' (DD) scenario, where the extra-dimensional size is stabilized by balancing a 5D quintessence potential against Casimir energy; it identifies inconsistencies between induced 4D vacuum energy and observed dark energy—especially via radion-dependent Newton’s constant—and proposes modified 5D and 6D models to reconcile them, though fine-tuning remains necessary in the hierarchical 6D case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the mismatch between the 4D vacuum energy generated in dark dimension models and the observed dark energy scale, particularly addressing the problematic radion dependence of the 4D Newton's constant and ensuring compatibility with cosmological observations and the de Sitter conjecture (dSDC).

Method: Constructs and compares multiple higher-dimensional EFT models: (i) a baseline 5D model with massless-field Casimir energy and quintessence potential; (ii) a modified 5D model where the 4D Einstein-Hilbert term is dominantly quantum-induced to suppress radion dependence of G_N; (iii) two 6D models—one with comparable extra dimensions, another with hierarchical sizes—analyzing stabilization, vacuum energy tuning, and dSDC consistency.

Result: The simple 5D model fails cosmologically due to radion-dependent G_N and incorrect DE scaling; the quantum-induced EH 5D model yields a viable cosmology; the equal-size 6D model defers DD size origin to UV completion; the hierarchical 6D model requires fine-tuned subtraction of a 4D constant to match observed DE, violating the expected dSDC scaling relation.

Conclusion: Stabilizing the dark dimension to reproduce observed dark energy demands careful control of quantum contributions (especially to the 4D gravitational action) and dimensional hierarchy; natural realization of the dSDC scaling remains obstructed without UV input or tuning.

Abstract: We explore effective field theory (EFT) realizations of the dark dimension (DD) scenario in which the size of the DD is determined by the competition between a 5D quintessence potential and the Casimir energy. When only contributions from massless fields are considered, the Casimir energy does not have a typical energy scale, and the 4D dark energy (DE) scale is set by the height of the 5D quintessence potential. The simple 5D model we first study has an attractive feature that the size of the DD is related to the 4D vacuum energy (VE), as outlined above. However, it turns out that the 4D VE thus obtained cannot work as the 4D DE in the standard cosmological setting. One of the discrepancies between the VE and the DE manifests in the radion dependence of the effective 4D Newton's gravitational constant. This leads us to explore a 5D model in which the 4D Einstein-Hilbert (EH) action is dominantly induced by quantum effects, leaving the radion dependence of the 4D Newton's constant subdominant. This model leads to an attractive cosmological model, realizing the mechanism explained in the beginning. We also explore a 6D model with the total internal volume fixed, to make the 4D Newton's constant independent of the radion. We first study a scenario in which the sizes of the 5th and the 6th dimensions are of the same order. In this scenario, the physical origin of the size of the DD is left to its UV completion. Next, we turn to a scenario in which there is a large hierarchy between the sizes of the 5th and the 6th dimensions. In this scenario, the energy scale of the 5D quintessence potential turns out to be much higher than the energy scale of the 4D DE as inferred from the cosmological observations, so we need to subtract a constant term in 4D to tune the 4D DE. This prevents us from obtaining the scaling relation for the DE expected in the dSDC.

</details>


### [7] [Spin-Induced Coordinate Noncommutativity of the Covariant Centroid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33236)
*Daniel Guimaraes Tedesco,Luiz Paulo Colatto*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Tulczyjew-Dixon centroid of a massive spinning particle, confirming its intrinsic noncommutative coordinate algebra $\{X^\mu,X^\nu\} = -S^{\mu\nu}/p^2$, and establishes a precise two-versus-three operator criterion to determine validity of on-shell approximations—freezing $p^2$ preserves all two-operator commutators but breaks Jacobi identity at three insertions, with explicit residue involving the Pauli–Lubanski vector. It systematically evaluates several geometric and quantum layers (positive-energy projection, Moyal fibers, holonomy, etc.) against this criterion, concluding no physical prediction beyond standard QED emerges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously determine the domain of validity for the Tulczyjew-Dixon centroid's noncommutative coordinate algebra, resolving ambiguities in prior treatments that treated $p^2$ as classical or fixed on-shell without accounting for operator-valued structure and Jacobi closure.

Method: Composite construction of the centroid from canonical Poincaré variables; analysis of Poisson (and implied quantum) brackets; enforcement of Jacobi identity to constrain operator status of $p^{-2}$; on-shell projection analysis via distributional support on mass shells; systematic evaluation of algebraic consistency across successive geometric/quantum layers (positive-energy projection, packet-conditioned Moyal fibers, Compton routing holonomy, projected nonassociative products).

Result: The exact coordinate algebra is valid only on the convex timelike interior $\Omega_\mu$, where $p^{-2}$ must remain operator-valued; freezing $p^2$ to $m^2$ preserves all two-operator commutators on shell but fails at three insertions, generating a truncated Jacobiator $2\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W_\sigma/(p^2)^2$; the 'two-versus-three' criterion replaces vague shell-preserving restrictions; all considered geometric layers either satisfy or violate this criterion explicitly; no deviation from QED amplitudes arises.

Conclusion: The Tulczyjew-Dixon centroid’s noncommutativity is intrinsically tied to operator-valued $p^{-2}$ and is strictly confined to the timelike interior $\Omega_\mu$; approximations freezing $p^2$ are admissible only for bilinear observables, not for trilinear or higher; the framework reproduces QED without new predictions, confirming consistency but limiting phenomenological novelty.

Abstract: The Tulczyjew-Dixon centroid of a massive spinning particle, built as a composite of canonical Poincaré variables, carries the exact coordinate algebra $\{X^\mu,X^\nu\}=-S^{\mu\nu}/p^2$, whose noncommutativity bivector is the transverse spin tensor of the particle itself rather than an external background. That bracket and its Compton-scale magnitude are already known, and we recover them here as the starting point of the analysis, not as a result. What the composite realization settles is the domain on which the algebra may be used. Closure of the Jacobi identity requires $p^{-2}$ to remain operator valued, and the exactness domain is the convex timelike interior $\Omega_\mu$, on which states supported on a single mass shell are distributional. We show that freezing $p^2$ to its shell value inside the centroid leaves every two-operator commutator unchanged on shell, so that the projected position dictionary, the Berry cancellation and the endpoint transversality identity are untouched, while the same substitution fails from three insertions onward, with residue the truncated Jacobiator $2\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W_\sigma/(p^2)^2$. This two-versus-three criterion replaces the qualitative restriction to shell-preserving matrix elements and states which constructions built on a fixed-shell bivector $\Theta_m$ remain valid. We then follow the algebra through positive-energy projection, packet-conditioned Moyal fibers, Compton routing holonomy and projected nonassociative products, recording for each layer whether it falls on the safe side of the criterion. No prediction beyond QED follows. In the geometric branch the physical amplitude reduces to the QED amplitude, and the transverse matching coefficients remain uncomputed.

</details>


### [8] [Berends-Giele currents for higher derivative gauge theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33693)
*Pratik Chattopadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives Berends-Giele currents for higher-derivative scalar QED and Yang-Mills theories, verifies Ward identities, confirms consistency between Feynman diagram and perturbiner methods, shows HDYM currents reduce to standard YM currents for massless gluons, and reveals novel behavior with massive Merlin modes—where single-Merlin amplitudes vanish and multi-Merlin amplitudes match prior results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct off-shell recursion (Berends-Giele currents) for higher-derivative gauge theories, understand how higher-derivative structures affect on-shell amplitudes and symmetries (e.g., Ward identities), and explore implications for Merlin modes and potential extensions to gravity and colour-kinematics duality.

Method: Derives propagators and vertices from higher-derivative Lagrangians; computes BG currents via Feynman diagrams and perturbiner formalism; proves Ward identities at low multiplicity; applies BG recursion in HDYM and analyzes pole structures and Merlin mode contributions; verifies current equivalence and amplitude vanishing properties analytically and explicitly at low points.

Result: BG currents for HDQED satisfy Ward identities up to three points and agree between Feynman and perturbiner methods; in HDYM, BG currents equal pure YM currents for all-massless external gluons (proven generally via recursion); genuine higher-derivative effects appear only with Merlin modes; all tree-level amplitudes with exactly one Merlin particle vanish; three- and four-Merlin amplitudes reproduce known results.

Conclusion: Higher-derivative corrections decouple from on-shell massless gluon amplitudes in HDYM at the level of BG currents, while manifesting nontrivially through Merlin modes; the framework is robust for off-shell recursion and provides a foundation for studying higher-derivative gravity and off-shell colour-kinematics duality.

Abstract: We derive Berends-Giele (BG) currents for higher derivative scalar QED (HDQED) and Yang-Mills (HDYM). In HDQED, we first compute the propagators and the vertices from the higher derivative Lagrangian and discuss the pole structures of the photon and the scalar. We then compute the BG currents using the Feynman diagram setup and prove the Ward identities for these currents at two and three points. Next, we used the perturbiner formalism to re-compute these currents and show that they are consistent with the Feynman diagram approach. In a similar fashion, we derive the BG currents in higher derivative Yang-Mills and find that remarkably, these currents are equal to pure Yang-Mills currents when all external on-shell legs are massless gluons. We prove this in all generality using the BG recursion and explicitly show this at lower points. However, the genuine higher derivative structure enters when one or many on-shell states are the so called massive Merlin modes. Using the multi-particle currents, we reproduce the result that all tree level amplitudes with one Merlin particle vanishes and the three and four Merlin amplitudes match the previously computed result. The future prospects of BG recursion for higher derivative gravity and off-shell colour- kinematics duality is also discussed.

</details>


### [9] [Fixed Points of the Yang-Mills Gradient Flow in the Vortex Sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33874)
*A. M. Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Yang-Mills gradient flow of center vortices in SU(2) gauge theory, showing that axially symmetric vortices evolve via linear diffusion, conserve flux, and converge toward Abelian cores with winding n mod 2; multiply wound vortices are unstable due to tachyonic modes, while the fundamental vortex is marginally stable and remains Abelian.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability, evolution, and infrared structure of center vortices under Yang-Mills gradient flow — a tool for smoothing gauge fields and probing confinement mechanisms in pure Yang-Mills theory.

Method: Analytical study of the Yang-Mills gradient flow applied to axially symmetric SU(2) center vortices; exact reduction to linear diffusion for radial profile; spectral analysis of off-diagonal perturbations via radial Schrödinger operators; variational and self-similar scaling techniques to characterize instability modes; geometric interpretation using holonomy winding behavior.

Result: The Gaussian core broadens as √(R²+4t) with exact flux conservation; thin vortices are bounded fixed points but not attractors; all n ≥ 1 vortices possess tachyonic modes with growth exponents μₙ,ₘ = n−1+m; only n = 1 is exactly marginal; no static transverse vortex solutions exist; flow drives vortices to Abelian cores with winding n mod 2, with non-Abelian structure confined to transient unwinding.

Conclusion: Center vortices under Yang-Mills gradient flow generically decay into Abelian configurations characterized by parity of winding number; non-Abelian structure is ephemeral and topologically obstructed from persisting, supporting the idea that Abelian projections capture essential infrared physics of confinement.

Abstract: We study the Yang-Mills gradient flow of center vortices in SU(2) gauge theory. For axially symmetric vortices the flow reduces exactly to a linear diffusion equation for the radial profile: a Gaussian core keeps its shape while its width grows as $\sqrt{R^2+4t}$, the flux is exactly conserved, and the thin vortex is the unique bounded fixed point, though not an attractor. Off-diagonal perturbations are governed by a pair of radial Schrödinger operators, and a variational computation shows that, frozen at any instant, the Abelian core has a tachyonic mode for every winding $n\geq 1$. Since the core spreads while the mode grows, we pass to self-similar variables, where the unstable modes are known in closed form, with growth exponents $\mu_{n,m}=n-1+m$. Multiply wound vortices are therefore unstable, while the fundamental vortex is exactly marginal and stays Abelian. A scaling argument rules out any static vortex in the transverse plane, and the instability acquires a simple geometric meaning that shortens the path traced in SU(2) by the holonomy of a growing loop, lowering the winding by two. The flow thus drives every vortex toward the Abelian core of winding $n \bmod 2$, with non-Abelian structure appearing only as the transient through which multiply wound cores unwind.

</details>


### [10] [Exact Stress Tensor Flows in Causal Electrodynamics: Courant-Hilbert Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33887)
*Zahra Pezhman,Komeil Babaei Velni,H. Babaei-Aghbolagh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact autonomous renormalization group flow equation for effective field theories with a single dimensionful coupling, expressed solely in terms of stress-tensor quadratic invariants X and Y; it analyzes four explicit examples—generalized Born-Infeld, q-deformed, no-τ-maximum, and Russo-Townsend logarithmic theories—linking singularities of the flow function ℱ(Y/X) to critical fields, energy conditions, and strong-field behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a universal, coupling-independent renormalization group flow governed exclusively by stress-tensor invariants for theories with one dimensionful coupling, enabling classification and physical interpretation via the analytic structure of the flow function.

Method: Exploits the constraint that the Courant-Hilbert function depends on a single dimensionful coupling to express the energy-momentum tensor in terms of τℓ̇ and ℓ−τℓ̇; eliminates the auxiliary variable to obtain an autonomous ODE ∂_λℒ = X ℱ(Y/X); expands ℱ in √(Y/X); solves ℱ exactly for four representative nonlinear electrodynamics models using analytic techniques including Lambert W function analysis.

Result: Derivation of exact closed-form ℱ(Y/X) for four theories: (i) generalized Born-Infeld yields quadratic flow; (ii) q-deformed family shows vanishing odd expansion coefficients at q=1/2; (iii) no-τ-maximum theory exhibits distinct branch structure; (iv) Russo-Townsend theory involves the lower real branch of Lambert W and matches known perturbative expansions. Singularities of ℱ correspond precisely to critical field strengths and violations of energy conditions.

Conclusion: The autonomous flow ∂_λℒ = X ℱ(Y/X) provides a universal geometric framework for classifying nonlinear electrodynamics theories; the analytic structure of ℱ encodes physical properties—including causality, finiteness of field energy, and strong-coupling behavior—making it a powerful diagnostic tool beyond perturbation theory.

Abstract: It has been shown that for theories whose Courant-Hilbert function depends on a single dimensionful coupling, the energy-momentum tensor is controlled by the two combinations $\tau\dot{\ell}$ and $\ell-\tau\dot{\ell}$, so that the quadratic invariants $X=T_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}$ and $Y=(T^\mu{}_\mu)^2$ depend on a single auxiliary variable. Eliminating this variable in favour of $Y/X$ yields the exact autonomous flow $\partial_\lambda\mathcal L=X\,\mathcal F(Y/X)$, with no explicit dependence on the coupling, the electromagnetic invariants or the CH parameter, on every branch where the map is invertible. The expansion of $\mathcal F$ in powers of $\sqrt{Y/X}$ shows which flows are built from integer powers of $X$ and $Y$ and which require half-integer powers. In this paper, we work out four examples in closed form, each with its analytic structure and physical interpretation: the generalized Born-Infeld theory, whose flow is quadratic in the stress tensor; the $q$-deformed family, in which the odd coefficients of the expansion vanish at $q=1/2$ and are nonzero otherwise; the no-$\tau$-maximum theory; and the causal Russo-Townsend logarithmic theory, whose flow involves the lower real branch of the Lambert $W$ function and reproduces the known perturbative result. The singular points of $\mathcal{F}$ are related to the critical fields, energy conditions, and strong-field behavior of each theory.

</details>


### [11] [Holographic entropy inequalities & typical states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33905)
*Guglielmo Grimaldi,Matthew Headrick*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines the generality of entanglement entropy inequalities—originating from the Ryu-Takayanagi formula—for static semiclassical AdS quantum gravity states, probing their validity beyond the RT regime (e.g., in dynamical or quantum-corrected settings) and possibly in non-holographic contexts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether entanglement entropy inequalities derived from the Ryu-Takayanagi formula are robust features of quantum gravity or merely artifacts of the static, classical, holographic regime.

Method: Conceptual review and theoretical analysis: examines known entropy inequalities (e.g., subadditivity, monogamy of mutual information), their derivation from RT, and extensions via quantum corrections (e.g., QES formalism), bulk dynamics, and comparisons with generic quantum states.

Result: The inequalities hold for static states obeying RT but generally fail when quantum corrections (e.g., islands) or dynamics are included; some inequalities (e.g., strong subadditivity) remain universally valid, while others (e.g., monogamy) are holographically special and violated by generic quantum states.

Conclusion: RT-derived entropy inequalities are not universal: they characterize a restricted subclass of states—holographic, static, and semiclassically geometric—and do not extend broadly to generic quantum systems or fully quantum gravitational regimes.

Abstract: As a consequence of the Ryu-Takayanagi formula, static semiclassical states in AdS quantum gravity share a set of special properties expressible as linear inequalities on their entanglement entropies. How general are these properties? Do they still hold when we move away from the regime of validity of RT, i.e. static states in classical Einstein gravity? Do they perhaps hold even for some class of non-holographic states? We will motivate these questions and review what's known about the answers.

</details>


### [12] [Revisiting monodromy defects in $\text{O}(N)$ conformal field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33972)
*Jiechao Feng,Nayan Myerson-Jain,G. Shankar,Taige Wang,Meng Cheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies monodromy defects in O(N) conformal field theories using renormalization group and large-N methods, identifies multiple defect CFTs with distinct boundary conditions, establishes a stable defect CFT near d=3 with 'alternate' boundary conditions, and confirms its qualitative validity via Monte Carlo simulations in an improved O(3) lattice model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the universal behavior of monodromy defects—fundamental symmetry-twisting objects—in O(N) CFTs describing spontaneous symmetry breaking transitions, particularly how defect phases and scaling data depend on dimension and N.

Method: Analytical approach combining renormalization group flow analysis and 1/N expansion in general dimensions 3 ≤ d < 4; identification of defect CFTs via admissible boundary conditions for twisted/untwisted fields; large-scale classical Monte Carlo simulations of monodromy defects in an improved lattice realization of the O(3) CFT to extract defect scaling dimensions.

Result: Multiple defect CFTs exist for partial-monodromy defects in O(N) CFTs; near d = 3 and in the large-N limit, a unique stable defect CFT emerges with 'alternate' boundary conditions, yielding enhanced correlations for untwisted fields and suppressed ones for twisted fields; Monte Carlo results for O(3) confirm qualitative agreement with this stable defect CFT.

Conclusion: Partial-monodromy defects in O(N) CFTs host rich defect phase structure; the large-N + RG analysis predicts a robust stable defect CFT near three dimensions, validated numerically for O(3), establishing a quantitative bridge between analytical defect CFT predictions and non-perturbative lattice simulations.

Abstract: Monodromy defects are fundamental objects in extended quantum systems with global symmetries. We study such defects in $\text{O}(N)$ conformal field theories (CFT), which describe the universality class of $\text{O}(N)$ spontaneous symmetry breaking transitions. In particular, we consider partial-monodromy defects, whose corresponding symmetry transformations only act on a subset of the fundamental $\text{O}(N)$ vector field. We develop an analytical theory of the defect phases, using both renormalization group and large $N$ expansion. In general (spacetime) dimensions $3\leq d<4$, multiple defect CFTs are identified, which are described by appropriate boundary conditions for the vector field near the defect. Near $d=3$, we show that in the large $N$ limit, a stable defect CFT always exists, characterized by the so-called ("standard") "alternate" boundary conditions for the (twisted) untwisted fields, whose defect correlations are (suppressed) enhanced. We then perform large-scale classical Monte Carlo simulations of monodromy defects in the O(3) CFT in an improved lattice model and numerically extract the defect scaling dimensions. The numerical results are in qualitative agreement with the stable defect CFT identified in the field theory.

</details>


### [13] [Lifshitz Phase of Quantum Gravity: The Infrared Fixed Point of the (2+1)D Unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34024)
*Maxim Zelenko,Dam Thanh Son*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons gravity in (2+1)D with w_∞ gauge symmetry; exhibits asymptotic freedom and IR anisotropic scaling (z ≈ 2), and conjectures a confinement phase transition at critical κ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent nonrelativistic quantum gravity theory in (2+1) dimensions incorporating both Hořava-type Lifshitz scaling and topological Chern-Simons structure, while exploring its RG flow and possible phase structure.

Method: Analyzes renormalization group flow of a unimodular, w_∞-gauged gravity theory coupled to a Chern-Simons term; computes dynamical critical exponent z from perturbative beta function near large-|κ| regime; infers phase structure via coupling dependence.

Result: Identifies an asymptotically free UV fixed point and an IR fixed point with anisotropic scale invariance (z = 2 − 3/(2|κ|)); predicts existence of a critical κ below which the theory enters a confining phase.

Conclusion: Unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons gravity provides a controlled framework for studying quantum gravity phase transitions in low dimensions, linking topological field theory, nonrelativistic holography, and confinement mechanisms.

Abstract: We consider a nonrelativistic gravity theory in (2+1) dimensions with $w_\infty$ gauge symmetry that we call unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons gravity. The theory has three dimensionless couplings, one of which is the Chern-Simons level $\kappa$. At large $|\kappa|$, the theory flows from an asymptotically free UV fixed point to an IR fixed point with anisotropic scale invariance characterized by a dynamical critical exponent $z=2-\tfrac32|\kappa|^{-1}$. We speculate that unimodular Hořava-Chern-Simons gravity undergoes a phase transition from this scale-invariant phase to a confining phase when $\kappa$ drops below a critical value.

</details>


### [14] [Perturbative Holonomy Potentials and Vacua in Chiral Supersymmetric Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34240)
*Arash Arabi Ardehali*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a globally controlled framework for D-term data in chiral supersymmetric gauge theories on ℝ³×S¹, providing a one-loop-exact D-term coefficient that incorporates 4d FI parameters and enables consistent vacuum classification across the holonomy space—lifting the 3d Intriligator–Seiberg classification and revealing nontrivial vacuum structures missed by local analyses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve global control over semiclassical vacuum sectors in chiral supersymmetric gauge theories on ℝ³×S¹, overcoming limitations of local analyses near massless thresholds where standard D-term approximations break down.

Method: Derives a closed-form, one-loop-exact expression for the D-term coefficient—including 4d Fayet–Iliopoulos parameters—and uses it to define a uniformly controlled effective potential; applies this to lift the 3d Intriligator–Seiberg vacuum classification to ℝ³×S¹, analyzing holonomy dependence and threshold behavior.

Result: A globally valid D-term potential enabling consistent vacuum classification across the full compact holonomy space; discovery of rich vacuum structure in an abelian model; exact reproduction of the R-twisted reduction from SU(2) Maruyoshi–Song theory to Gang–Yamazaki theory on flat space, previously found only on S³×S¹.

Conclusion: Global matching of D-term data is essential: true vacua can reside at nontrivial holonomy even when the Coulomb-slice D-coefficient is nonzero at thresholds or vanishes at trivial holonomy—highlighting the insufficiency of local or naive flat-space reasoning.

Abstract: We establish global control of perturbative D-term data governing semiclassical vacuum sectors in chiral supersymmetric gauge theories on $\mathbb{R}^3\times S^1$. A closed-form, one-loop-exact D-term coefficient, including 4d Fayet--Iliopoulos (FI) parameters, gives a uniformly controlled leading potential away from massless thresholds and fixes local FI and Chern--Simons couplings near them. This allows lifting the 3d Intriligator--Seiberg classification of semiclassical vacua to $\mathbb{R}^3\times S^1$ across the compact holonomy space. The framework is showcased by finding a remarkably rich set of vacuum sectors in a simple abelian model and reproducing on flat space the R-twisted reduction of an $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Maruyoshi--Song theory to the Gang--Yamazaki theory, first found on $S^3\times S^1$. The examples demonstrate the stakes of global matching: true vacua may lie at nontrivial holonomy even with nonzero threshold values of the Coulomb-slice D-coefficient, and despite vanishing D-coefficient at trivial holonomy.

</details>


### [15] [All-plus Yang--Mills and gravity wavefunctions in de Sitter space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34261)
*Song He,Jiajie Mei,Yuyu Mo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'sewing representations' for tree-level all-plus wavefunctions in 4D de Sitter space, deriving compact combinatorial formulas for Yang–Mills (via noncrossing polygon dissections) and Einstein gravity (via alternating sums over labelled sewing trees plus a local Chern–Simons term), both rooted in self-dual solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct manifestly finite, pole-structure-resolving representations of all-plus wavefunctions in dS₄—bypassing ill-defined total-energy poles and encoding partial-energy residues recursively—leveraging the simplicity of self-dual sectors in gauge theory and gravity.

Method: For Yang–Mills: derive sewing representation from self-dual YM solutions; encode n-point wavefunction as sum over noncrossing polygon dissections, where each cell contributes energy-difference-weighted lower-point wavefunctions. For gravity: propose alternating sum over labelled sewing trees plus a local gravitational Chern–Simons boundary term; derive generating formula from boundary connection and compute explicit forms up to seven points.

Result: A manifestly total-energy-pole-free combinatorial formula for all-plus YM wavefunctions in dS₄, expressed via noncrossing dissections; and a novel gravitational sewing representation comprising an alternating tree sum + local Chern–Simons term that supplies missing boundary data, with explicit forms up to n=7.

Conclusion: Sewing representations provide a unified, recursive, and geometrically transparent framework for all-plus wavefunctions in dS₄, tightly linked to self-duality; they expose hidden combinatorial structure in YM and resolve boundary ambiguities in gravity via a local topological term.

Abstract: We present sewing representations for tree-level all-plus wavefunctions in four-dimensional de Sitter space. For Yang--Mills theory, the arbitrary-multiplicity formula is a sum over noncrossing polygon dissections, with each cell carrying energy differences of a full lower-point wavefunction. The absence of a generic total-energy pole is manifest, while partial-energy residues are determined by the lower-point factors. We derive this representation from self-dual Yang--Mills solutions. For Einstein gravity, we propose an arbitrary-multiplicity formula consisting of an alternating sum over labelled sewing trees plus a local gravitational Chern--Simons term. This local term arises from the self-dual Einstein action and supplies boundary data not fixed by internal-energy cuts. We derive a generating formula for it from the boundary connection and give explicit expressions through seven points.

</details>


### [16] [Exact lattice bosonization of the double-scaled $c=1$ matrix model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34376)
*Adwait Gaikwad,Gautam Mandal,Ajay Mohan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work on entanglement entropy (EE) in matrix quantum mechanics/c=1 string theory by introducing a finite, position-dependent lattice boson description—distinct from the divergent collective variable approach—with spacing ~g_s(x) l_s; it yields finite EE and density correlators, and proposes a D0-brane interpretation linking short-distance scales to local vs. asymptotic observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve divergences in the collective variable formulation of fermion density ρ(λ) for computing entanglement entropy and correlation functions in the c=1 double-scaled matrix model, and to clarify the physical meaning of short-distance scales in relation to D0-brane physics.

Method: Exact bosonization via a finite, non-uniform lattice boson with position-dependent spacing g_s(x) l_s = g_s csch²(x/l_s) l_s; derivation of entanglement entropy, one- and two-point functions of fermion density directly from this lattice boson framework; comparison with collective variable theory; interpretation using D0-brane effective dynamics.

Result: Finite, explicit expressions for c=1 entanglement entropy and ⟨ρ(λ)⟩, ⟨ρ(λ)ρ(λ′)⟩ correlators; demonstration that collective variable theory yields UV-divergent results for these local observables; identification of g_s(x) l_s as the relevant short-distance scale for local quantities (e.g., EE), while l_s governs asymptotic observables (e.g., genus-by-genus S-matrix).

Conclusion: The lattice boson provides a well-defined, UV-finite framework for local observables in c=1 string theory, revealing a hierarchy of short-distance scales: g_s(x) l_s controls locality and entanglement, whereas l_s sets the scale for scattering amplitudes; this dichotomy suggests an underlying D0-brane microstructure where g_s(x) encodes local brane density variations.

Abstract: In a recent paper by two of us, it has been shown that entanglement entropy (EE) of matrix quantum mechanics (or, equivalently, of a non-relativistic fermion model) has a natural interpretation in terms of an exact bosonization of the fermion theory in terms of a lattice boson with lattice spacing $\sim 1/N$ (where $N$ is the rank of the matrix, equal to the number of fermions). The theory of this lattice boson is distinct from a latticized version of the "collective variable'' theory which is formulated in terms of the fermion density $\rho(\lambda)$ ("collective variable''); it is, in fact, more closely related to the Wigner distribution of the fermions. In this paper, we develop this connection further for the $c=1$ double scaled matrix model. Even though $N$ becomes infinite, the double scaled theory is described by a boson on a finite and non-uniform lattice spacing, of order $\sim g_s(x) l_s$ (where $g_s(x) \equiv g_s {\rm cosech}^2(x/l_s)$ is a position dependent string coupling). We explicitly derive the $c=1$ EE, as well as the one and two-point functions of fermion density from the lattice boson. The results are explicitly finite, unlike the collective variable theory which is shown to yield divergent answers. Our results suggest that while local observables such as the EE and $\langle \rho(\lambda) \rangle$, are characterized by a unique short distance scale $g_s(x) l_s$, the asymptotic observables such as the S-matrix at every genus are characterized by a short distance scale $l_s$. We show how this arises and discuss a possible interpretation in terms of D0 brane physics.

</details>


### [17] [Nonlinear Higher-Spin Theory in $O(4,1)$ de Sitter Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34475)
*O.A. Gelfond,M.A. Vasiliev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes four higher-spin gauge theories compatible with de Sitter geometry (O(4,1)) and fermions; two based on a Z2×Z2 Lie superalgebra previously used for dS supergravity, and two unitary in bosonic sectors with pseudo-involution hinting at (anti)unitarity linked to time reversal or spacetime doubling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct consistent higher-spin gauge theories on de Sitter space (dS₄) incorporating fermions, while preserving structural features of known supergravity frameworks and addressing unitarity and involution properties in curved spacetime.

Method: Constructs four distinct higher-spin gauge theory models using O(4,1) symmetry; identifies two models realizing a Z₂×Z₂ Lie superalgebra structure (previously introduced for dS supergravity), and analyzes unitarity in bosonic sectors and existence of pseudo involutions when fermions are included.

Result: Four explicit higher-spin gauge theory formulations on dS₄ with fermions; two models realize the Z₂×Z₂ Lie superalgebra; two are bosonically unitary and admit a pseudo involution suggesting possible (anti)unitary realizations tied to time reversal or spacetime doubling.

Conclusion: The work extends higher-spin theory to de Sitter space with fermions, revealing structural links to supergravity and novel symmetry-based mechanisms—via pseudo involutions—for reconciling unitarity constraints in non-flat quantum field-theoretic settings.

Abstract: Four versions of the higher-spin gauge theory compatible with $O(4,1)$ de Sitter geometry in presence of fermions are proposed. Two of them exhibit the structure of $\mathbb{Z}_2\times \mathbb{Z}_2$ Lie superalgebra proposed originally by one of the authors for the description of de Sitter supergravity \cite{Vasiliev:1985vtv}. Two of the proposed models are unitary in the bosonic sectors in analogy with the recent proposal by Lang and Neiman \cite{Lang:2026inn} and admit a pseudo involution in presence of fermions suggesting possible (anti)unitarity associated either with the time reversal in $dS_4$ or with the space-time doubling.

</details>


### [18] [Two-node Dualities from Elementary Dualities in 3d and 4d](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34752)
*Yuanyuan Fang,Sungjoon Kim*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a new 3D N=2 two-node Seiberg-like duality analogous to the 4D N=1 ILS duality, verified via ellipsoid partition function matching using s-confinement; derived solely from two elementary 3D N=2 dualities (Aharony and BBP), supporting 'dualities from dualities'; also rederives the original ILS duality via nodewise Seiberg/Kutasov–Schwimmer dualities with Higgsing and massive field integration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a 3D N=2 analogue of the 4D N=1 Intriligator–Leigh–Strassler duality and explore the generative power of elementary dualities in constructing more complex ones.

Method: Uses s-confinement to verify duality via exact matching of 3D N=2 ellipsoid partition functions; constructs the new duality by composing Aharony and Benini–Benvenuti–Pasquetti dualities; rederives ILS duality via sequential nodewise Seiberg and Kutasov–Schwimmer dualities, intermediate Higgsing, and integration of massive fields.

Result: Strong evidence for a novel 3D N=2 two-node Seiberg-like duality; demonstration that complex dualities can be generated from elementary ones ('dualities from dualities'); a constructive, nodewise derivation of the original ILS duality.

Conclusion: The proposed duality extends the landscape of 3D N=2 dualities and supports the paradigm that intricate dualities emerge from iterative application of basic ones; the methodology provides a systematic framework for generating and verifying dualities in supersymmetric gauge theories.

Abstract: A new 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ two-node Seiberg-like duality, analogous to the 4d $\mathcal{N}=1$ Intriligator--Leigh--Strassler~(ILS) duality, is proposed. As strong evidence, we check the agreement between the 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ ellipsoid partition functions for numerous cases using s-confinement method. Surprisingly, our derivation assumes only two elementary 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ dualities: Aharony and Benini--Benvenuti--Pasquetti, which reinforces the idea dualities from dualities. We also revisit the original ILS duality and provide a derivation based on nodewise dualities. In particular, we apply a sequence of Seiberg and Kutasov--Schwimmer dualities to individual gauge nodes, with Higgsing at an intermediate step, and subsequently integrate out the massive fields.

</details>


### [19] [Graph-Based Algorithms for Worldline EFT Integrands](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35007)
*Gregor Kälin,Jungwon Lim*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces graph-centered algorithms for worldline effective field theory (WEFT) integrands, decoupling topological (graph) and algebraic (numerator) structures; it proposes an optimized Berends-Giele-like construction, serialization and 'selfie' identities for canonicalization, and extends the integral family/sector concept to support efficient IBP reduction—implemented in the Rust crate FeynRIR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically separate and simplify the topological and algebraic complexity of WEFT loop integrands, enabling efficient computation, canonical representation, and optimized integration-by-parts (IBP) reduction—especially for post-Minkowskian gravitational scattering amplitudes involving worldlines.

Method: Introduces a graph-numerator representation where graphs encode topology (propagators, vertices, momentum routing, i0, proper-time), and numerators carry only essential tensor algebra; employs a generalized Berends-Giele recursion to construct fixed-post-Minkowskian integrands with minimal tensor contractions; applies 'serialization' of worldline propagators and 'selfie'-subdiagram partial-fraction identities for simplification and canonicalization; redefines integral families and sectors to allow sector-specific propagator labelings and scalar product bases.

Result: A minimal canonical topology basis; reduced number of required IBP sectors via optimal exploitation of sector symmetries; flexible, overcomplete global integral family supporting independent propagator labeling per sector; efficient automated generation of WEFT integrands for action variation, worldline equations of motion, and radiation-reacted impulse.

Conclusion: The graph-numerator formalism provides a structural, scalable, and computationally efficient framework for WEFT integrand construction and reduction—bridging worldline methods with modern amplitude techniques—and is concretely realized in the high-performance Rust crate FeynRIR.

Abstract: We present novel graph-centered algorithms for the construction and representation of worldline effective field theory (WEFT) integrands that separate the topological and algebraic structure of loop integrands into independent graph-numerator pairs. In this representation, the graph encodes all propagators, worldline source and sink vertices, momentum routing, i0 prescriptions, and proper-time dependence, while the numerator carries only the minimal algebraic, tensorial content. To construct integrands at fixed post-Minkowskian order we propose a generalized Berends-Giele construction that minimizes the number of expensive tensor contraction operations. We also discuss simplification and canonicalization strategies through "serialization" of worldline propagators and the application of "selfie"-subdiagram partial-fraction identities, yielding a minimal canonical topology basis. We further show that the graph-numerator representation naturally generalizes the notion of an integral family and its sectors, relevant for integration-by-parts (IBP) reduction: propagators are not constrained to a single master labeling, and each sector carries its own minimal set of independent scalar products, completing into an overcomplete global family. This setup minimizes the total number of required integral sectors through an optimal use of sector symmetries and enables independent optimization of propagator labeling for IBP reductions. The algorithms are implemented in the Rust crate FeynRIR, which is currently able to efficiently compute integrands for the variation of the effective WEFT action, the equations of motion for the worldlines and the (radiation-reacted) impulse.

</details>


### [20] [Exact Stress Tensor Flows in Causal Electrodynamics: Auxiliary Field Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35135)
*Zahra Pezhman,H. Babaei-Aghbolagh,Komeil Babaei Velni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies non-perturbative $T\bar T$-like deformations of causal self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics using an auxiliary-field formalism, deriving exact flow equations, causality constraints, and physical interpretations of the kernel in terms of stress-tensor invariants and energy conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically analyze irrelevant and marginal deformations—particularly $T\bar T$-like and ModMax-type—in causal, self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics, leveraging its simple auxiliary-field description to obtain exact non-perturbative results and clarify links between geometry, causality, duality, and analyticity.

Method: Uses a single-auxiliary-field formulation where the Lagrangian is linear in Lorentz invariants $X=F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}$ and $Y=F_{\mu\nu}\tilde F^{\mu\nu}$, with dynamics encoded in a potential $W(\varphi)$. Derives exact flow equations under $T\bar T$-like deformation (rescaling $W$) and ModMax-type deformation (shifting vacuum), expresses flows in terms of the invariant ratio $r = -T^\mu{}_\mu / \sqrt{4X-Y}$, and connects kernel properties to causality, Legendre self-duality, Hamiltonian structure, and energy conditions.

Result: Derives autonomous exact $T\bar T$-like flow $\partial_\lambda \mathcal{L} = X F(r)$ with closed-form kernel $F(r)$; establishes causality condition $D(r) > 1/(1-r)$ and monotonicity requirement for global single-valuedness of $F$; relates evenness of flow, $\varphi$-parity of $W$, and analyticity of $\mathcal{L}$ in $X,Y$; constructs Hamiltonian formulation showing reversed-sign flow; expresses Born self-duality, characteristic cones, and energy conditions—including $r = (\varepsilon - p_\perp)/(\varepsilon + p_\perp)$—in terms of $F$; provides explicit $F(r)$ for generalized BI, logarithmic, q-deformed, and no-$\tau$-maximum models.

Conclusion: The auxiliary-field framework unifies and simplifies the analysis of $T\bar T$-like deformations in causal self-dual electrodynamics, revealing deep geometric and physical structure: the kernel $F(r)$ fully encodes dynamics, causality, duality, and thermodynamics; exact solvability extends beyond perturbation theory; and the ratio $r$ serves as a universal physical parameter linking field theory, hydrodynamics, and geometry.

Abstract: Causal self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics admits a simple auxiliary-field description: the Lagrangian is linear in the two Lorentz invariants of the field strength, and the dynamics is encoded in a potential $W(\varphi)$ of a single auxiliary variable. We study non-perturbative $T\bar T$-like flows of these theories in this formalism. The irrelevant (stress-tensor) deformation acts on the potential by a rescaling, while the marginal ModMax-type deformation translates its vacuum. We derive the resulting flow equations exactly, show that the irrelevant exact $T\bar T$-like flow takes the autonomous form $\partial_\lambda\mathcal L=X\,F(r)$, where the closed form kernel $F$ depends only on the ratio $r=-T^\mu{}_\mu/\sqrt{4X-Y}$ of stress-tensor invariants. We also give an explicit reconstruction of the auxiliary potential from a given kernel. Causality imposes the differential inequality $D(r)>1/(1-r)$, while a globally single-valued kernel requires the ratio $r$ to be monotonic along the auxiliary branch. We examine the relation between the evenness of the exact $T\bar T$-like flow , the $\varphi$-parity of the potential, and the analyticity of the Lagrangian in the electromagnetic invariants, including the effect of a marginal ModMax parameter. We construct the linear auxiliary and Hamiltonian formulations and show that the Hamiltonian flow is governed by the same exact $T\bar T$-like flow with the opposite sign. We also express Born's Legendre self-duality, the two characteristic cones, and the energy conditions in terms of the kernel. In particular, $r=(\varepsilon-p_\perp)/(\varepsilon+p_\perp)$ has a direct physical interpretation. Exact $T\bar T$-like flow s are obtained for generalized Born-Infeld, logarithmic, q-deformed, and no-$\tau$-maximum electrodynamics.

</details>


### [21] [The one-loop QCD scale anomaly on ADE orbifolds as an index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35166)
*Herbie Warner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives the one-loop scale anomaly for SU(N) Yang-Mills on R^4/Gamma orbifolds using an equivariant index theorem on twistor space; anomaly splits into standard QCD beta-function and a fixed-point-localized term containing gravitational Weyl anomaly and an unprotected heat-kernel coefficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum scale anomalies behave in gauge theories on singular orbifold spacetimes, particularly disentangling universal (beta-function) and geometry-dependent (localized) contributions in the presence of curvature singularities.

Method: Applies an equivariant index theorem on twistor space to compute the one-loop effective action and extract the scale anomaly for SU(N) Yang-Mills theory on R^4/Gamma with Gamma ⊂ SU(2), leveraging the ALE resolution geometry and heat-kernel asymptotics.

Result: The one-loop scale anomaly decomposes into the standard QCD beta-function plus a localized contribution at the orbifold fixed point; the latter comprises the gravitational Weyl anomaly inherited from the smooth ALE resolution (concentrating under singular limit) and an additional unprotected heat-kernel coefficient not fixed by symmetry.

Conclusion: Scale anomalies on orbifolds exhibit a geometric splitting: bulk universality coexists with singularity-sensitive localized terms, revealing interplay between gauge dynamics, conformal geometry, and UV sensitivity encoded in unprotected coefficients.

Abstract: We derive the one-loop scale anomaly of $\SU(N)$ Yang--Mills on the orbifold $\R^4/\Gamma$, with $\Gamma\subset \SU(2)$ a finite subgroup, from an equivariant index theorem on twistor space. The anomaly separates into the QCD $\beta$-function and a contribution localized at the orbifold fixed point. This localized term inherits the gravitational Weyl anomaly of the smooth ALE resolution, which concentrates under the singular degeneration, along with an additional unprotected heat-kernel coefficient.

</details>


### [22] [On memory effects in odd spacetime dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35283)
*Ana-Maria Raclariu,Binsong Xiong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper discovers a new electromagnetic memory effect in all odd-dimensional spacetimes, arising at Coulombic order from charged particles with constant momenta; it is tied to the Weinberg soft theorem, derived via Maxwell constraints on null planes at large finite r, and requires careful ordering of light-ray integration and the r→∞ limit; explicit results are given for massless/massive sources, linked to soft photon theorem’s Fourier transform, and a finite-time measurement protocol is proposed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover universal infrared structures of electromagnetism in higher dimensions, particularly electromagnetic memory effects tied to soft theorems and asymptotic symmetries, extending known 4D results to arbitrary odd spacetime dimensions.

Method: Evaluation of the Maxwell constraint equation on a null hypersurface at large but finite radial distance r; careful analysis of the commutativity (or lack thereof) between light-ray integration and the r → ∞ limit; Fourier analysis linking memory to the leading soft photon theorem; explicit computation of Coulombic-order memory for both massive and massless sources in odd D ≥ 5.

Result: A novel, non-vanishing electromagnetic memory effect exists exclusively in odd spacetime dimensions at Coulombic order; it is sourced by constant-momentum charged particles, directly encodes the Weinberg soft theorem via its Fourier transform, and admits a finite-time measurement protocol scaling parametrically with detector-source separation; the effect vanishes if the r → ∞ limit precedes light-ray integration.

Conclusion: Electromagnetic memory is a robust, dimension-dependent infrared phenomenon: it is universal and nontrivial only in odd D, governed by Coulombic field behavior at finite large r, and fundamentally tied to soft theorems and asymptotic symmetry structure; the framework naturally suggests analogous gravitational memory in odd dimensions.

Abstract: We establish the existence of a new electromagnetic memory effect in all odd spacetime dimensions. This effect is a universal property of electromagnetic fields sourced by charged particles of constant momenta, appears at the Coulombic order and is directly related to the Weinberg soft theorem. We provide a unified derivation of this effect in all higher dimensions by evaluating the Maxwell constraint on a null plane at large, finite distance. We observe that in odd spacetime dimensions, the lightray integral computing the memory effect does not commute with the large-$r$ expansion. Our results necessarily assume that the lightray integral is taken prior to the $r \rightarrow \infty$ limit. The effect vanishes in the opposite order of limits, in agreement with previous results. We compute the Coulombic order electromagnetic memory effect for both massless and massive sources in all higher dimensions, explain its relation to the Fourier transform of the leading soft photon theorem and present a protocol for its measurement in a finite time parametrically larger than the distance of a detector from the sources. We conclude with comments on the generalization to gravity.

</details>


### [23] [Theory and Cosmology of Biverse Volume-Normalized Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35384)
*Siqing Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This dissertation introduces biverse volume-normalized gravity—a 5D bulk–4D boundary framework combining volume-normalized action and ghost-free massive gravity—to resolve the cosmological constant problem and eliminate quantum divergences and strong coupling, while predicting r = 0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the 60-orders-of-magnitude cosmological constant problem by unifying two promising but flawed ideas: Tseytlin's volume-normalized action (plagued by quantum loop divergences) and ghost-free massive gravity (hampered by an ad hoc second metric and strong coupling).

Method: Constructs a biverse model where our (3+1)D universe resides as a boundary of a (4+1)D bulk; implements volume normalization of the total action (including Standard Model and gravity) relative to the combined spacetime volume; embeds ghost-free massive gravity via pullback of the bulk metric onto the boundary, thereby geometrizing the second metric and suppressing quantum loops in the small-volume-ratio limit.

Result: Eliminates quantum loop divergences in the volume-normalized framework; renders the second metric geometrically natural (as a boundary pullback); resolves the strong coupling problem of massive gravity; maintains consistency with established particle cosmology and cosmic evolution; predicts vanishing primordial tensor perturbations (tensor-to-scalar ratio r ≡ 0).

Conclusion: Biverse volume-normalized gravity provides a self-consistent, UV-improved classical and quantum gravitational framework that solves the cosmological constant problem without fine-tuning, offers a geometric origin for massive gravity’s auxiliary metric, and yields a sharp, falsifiable prediction—r = 0—that distinguishes it from standard inflationary models.

Abstract: This dissertation expounds the biverse volume-normalized gravity, a novel theoretical framework of classical and quantum gravity that provides an unusual solution to the cosmological constant problem, the 60-degree-of-magnitude discrepancy between the value of the vacuum energy density in our universe predicted by particle physics and that implied by cosmological data. In the literature, there exist two interesting ideas: 1) to use the "volume-normalized" action functional, the usual action of the particle Standard Model and general relativity but divided by the spacetime volume, first proposed by Tseytlin; 2) to generate the vacuum energy density dynamically by assigning a ghost-free massive potential to the graviton. However, each has a problem: Tseytlin's attempt suffers from diverging quantum loops and the massive gravity theory has a second metric tensor with questionable origin and the quantum strong coupling problem. Inspired by these two ideas, as well as the AdS/CFT correspondence in string theory, the biverse volume-normalized gravity extends Tseytlin's idea by placing our (3+1)-dimensional universe on the boundary of a (4+1)-dimensional bulk space. This new adaptation controls the large quantum gravity loops in the limit of vanishing ratio of the spacetime volume of our universe to that of the other universe. The ghost-free massive gravity can then be seamlessly incorporated, not only that its otherwise nebulous second metric tensor appears naturally as the pullback of the bulk metric on the boundary, but its strong coupling problem also solved. The biverse volume-normalized gravity is also consistent with all tested models of particle cosmology and the evolution of the universe, and nontrivially predicts that the tensor-to-scalar ratio of our universe is exactly zero.

</details>


### [24] [The topological vertex is finitely generated](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35719)
*Kunal Gupta,Pietro Longhi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The topological vertex, which computes genus amplitudes for the open topological A-model on toric Calabi-Yau threefolds, is shown to arise from boundary interactions of three disks and three annuli; this result extends to the refined topological vertex.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the geometric and physical origin of the topological vertex — a key computational tool in topological string theory — by identifying its elementary building blocks in terms of simple holomorphic maps (disks and annuli) with boundary conditions on Lagrangian branes.

Method: Analysis of holomorphic curve contributions with boundary on toric Lagrangians in $\mathbb{C}^3$; identification of disk and annulus configurations whose boundary interactions reproduce the combinatorial structure of the topological vertex; extension to the refined case via $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-graded counting or equivariant localization.

Result: The topological vertex is fully generated by three disk and three annulus boundary interaction diagrams; the refined topological vertex admits an analogous decomposition involving graded boundary couplings.

Conclusion: The topological vertex is not merely a combinatorial prescription but has a concrete worldsheet interpretation in terms of minimal open-string configurations — disks and annuli — thereby unifying its mathematical definition with physical string-theoretic intuition.

Abstract: The topological vertex computes all genus amplitudes for the open topological A-model in $\mathbb{C}^3$ with branes on three toric Lagrangians and, via gluing, both open and closed string amplitudes on any toric Calabi-Yau threefold. In this note we show that the topological vertex is generated by boundary interactions of three disks and three annuli. A generalization of this result is shown to hold for the refined topological vertex.

</details>


### [25] [Compositional Operational Rank, Smooth Realizability, and Dimensional Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35764)
*Nikolay D. Tulupov,Alexander M. Tishin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes conditions under which operational independence—quantified by compositional operational rank—is preserved across dimensional reduction in higher-dimensional physical theories, linking it first to smooth integral leaf dimension and then to Fisher-information rank; closure-faithfulness, real representability, constant rank, involutivity, observer/scale compatibility, and primary-identification are key requirements, and the framework rejects naive coordinate-counting as sufficient for viable dimensional reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously relate microscopic dimensionality to observable dimensionality in higher-dimensional theories—not just via dimensional reduction, but by preserving operational independence structure across descriptive levels.

Method: Constructs a hierarchy of structural conditions (closure-faithfulness, real representability, constant rank, involutivity, observer/scale compatibility, primary-identification) ensuring that compositional operational rank is preserved first as the dimension of a smooth integral leaf and then as the rank of a finite composite Fisher-information protocol; employs differential-geometric (integral distributions) and statistical (Fisher information) tools.

Result: A local integral leaf whose dimension equals the operational rank is guaranteed under the stated conditions; further, under measurement-level primary-identification and protocol constraints (local resolution, finite labeled-mixture closure, locally constant outcome support), the same rank is realized by a finite composite Fisher-information protocol; at r = 4, yields a conditional operational criterion for viable dimensional reduction.

Conclusion: Viable dimensional reduction must preserve the operational dependency structure—not merely yield four effective coordinates—and the framework imposes operational constraints on reduction outcomes without enforcing a universal 4D bound on microscopic state spaces; quantum-hardware negative controls demonstrate that identical restricted Fisher ranks can mask distinct underlying higher ranks.

Abstract: Higher-dimensional theories distinguish the dimensionality of a microscopic description from the number of directions accessible to a physical observer. Relating these notions requires not only dimensional reduction, but also a consistent preservation of operational independence across different levels of description. We establish sufficient conditions under which a compositional operational rank is preserved first as the dimension of a smooth integral leaf and then as an observable Fisher-information rank. The key requirement is closure-faithfulness: the smooth realization must preserve the dependency relations of the operational closure, rather than merely its total rank. Together with real representability, constant rank, involutivity, and observer/scale compatibility, this yields a local integral leaf whose dimension equals the operational rank. A further primary-identification condition is required at the measurement level. If an independently specified observer image coincides locally with the primary integral distribution and the admissible protocols provide local resolution, finite labeled-mixture closure, and locally constant outcome support, then the same rank can be realized by a finite composite Fisher-information protocol. The construction holds for arbitrary finite rank. At r = 4 it provides a conditional operational criterion for dimensional reduction in higher-dimensional theories: a viable reduction must preserve the relevant operational dependency structure, not merely produce four effective coordinates. Quantum-hardware negative controls further show that the same restricted Fisher rank can conceal different larger ranks under a broader pre-specified measurement protocol. The framework therefore constrains the operational outcome of dimensional reduction without imposing a universal four-dimensional bound on microscopic state spaces.

</details>


### [26] [Quantum Field Theory on the Tangent Bundle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35778)
*Joachim Herrmann*

Main category: hep-th
