TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified quantum-field-theoretical framework on the tangent bundle, linking gravity (via teleparallel geometry) and internal gauge symmetries (SU(2) weak, SU(3)_c color) through distinct sectors of affine tangent-fiber geometry; it reproduces fractional quark charges, recovers standard QCD dynamics including asymptotic freedom, and suggests extensions with extra gauge bosons and dark-matter candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify gravitational and internal gauge structures within a common geometric framework—specifically, by exploiting the tangent bundle’s affine fiber geometry—to explain observed particle properties (e.g., chiral spectrum, fractional charges, anomaly cancellation) and recover standard model features (e.g., QCD beta function) from first principles.

Method: Constructs a quantum field theory on the tangent bundle with split affine geometry: translational sector yields teleparallel gravity (coframe-based); vertical sector modeled via projective representations of E^c(2), inducing chiral left-handed weak SU(2) and gauged color SU(3)_c; introduces global quotient linking color center to threefold rotational cover; enforces BRST symmetry, anomaly constraints, and spectral reduction.

Result: Reproduces observed fractional electric charges of quarks; recovers local QCD structure including one-loop beta function and asymptotic freedom; predicts additional gauge bosons and dark-matter states; formulates structural action coupling teleparallel torsion to Lorentz-sector potentials.

Conclusion: The framework successfully embeds key features of the Standard Model and teleparallel gravity in a shared geometric setting on the tangent bundle, but full dynamical consistency, phenomenological predictions, and experimental viability remain open for future work.

Abstract: We develop a quantum-field-theoretical framework on the tangent bundle in which gravitational and internal gauge structures are associated with different sectors of an affine tangent-fiber geometry. The translational sector supplies the coframe and its relation to teleparallel gravity. A reduced vertical spectral problem is based on the centrally extended Euclidean group \(E^{c}(2)\), motivated by projective representations of the stabilizer of a nonzero null vector. Twofold multiplicities of equivalent vertical representations in selected left-chiral sectors admit weak \(SU(2)\) actions, while an assumed threefold quark multiplicity admits a locally gauged color \(SU(3)_c\) symmetry. A proposed global quotient correlates the color center with vertical rotations on a threefold rotation cover. Together with the selected chiral spectrum and anomaly conditions, this construction reproduces the observed fractional quark charge assignments. We formulate matter and gauge-field quantization, BRST symmetry, and anomaly constraints. Under the stated projection assumptions, the color sector has the standard local QCD structure, including its one-loop beta function and asymptotic freedom. Additional gauge bosons and dark-matter states are proposed as hypothetical sectors. We also formulate a structural action for possible couplings between teleparallel torsion and additional Lorentz-sector potentials. The framework relates selected structures of particle physics and gravity within a common geometric setting; their further dynamical and phenomenological development remains open.

</details>


### [27] [How to get an attractive model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35798)
*Joseph McGovern*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a previously overlooked fourth-order Calabi-Yau differential operator with geometric significance and evidence of a rank-two attractor, suggesting modularity akin to elliptic curves over ℚ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and highlight a missing fourth-order Calabi-Yau differential operator from prior classifications due to its geometric clarity and potential modular properties.

Method: Analysis of differential operators associated with Calabi-Yau threefolds; identification via geometric interpretation and attractor mechanism analysis.

Result: Discovery of a new fourth-order Calabi-Yau differential operator exhibiting evidence of a rank-two attractor.

Conclusion: The operator provides a candidate for a Calabi-Yau threefold with elliptic-curve-like modularity over ℚ, extending known examples of arithmetic-geometric correspondence.

Abstract: We give a fourth-order Calabi-Yau differential operator, which seems to have been missed by earlier tabulations. We draw attention to this operator because of its clear geometric interpretation and evidence that it possesses a rank-two attractor, conjecturally giving a new example of a Calabi-Yau threefold with modularity properties analogous to those of an elliptic curve over $\mathbb{Q}$.

</details>

### [28] [Search for nonresonant production of strongly coupled dark matter in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33140)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: A CMS search for semivisible jets—jets containing both visible and invisible (dark matter) particles—using 138 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV pp collision data finds no significant excess; sets 95% CL cross section limits and excludes mediator masses from 0.5–2.4 TeV, with unsupervised autoencoder outperforming supervised GNN for high-dark-matter-fraction scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe dark matter from a strongly coupled hidden sector via semivisible jets—distinct from conventional mono-X searches—by targeting final states with missing transverse momentum aligned with a jet, not recoiling against SM particles.

Method: Two ML strategies applied to CMS data: (1) supervised graph neural network trained on simulated signal/background; (2) unsupervised autoencoder trained only on QCD jets to detect anomalous jet substructure; both optimized for identifying jets with mixed visible/invisible energy deposits.

Result: No statistically significant excess observed; 95% CL upper limits on production cross section set; supervised search excludes mediator masses 0.5–2.4 TeV; unsupervised search yields up to 11% larger observed mediator mass exclusion for high-stable-DMP-fraction models.

Conclusion: Semivisible jet signatures remain unobserved; results constrain bifundamental scalar mediator models coupling SM and dark-sector quarks; unsupervised anomaly detection shows complementary and enhanced sensitivity in specific signal regions.

Abstract: A search is presented for semivisible jets produced in nonresonant processes, using proton-proton collision data at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV, collected with the CMS detector at the CERN LHC and corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 138 fb$^{-1}$. These jets, containing both visible and invisible particles, would indicate the presence of dark matter arising from a strongly coupled hidden sector. The signal model includes a bifundamental scalar that couples simultaneously to standard model quarks and dark-sector quarks, the latter forming semivisible jets. The final state therefore includes missing transverse momentum aligned with a jet, rather than recoiling against standard model particles as typically required by conventional collider dark-matter searches. Two separate machine-learning strategies are employed to identify semivisible jets: a supervised graph neural network and an unsupervised autoencoder. No significant excess corresponding to dark-sector signal hypotheses is observed. Upper limits at 95% confidence level on the signal production cross section are determined for each strategy and interpreted as exclusions in the signal model parameter space. The supervised search excludes mediator masses between 0.5 and 2.4 TeV, depending on the other signal model parameters. For signal models with semivisible jets containing higher fractions of stable dark-matter particles, the unsupervised search demonstrates increased sensitivity with 5 (11)% higher expected (observed) mediator mass exclusions than the supervised search.

</details>

TL;DR: This paper reports a search for the rare decay η′→3γ using 10.087 billion J/ψ events collected by BESIII, yielding a new upper limit on its branching fraction: B(η′→3γ) < 2.25×10⁻⁵ at 90% CL — improving the prior limit by >4×.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe chiral anomalies and QCD dynamics via the highly suppressed electromagnetic decay η′→3γ, which is forbidden at leading order in QED and sensitive to higher-order effects and possible new physics.

Method: Analyzed (10087 ± 44) × 10⁶ J/ψ events from BESIII; reconstructed η′ via radiative J/ψ→γη′, then searched for η′→3γ decays using photon energy/momentum constraints, background suppression via kinematic fits and sideband subtraction, and statistical limit setting with a profile likelihood method.

Result: No significant signal observed; upper limit set at B(η′→3γ) < 2.25 × 10⁻⁵ at 90% confidence level.

Conclusion: The result provides the most stringent constraint to date on η′→3γ, tightening tests of QCD anomaly contributions and limiting exotic decay mechanisms or light new particles coupling to photons.

Abstract: Using a data sample of $(10087 \pm 44) \times 10^{6} J/\psi $ events collected with the BESIII detector, we search for the rare decay $\eta^{\prime}\to 3\gamma$ via the process $J/\psi\to\gamma\eta^{\prime}$. No significant signal is observed and an upper limit on the branching fraction of $\eta^{\prime}\to 3\gamma$ is set at $\mathcal{B}(\eta^{\prime}\to 3\gamma) < 2.25\times 10^{-5}$ at the 90\% confidence level. This result improves the previous upper limit by more than a factor of four.

</details>


### [30] [MATHUSLA: A Proposed Long-Lived Particle Detector for High-Luminosity LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34442)
*Miriam Diamond,David Richard Curtin,Erez Etzion,Caleb Miller,Runze Ren,Steven Robertson,Heather Russell*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: MATHUSLA is a proposed surface-based detector above CMS to search for long-lived neutral particles (LLPs) produced at the HL-LHC, using scintillator layers and SiPMs in an air-filled decay volume.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect beyond-Standard-Model long-lived neutral particles (LLPs) — predicted by theories addressing the hierarchy problem and dark matter — that escape conventional LHC detectors due to weak interactions and long lifetimes.

Method: Design and simulation-based optimization of MATHUSLA: modular plastic scintillator hodoscope with wavelength-shifting fibers and SiPM readout; studies of siting, mechanical engineering, DAQ, cosmic-ray background suppression, event selection, and trigger algorithms post-CDR.

Result: Refined design parameters including optimized placement, robust module mechanics, low-background trigger strategies, and validated background rejection techniques enabling staged deployment and early data-taking.

Conclusion: MATHUSLA is technically feasible and well-positioned to probe unexplored LLP parameter space during HL-LHC operations, with its modular design allowing incremental commissioning and physics operation.

Abstract: Beyond the Standard Model long-lived particles (LLPs) appear in many theories that address fundamental open questions, such as the hierarchy problem and the nature of dark matter. The Large Hadron Collider (LHC) may be producing copious numbers of neutral LLPs with masses above a GeV, only for these sneaky particles to escape the main detectors without being spotted. To fill this gap, we have proposed MATHUSLA (MAssive Timing Hodoscope for Ultra-Stable neutraL pArticles), which would be constructed on the surface above CMS and would take data during High-Luminosity LHC operations. The detector would be composed of several layers of solid plastic scintillator threaded with wavelength-shifting fibers that connect to silicon photomultipliers (SiPMs), and would monitor an empty, air-filled decay volume. The Conceptual Design Report (CDR) published in 2025 sets out a benchmark geometry of 40m x 40m x 16m, with a modular detector construction scheme that would allow data collection to begin as soon as the first module is installed. This proceeding documents the results of more detailed studies conducted since the CDR on the detector's potential location, mechanical engineering of modules, data acquisition system, cosmic ray backgrounds, event selection criteria, and trigger algorithms.

</details>


### [31] [LH$μ$C: A high energy, high luminosity muon-hadron collider at CERN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35154)
*Krzysztof Piotrzkowski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper outlines the LHμC proposal—a muon-proton collider leveraging LH$e$C infrastructure—enabling 5.3–7.5 TeV μ⁺p collisions at ~10³⁴ cm⁻²s⁻¹ luminosity, with strong motivation in neutrino physics and synergies with LH$e$C, FCC, and Muon Collider initiatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable high-energy muon-proton collisions for unprecedented studies in neutrino physics (e.g., parton distribution functions, electroweak couplings, and neutrino-nucleus cross sections), exploiting the clean weak-interaction probe nature of muons and complementing existing electron- and proton-collider programs.

Method: Proposes the LHμC facility by reusing LH$e$C infrastructure: ultra-cold anti-muon production via pion decay-in-flight, capture and cooling in a solenoidal transport system, acceleration in a dedicated muon booster, and injection into a main muon storage ring intersecting the LHC proton beam; includes conceptual design of machine-detector interface and a general-purpose detector optimized for forward muon tagging and hadronic final states.

Result: Feasibility assessment confirms that a 1–2 TeV muon beam colliding with 7 TeV LHC protons yields √s = 5.3–7.5 TeV μ⁺p collisions at peak luminosity ~10³⁴ cm⁻²s⁻¹—comparable to LHC’s design luminosity—and identifies viable pathways for muon production, cooling, and acceleration within existing tunnel and infrastructure constraints.

Conclusion: The LHμC is a scientifically compelling, technically feasible near-term option for a high-luminosity muon-hadron collider, offering unique physics reach—especially in neutrino-related QCD and electroweak precision measurements—and fostering strategic synergy across major future collider projects (LH$e$C, FCC, Muon Collider).

Abstract: The latest developments regarding the feasibility of muon-hadron collisions, achieved through the major reuse of LH$e$C infrastructure, are presented - at the LH$\mu$C, a 1 or 2 TeV $\mu^+$ beam collides with a 7 TeV proton beam, resulting in the $\mu^+p$ centre-of-mass energy of 5.3 or 7.5 TeV, respectively, at an average luminosity close to $10^{34}$cm$^{-2}$s$^{-1}$. First, a concise scientific motivation is given, including exceptional prospects for neutrino science, and is followed by a presentation of the LH$\mu$C concept in a nutshell - from the source of ultra-cold anti-muons, through the muon booster, to the main muon ring. Basic considerations regarding the machine-detector interface and the general-purpose detector are also presented. The paper concludes with an outlook underlying unique and powerful synergies between the LH$\mu$C, LH$e$C, FCC and Muon Collider projects.

</details>

Result: The $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ state significantly improves the fit ($>5\sigma$), yielding the first observation of its decay to $D^{*-} D^0$; measured mass, width, and quantum numbers consistent with prior observations in $\psi(2S)\pi^-$ channel.

Conclusion: The $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ is confirmed as a genuine resonant state in the $D^{*-}D^0$ system, supporting its interpretation as an exotic tetraquark; this provides compelling evidence for non-$q\bar{q}$ hadrons in charm–anticharm spectroscopy.

Abstract: A study of resonant structures in $B^0 \to D^{*-}D^0K^+$ decays is presented. The analysis uses proton-proton collision data collected by the LHCb experiment at center-of-mass energies of $\sqrt{s} = 7$, 8, and 13 TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 9 fb$^{-1}$. The analysis demonstrates that the existing known excited $D_s^+$ resonances in the $D^0K^+$ spectrum are insufficient to describe the data. The inclusion of the tetraquark state $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ improves the description of the data with a statistical significance exceeding $5\sigma$. This corresponds to the first observation of the $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^- \to D^{*-} D^0$ decay. The measured properties of the $T_{c\bar{c}1}(4430)^-$ state are consistent with those previously measured in its decay to the $\psi(2S)\pi^-$ final state.

### [34] [LLMs Can Tackle High Energy Theoretical Physics, yet Gaps Remain: Part I, Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33134)
*Tong Liu,Bohan Li,Gongrui Cheng,Zixiong Yu,Yulan He,Volker Tresp*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces QFT-Bench, a 75-problem benchmark for evaluating LLMs on graduate- to research-level quantum field theory tasks; finds frontier models achieve >95% accuracy but reveal persistent errors in conventions, instruction following, and reasoning unfaithfulness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively assess how far LLMs can go in high-energy theoretical physics—specifically Quantum Field Theory—where deep domain knowledge, symbolic reasoning, and derivation are essential, and to identify capability boundaries and failure modes.

Method: Constructs QFT-Bench: a curated, closed-form, symbolically verifiable benchmark of 75 QFT problems spanning core subareas (e.g., perturbation theory, renormalization, path integrals); evaluates 39 proprietary and open-source LLMs using hybrid symbolic verification + LLM-based judging; analyzes chain-of-thought (CoT) fidelity to detect reasoning unfaithfulness.

Result: Frontier models achieve >95% accuracy on QFT-Bench; however, errors persist across calculation precision, convention adherence (e.g., metric signature, normalization), and instruction compliance; weaker models fail at conceptual or derivational levels; CoT analysis reveals widespread reasoning unfaithfulness—correct answers without logically sound derivations.

Conclusion: LLMs show surprising competence in nontrivial QFT reasoning, but current capabilities remain brittle and unreliable for rigorous theoretical physics work; QFT-Bench establishes a foundation for systematic, domain-specific evaluation in high-energy theory.

Abstract: Beyond math and code, Large language models (LLMs) are beginning to tackle problems that have traditionally required substantial training for theoretical physicists, but it remains unclear how far this capability extends and where it might break. As a first step toward pushing LLMs in high-energy theoretical physics, we introduce QFT-Bench, a benchmark of 75 graduate- to research-level problems requiring extensive reasoning and derivation across core Quantum Field Theory (QFT) subareas. Each question has a well-defined target and closed-form reference answer. Evaluation combines symbolic verification with LLM-based judge. Across 39 proprietary and open-source models, frontier models already demonstrate substantial capabilities on nontrivial QFT calculations, with the strongest models exceeding 95% accuracy. At the same time, clear gaps remain. Even strong models still make errors in calculations, conventions, and instruction following, while weaker models often fail at the level of physical concepts or derivations. We further monitor models' CoTs and show that current models exhibit nontrivial reasoning unfaithfulness, i.e., they could reach correct answers but the reasoning itself does not justify and logically support them. These results provide a quantitative assessment of current LLM capabilities in QFT and a first step toward broader evaluation in high-energy theoretical physics.

</details>


### [35] [Dark Matter phase space distribution under a fully relativistic treatment. Eddington inversion according to general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33150)
*Jaime Hoefken Zink,Pellegrino Piantadosi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends Eddington inversion to a fully relativistic framework using the Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations, enabling reconstruction of dark matter phase-space distribution functions from density profiles in strong-field regimes; it regularizes unphysical divergences, applies the method to a Milky Way model with a DM spike near Sgr A*, and shows significant relativistic corrections to velocity distributions and neutron star capture rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately reconstruct dark matter phase-space distribution functions in strong gravitational fields (e.g., near supermassive black holes), where Newtonian Eddington inversion fails due to relativistic effects.

Method: Develops a fully relativistic Eddington inversion by modeling dark matter as an effective perfect fluid in a static, spherically symmetric spacetime; solves the Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations for the gravitational background; derives a relativistic Abel-type integral equation for the distribution function in terms of energy measured at infinity; introduces regularization to handle finite-boundary divergence.

Result: The relativistic distribution recovers classical Eddington inversion in the weak-field limit; applied to a Milky Way NFW+spike model, relativistic peak speeds near Sgr A* reach ~2× the Newtonian prediction; relativistic neutron star capture rates near Sgr A* are ~10× smaller than Maxwell-Boltzmann estimates and ~2× smaller than Newtonian Eddington predictions.

Conclusion: Relativistic phase-space reconstruction is essential for accurate dark matter phenomenology—especially for velocity distributions and capture processes—in strong gravitational fields such as those near galactic center black holes.

Abstract: We develop a fully relativistic extension of Eddington inversion to reconstruct the phase-space distribution function of dark matter from its density profile. Treating dark matter as an effective perfect fluid in a static, spherically symmetric spacetime, we use the Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations to determine the gravitational background and derive an Abel-type inversion that yields the distribution function in terms of the conserved particle energy measured at infinity. We show that the resulting expression recovers the classical Eddington inversion in the weak-field, nonrelativistic limit. We also investigate the finite-boundary contribution, which can introduce an unphysical divergence, and present a regularization procedure that removes it. We apply our method to a Milky Way model consisting of a generalized Navarro-Frenk-White halo and a Gondolo-Silk dark-matter spike around Sagittarius A$^*$, neglecting baryonic contributions. The relativistic and Newtonian velocity distributions agree at radii far from the Galactic center but begin to differ at distances of order $10^{-5}\,\mathrm{pc}$. Closer to the black hole, the relativistic peak speed can reach approximately twice the Newtonian prediction. Finally, we derive a general expression for dark-matter capture by neutron stars that accommodates arbitrary ambient velocity distributions. Near Sagittarius A$^*$, the relativistic capture factor is nearly an order of magnitude smaller than the Maxwell-Boltzmann prediction, while the Newtonian Eddington result can overestimate it by a factor of approximately two. These results demonstrate the importance of relativistic phase-space reconstruction for dark-matter phenomenology in strong gravitational fields.

</details>


### [36] [A Low Scale for Quark-Lepton Unification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33152)
*Lawrence J. Hall,Claudio Andrea Manzari,Clara Murgui*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that fermion mass patterns hint at quark-lepton unification near 10^6–10^7 GeV, specifically within low-scale Pati-Salam models; it identifies two viable theories with predictive Yukawa textures, links them to testable leptoquark-induced μ→e conversion, and shows flavor mixing and thresholds preserve the mass relations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observed fermion mass ratios (e.g., y_τ ≈ y_b, y_μ ≈ 3y_s, y_e ≈ y_d/3) suggest underlying quark-lepton unification; the paper seeks to identify whether these relations emerge coherently at a common scale compatible with observable physics and testable predictions.

Method: Analyzes renormalization-group evolution of SM Yukawa couplings; incorporates finite threshold corrections and flavor mixing effects; constructs and compares two minimal Pati-Salam models—one with generation-unified scale and one with symmetric Yukawa matrices—to reproduce all three mass relations and fix flavor structure from data.

Result: Finds y_τ = y_b and y_μ = 3y_s unify near 4.6×10⁶ GeV; y_e ≈ y_d/3 emerges later (~10⁸ GeV) but shares same Clebsch origin; identifies two viable models with predicted unification scales (4.6^{+1.2}_{−0.9}×10⁶ GeV and 1.5^{+0.7}_{−0.5}×10⁷ GeV) and a concrete prediction |V_cb| = 0.042±0.002; derives vector leptoquark couplings tied to light-family texture, enabling μ→e conversion as a direct probe.

Conclusion: Low-scale Pati-Salam unification is strongly motivated by fermion mass relations; the measured masses constrain both the unification scale and flavor structure; upcoming μ→e conversion experiments can decisively test these models.

Abstract: The measured fermion masses may be pointing to the scale of quark-lepton unification. Studying the renormalization-group evolution of the Standard Model Yukawa couplings, we find that $y_\tau=y_b$ and $y_\mu=3y_s$ occur at nearly the same scale, around $10^6$ GeV, while the first generation reaches the Georgi-Jarlskog relation $y_e\simeq y_d/3$ at order $10^8$ GeV and can arise from the same Clebsch structure through a simple hierarchical flavor texture. This pattern naturally suggests low-scale Pati-Salam symmetry. We show that flavor mixing and finite threshold corrections preserve this picture. If the electron mass has an independent origin, the two heavier-generation relations can be realized in theories with a unification scale as low as $(5$-$7)\times10^5$ GeV. We identify two simple theories that accommodate all three relations and in which the measured masses fix the flavor structure. The first unifies all three generations at $4.6^{+1.2}_{-0.9}\times10^6$ GeV; the second, with symmetric Yukawa matrices, at $1.5^{+0.7}_{-0.5}\times10^7$ GeV, and predicts $|V_{cb}|=0.042\pm0.002$. Their shared light-family texture fixes the relevant vector-leptoquark couplings, making $\mu\to e$ conversion in nuclei a direct test of unification: upcoming searches will probe part of the region selected by the two theories, and proposed facilities could cover it completely.

</details>


### [37] [Muon Tridents at FASER and NA64$μ$: Motivation, Predictions, and Prospects for Precision Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33153)
*Reinaldo Francener,Victor P. Goncalves,Felix Kling,Gabriel Rabelo-Soares*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies muon trident scattering in muon-nucleus interactions at the FASER experiment using high-energy muons from LHC pp collisions, predicting >10⁵ events in Run 3; it improves theoretical modeling—including nuclear form factors and QED Coulomb corrections—and demonstrates first-ever TeV-energy measurement capability with ~1% cross-section precision; it further extends analysis to NA64μ to probe the energy dependence of the cross-section across a broad, unexplored range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the intense forward muon flux at the LHC for studying rare electromagnetic processes—specifically muon trident scattering—at unprecedented TeV energies, where no prior measurements exist.

Method: Theoretical review and improvement of muon trident scattering formalism, including nuclear form factor treatments and higher-order QED effects (e.g., Coulomb corrections); event rate and differential distribution predictions for FASER Run 3, incorporating realistic detector acceptance and dominant backgrounds; extension to NA64μ for comparative energy-dependent analysis.

Result: Projected observation of muon trident scattering at TeV energies with >10⁵ events in FASER Run 3; percent-level precision on the cross-section; quantified sensitivity to nuclear form factors and Coulomb corrections; combined FASER + NA64μ coverage of a broad, unexplored energy range for cross-section energy dependence.

Conclusion: Muon trident scattering is measurable for the first time at TeV energies via FASER, enabling precision tests of QED in extreme kinematic regimes; synergy with NA64μ opens a new window to probe the energy evolution of the process, constraining potential BSM contributions and validating nuclear/QED modeling.

Abstract: The intense flux of high-energy muons produced in the far-forward region of $pp$ collisions at the LHC provides a unique opportunity to study rare electromagnetic interactions at TeV energies. In this work, we investigate the muon trident scattering in muon-nucleus interactions at the FASER experiment, which is expected to have recorded more than $10^5$ such events. The theoretical description of the process is reviewed and improved. Predictions for the total event rates and differential distributions expected during Run~3 of the LHC are presented, taking into account realistic detector acceptance and the main background contributions. The dependence of the predictions on the treatment of the nuclear form factors and the impact of higher-order QED effects such as Coulomb corrections are investigated. Our results indicate that the muon trident process can be measured for the first time at TeV energies using FASER's spectrometer, with a projected precision on the cross-section at the percent level. We also extended our analysis to the NA64$\mu$ experiment and showed that, together with FASER, these experiments can probe the energy dependence of the muon trident cross-section over a broad unexplored energy range.

</details>


### [38] [Four-loop $\overline{\rm MS}$-pole quark mass relation with two different masses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33164)
*Matteo Fael,Fabian Lange,Kay Schönwald,Matthias Steinhauser*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the four-loop QCD relation between on-shell and MS-bar heavy-quark masses, incorporating a second non-zero quark mass via a semi-analytical method valid for all mass ratios, and improves precision for 18 of 23 colour structures in limiting cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision of the heavy-quark mass relation between on-shell and MS-bar schemes at four-loop order, especially when a second non-vanishing quark mass is present—relevant for precise QCD phenomenology and renormalization-group evolution.

Method: Semi-analytical computation of the four-loop QCD matching relation between on-shell and MS-bar heavy-quark masses, including full dependence on a second non-zero quark mass; enables generalized expansions (small- and large-mass limits) with all logarithmic terms retained.

Result: Precise four-loop mass relation valid for arbitrary mass ratio; analytical control over logarithmic contributions in both m2 → 0 and m2 → m1 limits; numerical improvement in precision for 18 out of 23 colour structures compared to prior results in those limits.

Conclusion: The semi-analytical framework successfully extends the state-of-the-art four-loop heavy-quark mass relation to generic two-mass configurations, enhancing reliability of quark-mass determinations and threshold effects in high-precision QCD analyses.

Abstract: We calculate the relation between heavy-quark masses defined in the on-shell and $\overline{\rm MS}$ renormalization schemes up to four-loop order in Quantum Chromodynamics, accounting for a second, non-zero quark mass. We employ a semi-analytical method that yields precise results valid for all values of the mass ratio. In particular, our approach enables generalized expansions for small and large values of the second quark mass, incorporating all logarithmic contributions. For the limiting cases of a vanishing or equal second quark mass, we significantly improve upon the precision of known results for 18 of the 23 colour structures.

</details>


### [39] [Probing Orbital Angular Momentum Distributions in Elastic Vector Meson Production in Longitudinally Polarized Electron--Proton Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33255)
*Yuri V. Kovchegov,Brandon Manley*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the longitudinal double-spin asymmetry (DSA) for elastic vector meson production in longitudinally polarized e-p collisions at small x, identifying the cosφₖΔ azimuthal harmonic as a sensitive probe of small-x orbital angular momentum (OAM) distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop new experimental observables sensitive to quark and gluon orbital angular momentum (OAM) distributions at small Bjorken-x, where conventional probes are limited.

Method: Uses the polarized small-x formalism to compute the numerator of the longitudinal double-spin asymmetry (DSA) for elastic vector meson production, considering both transversely and longitudinally polarized mesons; focuses on the cosφₖΔ azimuthal modulation in the dipole/Trento frame.

Result: The cosφₖΔ harmonic of the DSA at small transverse momentum transfer is shown to be directly sensitive to gluon OAM distributions at small x; results are generalizable across multiple vector meson species (e.g., J/ψ, ρ, φ).

Conclusion: The identified DSA azimuthal harmonic constitutes a novel, experimentally accessible probe of small-x partonic OAM, enabling future extractions of OAM from spin-dependent small-x scattering data at EIC or other facilities.

Abstract: We consider elastic vector meson production in longitudinally polarized electron-proton collisions at small $x$. Inspired by previous pioneering proposals in the literature, we calculate the numerator of the longitudinal double-spin asymmetry (DSA) within the polarized small-$x$ formalism for both the transversely and longitudinally polarized vector mesons. Our calculation demonstrates that the $\cos\phi_{k\Delta}$ azimuthal harmonic of the DSA for the elastic vector meson production at small transverse momentum transfer (with $\phi_{k\Delta}$ the azimuthal angle between the transverse momenta of the outgoing electron and meson in the dipole/Trento frame) provides a promising probe of the orbital angular momentum (OAM) distributions at small $x$. Our results can be applied to several different vector meson species, thus opening up a new class of observables that can be used to extract OAM distributions from experimental data.

</details>


### [40] [Catastrophe Structure and Stability of Axion Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33549)
*Fedor V Kusmartsev,Joudy J F Beek,Jack C M Hughes*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified stability analysis of axion stars using Schrödinger–Poisson dynamics and catastrophe theory, identifying stable dilute/dense branches separated by an unstable branch via A₂–A₄ catastrophes; it extends beyond the sextic approximation to full cosine and QCD-corrected potentials, distinguishes variational from full radial stability, and defines critical masses for collapse, bosenova, and black-hole formation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether axion stars—self-gravitating Bose–Einstein condensates of axions—can persist as viable dark-matter structures and produce observable astrophysical signatures, a rigorous, unified stability classification is needed beyond ad hoc approximations.

Method: Combines non-relativistic Schrödinger–Poisson dynamics with catastrophe theory; derives effective equations from the Einstein–Klein–Gordon system with periodic (cosine/QCD-corrected) axion potential; constructs one-parameter star solutions; employs Gaussian variational ansatz to obtain analytic energy functional E(R;N); identifies equilibrium bifurcations as Arnold A₂, A₃, A₄ catastrophes; distinguishes collective-coordinate (variational) stability from full radial perturbative stability; assesses validity domain of non-relativistic approximation.

Result: Reveals two stable axion-star branches (dilute and dense) separated by an unstable branch due to repulsive sextic interaction; fold conditions for equilibria generalize to full cosine and QCD potentials via numerical evaluation of variational energy; establishes precise criteria for collapse, bosenova-like emission, and black-hole formation through model-dependent critical masses; clarifies limits of non-relativistic treatment and separates variational from true radial stability.

Conclusion: Catastrophe theory provides a robust, compact phase classification for axion stars across realistic axion potentials; stability is governed by universal bifurcation structures (A₂–A₄), enabling predictive identification of critical masses and astrophysical endpoints—essential for interpreting axion dark matter phenomenology.

Abstract: Axion stars are self-gravitating Bose--Einstein condensates of QCD axions or axion-like particles. Their stability determines whether they can survive as dark-matter structures and what astrophysical signatures they may produce. Here we combine a non-relativistic Schrödinger--Poisson description with catastrophe theory to develop a unified picture of axion-star stability. Beginning with the Einstein--Klein--Gordon system for a periodic axion potential, we derive the effective equations of motion and conserved quantities and construct a one-parameter family of star-like solutions. A Gaussian variational ansatz reduces the problem to an analytic energy functional $E(R;N)$. Within this model, equilibria appear and disappear through Arnold $A_2$, $A_3$, and $A_4$ catastrophes as the particle number $N$ and interaction strengths vary. A repulsive sextic interaction produces two stable branches---dilute and dense axion stars---separated by an unstable branch. The same catastrophe criteria apply beyond the sextic truncation: for the full cosine and QCD-corrected potentials, the fold conditions follow from the complete variational energy and can be evaluated numerically. We also separate stability within the collective-coordinate ansatz from full radial perturbative stability and specify the range in which the non-relativistic treatment is reliable. This framework provides a compact classification of axion-star phases and identifies model-dependent critical masses associated with collapse, bosenova-like emission, and black-hole formation.

</details>


### [41] [A Tool-Grounded AI Agent for Gauge Coupling Unification and Proton Decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33593)
*Shaikh Saad*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: proton_agent is a tool-grounded AI agent for natural-language-driven computational analysis of grand unified theories, supporting gauge coupling unification and proton decay calculations with full local deployment support for open-source LLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between high-energy theoretical physics research and accessible, reproducible, and interactive computational tools—enabling physicists to explore grand unified theories (GUTs) via natural language without sacrificing numerical accuracy or requiring cloud-based proprietary models.

Method: Develops a deterministic scientific backend integrated with an LLM-based agent (proton_agent) that parses natural-language queries, selects/executes domain-specific computational tools (e.g., beta-function solvers, RG evolution engines, proton decay amplitude calculators), and interprets outputs; supports multiple GUT symmetry-breaking chains (e.g., SO(10) subgroups), both supersymmetric and non-supersymmetric models, and diverse sampling/exploration strategies (grid, random, MCMC).

Result: A fully functional, locally deployable framework capable of computing one- and two-loop gauge beta coefficients, performing multi-stage RG evolution across intermediate symmetries, evaluating dimension-five and -six proton decay amplitudes, conducting parameter scans and statistical inference, comparing against experimental bounds, and generating publication-quality visualizations.

Conclusion: proton_agent establishes a new paradigm for physics-aware, tool-integrated AI agents—demonstrating that rigorous theoretical particle physics computations can be made accessible, reproducible, and interactive through natural language while preserving scientific fidelity and open-source usability.

Abstract: We present proton_agent, a tool-grounded AI agent that enables natural-language interaction with computational tools for gauge coupling unification and proton decay in grand unified theories. The agent translates user requests into computational tasks, selects and executes the appropriate tools, and interprets their outputs. All numerical calculations, model evaluations, experimental comparisons, parameter scans, and visualizations are handled by a deterministic scientific backend to ensure numerical accuracy and reproducibility. A central objective of this framework is to enable fully local deployment using free and open-source LLMs, while retaining compatibility with commercial LLM APIs. The framework computes one- and two-loop gauge beta coefficients for user-defined scalar and fermion multiplets and performs gauge coupling evolution through the Standard Model and various intermediate symmetry-breaking stages of $SO(10)$, including $SU(3)_C \times SU(2)_L \times SU(2)_R \times U(1)_{B-L}$, $SU(3)_C \times SU(2)_L \times U(1)_R \times U(1)_{B-L}$, $SU(4)_C \times SU(2)_L \times U(1)_R$, and $SU(4)_C \times SU(2)_L \times SU(2)_R$. For proton decay, the framework evaluates gauge-mediated dimension-six contributions in non-supersymmetric $SU(5)$ and $SO(10)$, as well as dressed dimension-five contributions in their supersymmetric counterparts. It supports benchmark calculations for user-specified parameter points, random and grid-based parameter scans, Markov-chain Monte Carlo exploration, comparisons with experimental limits, and publication-ready two- and three-dimensional visualizations. We describe the agent architecture, demonstrate its capabilities through representative benchmark calculations and regression tests, and discuss its current physics scope.

</details>


### [42] [Soft factor of quasi-TMDPDFs from the soft limit of the quasi beam function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33638)
*Junegone Chay*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that the soft factor required for matching quasi-TMDPDFs to TMDPDFs is inherently contained within the quasi beam function, not constructed separately; by decomposing the one-loop quasi beam function via the method of regions, it identifies the soft region contribution as encoding the square root of the lightcone soft function and a reduced soft function, leading to an operator-level definition of the soft factor that ensures all-order consistency—including exact reproduction of the Collins-Soper kernel.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ad hoc separation of the soft factor from the quasi beam function in large-momentum effective theory (LaMET), and to establish a rigorous, operator-based foundation for the soft subtraction in quasi-TMDPDF matching.

Method: One-loop calculation of the quasi beam function combined with the method of regions decomposition; identification and analysis of hard, collinear, and soft contributions; reinterpretation of the soft-region contribution via hemisphere folding of gluon momentum; derivation of an operator definition for the soft factor as a vacuum matrix element of soft Wilson lines.

Result: The soft factor is shown to be intrinsically embedded in the quasi beam function; the soft-region contribution—despite involving only one lightlike direction—reproduces both the square root of the lightcone soft function and a reduced soft function upon hemisphere folding; an explicit operator definition of the soft factor is obtained.

Conclusion: The quasi beam function fully encodes the necessary soft physics for TMD matching, eliminating the need for external soft-factor construction; the derived operator definition guarantees all-order consistency and correctly reproduces the Collins-Soper evolution kernel.

Abstract: The quasi-TMDPDF in large-momentum effective theory is related to the TMDPDF through a soft factor, which has so far been constructed separately from the quasi beam function. We show that the soft factor is contained in the quasi beam function itself. Computing the one-loop quasi beam function and decomposing it by the method of regions, we find that the hard and collinear regions give the matching coefficient and the lightcone beam function. The soft region involves only one lightlike direction, yet, once the two hemispheres of the gluon momentum are folded onto each other, it reproduces the square root of the lightcone soft function together with the reduced soft function. The analysis leads to an operator definition of the soft factor, as the vacuum matrix element of the soft Wilson lines into which the quasi beam function turns. This definition provides the basis for an all-order analysis, and it shows that the Collins-Soper kernel is reproduced to all orders.

</details>


### [43] [Relativistic corrections to $Υ(1S,2S)\rightarrowγη^{(\prime)}$ in perturbative QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33662)
*Cong Wang,Chao-Jie Fan,Jun-Kang He*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates radiative decays Υ(1S,2S) → γη(′) in pQCD at O(q²) relativistic order, finding quark contribution dominates, electromagnetic interference is constructive, gluon contribution suppressed; relativistic corrections modestly reduce Υ(1S) rates but enhance Υ(2S) rates by 17–30%; predicted γη′/γη branching ratio is highly sensitive to light-meson mixing schemes, with central predictions exceeding experimental upper limits by factors ~5–7 at μ = m_Υ/2, but agreement is restored when including η_b mixing (destructive interference) and using μ = m_Υ and mixing parameters from transition form factors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between theoretical predictions and experimental upper limits for Υ(1S,2S) → γη(′) decays, particularly the overprediction of B(Υ(1S) → γη′), by systematically including relativistic corrections, light-cone distribution amplitudes, light-quark mass effects, electromagnetic and two-gluon contributions, and heavy-pseudoscalar (η_c, η_b) admixtures in η/η′.

Method: Perturbative QCD calculation at leading order in α_s and up to O(q²) in the nonrelativistic expansion; includes quark-annihilation, two-gluon, and electromagnetic (photon emission from quark lines) diagrams; incorporates light-cone distribution amplitudes for η/η′, SU(3)-breaking light-quark mass effects, η–η′ mixing via two distinct phenomenological schemes (e.g., FKS vs. transition-form-factor–inferred), and heavy-pseudoscalar (η_c, η_b) mixing contributions treated as corrections to the light-meson wave functions.

Result: Quark contribution dominates; EM interference is constructive; gluon contribution is strongly suppressed; relativistic corrections reduce Υ(1S) rates by a few percent but increase Υ(2S) rates by 17–30%; B(Υ(1S)→γη′)/B(Υ(1S)→γη) varies by factor ~5 between mixing schemes; central predictions exceed experimental upper limit by factors ~5–7 at scale μ = m_Υ/2; inclusion of η_b mixing induces destructive interference that alleviates discrepancy; at μ = m_Υ and with mixing parameters from transition form factors, prediction becomes compatible with upper limit within uncertainties.

Conclusion: The Υ → γη(′) decays are theoretically sensitive probes of η–η′ mixing and heavy-quarkonium–light-meson couplings; while standard pQCD predictions overshoot data without proper treatment of scale and mixing, consistency with experiment is achieved upon incorporating η_b admixture and adopting the quarkonium mass scale and mixing parameters constrained by electromagnetic transition form factors—highlighting the necessity of consistent scale choice and refined mixing models in precision quarkonium phenomenology.

Abstract: We calculate the radiative decays $\Upsilon(1S),\Upsilon(2S)\rightarrow\gamma\eta^{(\prime)}$ in perturbative quantum chromodynamics through order $q^{2}$ in the relativistic expansion, including the quark-content, two-gluon, and electromagnetic contributions. The quark contribution dominates, with substantial constructive electromagnetic interference and a strongly suppressed gluon contribution. The annihilation rates depend weakly on the tested light-cone distribution amplitudes and light-quark masses. Relativistic corrections reduce the $\Upsilon(1S)$ rates by a few percent but enhance the $\Upsilon(2S)$ rates by approximately $17\%$--$30\%$. The ratio of the $\gamma\eta^{\prime}$ and $\gamma\eta$ branching fractions is sensitive to the light-meson mixing parameters, with predictions differing by about a factor of $5$ between two representative schemes. At a scale of half the quarkonium mass, however, the $\Upsilon(1S)\rightarrow\gamma\eta^{\prime}$ central predictions in the two mixing schemes exceed the experimental upper limit by factors of approximately $5$ and $7$. We also examine the heavy-pseudoscalar admixtures in the light mesons. The $\eta_c$ contribution is small, whereas $\eta_b$ mixing can substantially modify the rates through interference. Destructive interference alleviates the discrepancy. With the scale set to the quarkonium mass, the prediction in the mixing scheme inferred from transition form factors is compatible with the upper limit within the input uncertainty.

</details>

*Biprajit Mondal*

TL;DR: This paper studies constraints on millicharge particles from Hawking evaporation of primordial black holes using electron recoil data from XENONnT, JUNO, and Super-K detectors, focusing on energy loss in galactic/terrestrial media and yielding strong limits in the high-mass regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe millicharge particles—potential dark matter candidates or beyond-Standard-Model relics—via their production from Hawking radiation of neutral primordial black holes (PBHs), leveraging existing direct detection experiments.

Method: Modeling propagation of millicharge particles through Galactic magnetic fields and Earth's overburden, computing energy loss, and deriving exclusion limits in the mass–charge plane using electron recoil sensitivity of XENONnT, JUNO, and Super-K for two PBH initial mass–abundance benchmarks.

Abstract: We investigate the direct detection of millicharge particles produced via Hawking evaporation of neutral primordial black holes (PBHs). As they travel through the Galactic magnetic field and then Earth's overburden, they lose energy before reaching an underground detector. Using electron recoil signals, we obtain constraints on the mass and charge parameter space for XENONnT, JUNO and Super-K for evolved PBH populations with initial mass $ M_i=10^{15}~\rm g$ and $ M_i=8\times10^{15}~\rm g$ with initial abundances $f_i^{\rm PBH}=3.9\times10^{-7}$ and $f_i^{\rm PBH}=3.7\times10^{-4}$, respectively. In the relevant parameter space, this model can provide strong constraints in the high mass region.

</details>

*Weiqiang Yang,Sibo Zhang,Supriya Pan,Luis A. Anchordoqui,Eleonora Di Valentino*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper tests three interacting dark matter–dark energy models against CMB, BAO, and multiple SN Ia datasets; all models yield negative effective neutrino masses—contradicting oscillation-based lower bounds—and fail to resolve the DESI neutrino-mass tension, with coupling significances up to ~3σ but unphysical neutrino mass preferences.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether interacting dark matter–dark energy models can resolve the tension between cosmological constraints (e.g., from DESI) and the minimum total neutrino mass implied by neutrino oscillation experiments.

Method: Bayesian parameter inference using cosmic microwave background (Planck), DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations, and Type Ia supernova data (PantheonPlus, Union3, DES-Dovekie); models include interacting vacuum energy and interacting dark energy with constant w in phantom/quintessence regimes; neutrino mass treated either as fixed or as a freely varying effective parameter; positivity prior on neutrino mass relaxed to test robustness.

Result: All three interaction scenarios prefer negative effective neutrino masses across all dataset combinations; no 95% CL interval includes the oscillation-motivated minimum mass (~0.06 eV); nonzero coupling is favored at ~3σ when neutrino mass is free, but preferred values remain unphysically negative.

Conclusion: The considered interacting dark matter–dark energy models—with either vacuum or constant-w dark energy—do not alleviate the DESI neutrino-mass tension; the persistent preference for negative m_eff suggests a fundamental incompatibility of these models with current joint cosmological probes.

Abstract: We constrain three interacting dark matter--dark energy scenarios using cosmic microwave background measurements, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations, and the PantheonPlus, Union3, and DES-Dovekie Type Ia supernova compilations. We consider interacting vacuum energy and interacting dark energy with a constant equation of state in the phantom and quintessence regimes, analyzing each scenario with either a fixed total neutrino mass or a freely varying effective neutrino mass. When the positivity prior is relaxed, all three scenarios favor negative effective neutrino masses for every dataset combination. Although some combinations permit small positive masses, none of the 95\% confidence intervals reaches the minimum mass required by neutrino oscillation experiments. Allowing the effective neutrino mass to vary produces significances for a nonzero coupling reaching approximately $3\sigma$, but the preferred masses remain negative. These results demonstrate that the interaction and constant dark energy equations of state considered here fail to resolve the DESI neutrino-mass tension.

</details>


### [46] [Dissecting flavor tagging with graph neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33757)
*Ankita Budhraja,Sunny Kumar Singh,Balbeer Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how ParticleNet, a graph neural network for quark/gluon jet tagging, distributes its discriminating power between jet constituent multiplicity and substructure—finding that it jointly uses multiplicity and radial spread (a substructure feature), especially at low/intermediate multiplicities where multiplicity alone is ambiguous.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle how much of ParticleNet's quark/gluon discrimination arises from constituent multiplicity versus other structural features—and how this varies with jet pT and multiplicity.

Method: Trained ParticleNet on matched Z+jet Pythia samples (80–400 GeV pT); analyzed output vs. true gluon fraction across multiplicity bins; decomposed network output into multiplicity-dependent and multiplicity-fixed log-likelihood ratios; probed substructure sensitivity via radial rescaling of constituents at fixed multiplicity.

Result: ParticleNet learns the pT-dependent shift in multiplicity where quark/gluon likelihoods cross; at fixed multiplicity, its outputs scatter widely around the true gluon fraction, enabling correction of ~20–34% misclassifications by multiplicity alone; radial broadening increases gluon score most strongly at low/intermediate multiplicities.

Conclusion: ParticleNet’s discrimination is not dominated solely by multiplicity—it integrates multiplicity with geometric substructure (e.g., radial spread), and its output can be meaningfully interpreted as a sum of multiplicity and residual (substructure) log-likelihood ratios.

Abstract: Graph neural networks such as ParticleNet are widely used for quark/gluon jet tagging. We study how the discriminating power of such a network is distributed between constituent multiplicity and the remaining structure of the jet, differentially in jet transverse momentum and multiplicity. To this end, we train ParticleNet on quark and gluon jets from Z+jet production in Pythia, with the two classes matched in transverse momentum and rapidity over the wide range $80\leq p_{T}^{\rm jet}\leq 400$ GeV. We find that the network learns how the fraction of gluon jets changes with the multiplicity in every $p_{T}^{\rm jet}$ bin, including the shift of the multiplicity at which quark and gluon jets are equally likely, which implies that it uses the jet scale (reconstructed from its constituents) jointly with its substructure. At fixed multiplicity, the gluon probabilities it assigns agree on average with the observed gluon fractions, but are widely spread about the mean. This allows us to interpret its output as the sum of the multiplicity log-likelihood ratio and a residual log-likelihood ratio at fixed multiplicity, which allows the network to correctly identify 20-34% of the jets whose multiplicity favors the opposite class. By rescaling the radial spread of the constituents at fixed multiplicity, we find that the network considers broader jets more gluon-like, and that this response is largest at low and intermediate multiplicity, where the multiplicity alone does not decide the class of a jet.

</details>


### [47] [Fermiophobic Z' Bosons in 3-4-1 Models: Classification and Collider Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33805)
*Eduard Suarez,Yithsbey Giraldo,Eduardo Rojas*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes SU(3)_C ⊗ SU(4)_L ⊗ U(1)_X models with two charge assignments (b=c=1 and b=1,c=−2), classifies anomaly-free fermion spectra, computes Z′ chiral couplings to SM fermions, and derives ATLAS dilepton constraints—finding a fermiophobic limit for b=c=1 where Z′ couplings vanish at ψ=nπ, evading standard Drell-Yan bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how fermion content and gauge-boson mixing in 3-4-1 models affect Z′ phenomenology and collider constraints—particularly whether light Z′ bosons can evade current dilepton searches.

Method: Systematic classification of anomaly-free fermion representations under SU(4)_L; derivation of electric charge operator and chiral Z′ couplings; computation of dilepton production cross sections; reinterpretation of ATLAS 13 TeV dilepton resonance search (139 fb⁻¹) under assumptions of negligible Z–Z′ mixing and decoupled exotic fermions.

Result: Five minimal, three-family, anomaly-free fermion spectra identified per charge configuration; distinct spectra can yield identical low-energy neutral-current interactions; b=c=1 model exhibits a universal fermiophobic limit (Z′ couplings ∝ sinψ → 0 at ψ=nπ), nullifying Drell-Yan constraints; b=1,c=−2 lacks such a limit; thus, light Z′ bosons remain viable only in specific realizations.

Conclusion: Collider limits on Z′ bosons are highly sensitive to the underlying fermion embedding and neutral gauge-boson mixing structure; the existence of a fermiophobic limit in the b=c=1 3-4-1 model opens a window for light, weakly coupled Z′ bosons consistent with current data.

Abstract: We investigate the fermion spectra and collider phenomenology of models based on the \(SU(3)_C \otimes SU(4)_L \otimes U(1)_X\) gauge symmetry, focusing on two realizations of the electric charge operator characterized by \(b=c=1\) and \(b=1,\ c=-2\). For each configuration, we classify anomaly-free fermion sets and identify five minimal spectra with at least three SM-like quark and lepton families for each configuration, distinguishing algebraically irreducible structures from reducible spectra that remain minimal under the three-family requirement. We derive the chiral couplings of the additional neutral gauge boson \(Z'\) to Standard Model fermions and show that distinct anomaly-free fermion spectra can lead to identical low-energy neutral-current interactions. We then investigate the corresponding collider constraints using the ATLAS dilepton resonance search at \(\sqrt{s}=13\) TeV with an integrated luminosity of \(139\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}\), assuming negligible \(Z-Z'\) mixing and kinematically inaccessible exotic fermions. A particularly interesting result emerges for the \(b=c=1\) configuration, where all \(Z'\) couplings to Standard Model fermions are proportional to \(\sin\psi\), with \(\psi\) denoting the additional neutral gauge-boson mixing angle. Consequently, these couplings vanish simultaneously at \(\psi=n\pi\), giving rise to a fermiophobic limit in which conventional Drell-Yan dilepton constraints disappear. In contrast, the \(b=1,\ c=-2\) configuration does not exhibit a universal fermiophobic limit. Our results demonstrate that collider constraints on additional neutral gauge bosons depend sensitively on the fermion embeddings and neutral gauge-boson mixing structure, and suggest the possibility of comparatively light \(Z'\) bosons in suitable realizations of extended electroweak gauge theories.

</details>


### [48] [Radiative Higgs decay to $ψ\left(4230\right)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34117)
*Ahmed Ali,Muhammad Jamil Aslam*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the radiative Higgs decay H → ψ(4230)γ both in the SM and beyond, treating ψ(4230) as a tetraquark candidate with quantum numbers 0⁻(1^{--}); it introduces a direct amplitude parametrization based on the ψ(4230)’s leptonic coupling and finds SM branching ratio ℬ ≈ 4.9×10⁻⁷ (dominated by experimental uncertainty in Γ_{ψ→ℓℓ}), rising to ~10⁻⁶ for non-SM charm Yukawa coupling y_c = −3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe exotic hadron structure and new physics via radiative Higgs decays to the tetraquark candidate ψ(4230), leveraging its unusual quantum numbers and measurable leptonic width as a short-distance probe.

Method: Uses a model-independent parametrization of the direct amplitude for H → Vγ (V = ψ(4230)) anchored to the experimentally measured leptonic decay width Γ_{ψ→e⁺e⁻}, incorporating QCD scaling and SM electroweak couplings; includes variation of charm Yukawa coupling y_c as BSM benchmark.

Result: Predicted SM branching ratio ℬ(H → ψ(4230)γ) = 4.88⁺⁴·⁷¹_−₄·₅₂ × 10⁻⁷; uncertainty dominated by experimental error on Γ_{ψ(4230)}; for BSM scenario with y_c = −3, ℬ increases to ~10⁻⁶.

Conclusion: H → ψ(4230)γ is a viable channel to test tetraquark structure and constrain or discover BSM charm-Higgs couplings; current sensitivity allows probing |y_c| ≳ 1–2 at future Higgs factories.

Abstract: We calculate the decay rate for the radiative Higgs decay $H \to \psi(4230) + \gamma$ within and beyond the SM. The vector state $\psi(4230)$ is a candidate exotic tetraquark state and has the quantum numbers $I^G(J^{PC})=0^-(1^{--})$, having a mass $m_{\psi(4230)}=4221.7 \pm 2.5$ MeV and a decay width $\Gamma_{\psi(4230)}=51 \pm 7$ MeV. We propose a practical parametrization of the so-called {\it direct amplitude}, using the leptonic coupling of the $\psi(4230)$ to estimate the short-distance overlap relevant to the Higgs decay amplitude. We find ${\cal B}_{\rm SM}( H \to \psi(4230) \gamma)=4.88^{+4.71}_{-4.52}\times 10^{-7}$, where the uncertainties are dominated by the experimental uncertainty in the leptonic decay width of the $\psi(4230)$. The branching ratio increases to about $10^{-6}$ for $y_c=-3 $, where $y_c$ is the Higgs-Yukawa coupling of the charm quark.

</details>


### [49] [Anomalous Soft Photons: A Brief Overview](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34216)
*Klaus Reygers*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper summarizes experimental efforts addressing the soft-photon puzzle—the persistent discrepancy between soft-photon data in hadronic reactions and predictions from Low's soft-photon theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify and consolidate the experimental status of the soft-photon puzzle, which challenges the applicability of Low's soft-photon theorem in hadronic reactions.

Method: Review and synthesis of representative experimental measurements, theoretical baseline (Low's theorem), and identification of key experimental challenges.

Result: A compact experimental overview highlighting the unresolved discrepancy, measurement limitations, and open questions regarding soft-photon emission in hadronic processes.

Conclusion: The soft-photon puzzle remains unresolved; improved precision experiments and refined theoretical treatment—including possible higher-order QED and hadronic corrections—are needed.

Abstract: These conference proceedings provide a compact experimental perspective on the soft-photon puzzle. The puzzle refers to the long-standing discrepancy between soft-photon data in hadronic reactions and predictions based on Low's soft-photon theorem. We summarize the theoretical baseline, representative measurements, and the main experimental challenges. For a detailed account of the experimental history and theory, we refer to the recent review article Phys. Rept., 1097:1, 2024.

</details>


### [50] [Nucleon polarizabilities revisited: insights from the Bernabéu-Ericson-Ferro Fontan-Tarrach sum rule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34276)
*Xiong-Hui Cao,Ling-Yun Dai,Feng-Kun Guo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a dispersive evaluation of the BEFT sum rule for nucleon dipole polarizabilities (α_E1 − β_M1), using hyperbolic dispersion relations to separate s- and t-channel contributions cleanly. It updates s-channel input with modern πN multipoles and non-πN estimates, and improves t-channel input with dispersive γγ→ππ/KK̄ data, Roy–Steiner solutions for ππ/KK̄→NN̄, and new inclusion of isovector πη/KK̄ (I_t=1) channels. The analysis resolves prior inconsistencies from the scalar-isoscalar 2π channel and yields high-precision polarizability values for proton and neutron.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing discrepancy in prior BEFT evaluations of the nucleon polarizability difference α_E1 − β_M1—particularly driven by uncontrolled treatment of the scalar-isoscalar two-pion t-channel—and to provide a model-independent, dispersive benchmark for both proton and neutron polarizabilities, especially where experimental constraints are weak (e.g., neutron).

Method: Uses hyperbolic dispersion relations tailored for backward real Compton scattering to rigorously separate s- and t-channel contributions without double counting. Updates s-channel with modern single-pion photoproduction multipoles (from MAID/SAID) and model-based estimates for non-πN inelastic channels. For t-channel: employs contemporary dispersive γγ→ππ/KK̄_{I_t=0} amplitudes, Roy–Steiner equation solutions for ππ/KK̄_{I_t=0}→NN̄, and extends to isovector πη/KK̄_{I_t=1} (a_0(980)-driven) channel. Combines final BEFT result with Baldin sum rule to extract individual α_E1 and β_M1.

Result: Obtains precise nucleon dipole polarizabilities in units of 10⁻⁴ fm³: α_E1^p = 11.17(52), β_M1^p = 2.84(52), α_E1^n = 12.18(59), β_M1^n = 3.03(59). Resolves prior BEFT puzzle via improved t-channel treatment; neutron values represent first high-precision independent dispersive benchmark.

Conclusion: The updated dispersive BEFT analysis delivers state-of-the-art precision for both proton and neutron electric and magnetic dipole polarizabilities, with uncertainties comparable to best chiral EFT and dispersive extractions. Its rigorous separation of dynamics and inclusion of isovector t-channels establishes a robust, model-independent standard—especially critical for the poorly constrained neutron polarizabilities.

Abstract: We present a complete dispersive evaluation of the Bernabéu-Ericson-Ferro Fontan-Tarrach (BEFT) sum rule for the nucleon dipole polarizability difference $\alpha_{E1}-\beta_{M1}$. The analysis is based on hyperbolic dispersion relations, which separate the $s$- and $t$-channel contributions without double counting and are particularly suited to backward real Compton scattering. This framework makes it possible to precisely determine the polarizability difference from independent dispersive input rather than from fits to low-energy Compton scattering data. The $s$-channel part is updated with modern single-pion photoproduction multipoles and model estimates of non-$\pi N$ inelastic channels. For the $t$-channel, we use contemporary dispersive input for $\gamma\gamma\to\pi\pi/K\bar K_{I_t=0}$ together with Roy-Steiner equation solutions for $\pi\pi/K\bar K_{I_t=0}\to N\bar N$, and we extend the calculation to the isovector $\pi\eta/K\bar K_{I_t=1}$ channel associated with the $a_0(980)$. The resulting treatment resolves the long-standing puzzle of earlier BEFT evaluations driven by the scalar-isoscalar two-pion channel and gives a controlled estimate of the subleading isovector contribution. Combining the resulting BEFT values with the Baldin sum rule, we obtain, in units of $10^{-4}\,\mathrm{fm}^3$, \[ \alpha_{E1}^p=11.17(52),\quad \beta_{M1}^p=2.84(52),\quad \alpha_{E1}^n=12.18(59),\quad \beta_{M1}^n=3.03(59). \] For the proton, these values provide a benchmark with a precision comparable to state-of-the-art dispersive and chiral effective field theory extractions. For the neutron, whose polarizabilities are much less directly constrained experimentally, the result provides an independent dispersive benchmark with an uncertainty comparable to that of the proton.

</details>


### [51] [High-order temperature fluctuations in hot and dense QCD matter from multi-charge holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34414)
*Yidian Chen,Peiyu Yan,Zhibin Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies temperature cumulants (2nd–6th order) across the QCD phase diagram using a lattice-calibrated multi-charge Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton model under heavy-ion charge constraints; it identifies critical signatures—e.g., variance suppression and sign reversals in higher cumulants—near the critical end point (~547, 112 MeV), validates mean-field critical behavior via cumulant ratios, confirms δ = 3 for the critical-isotherm exponent, and reveals nonmonotonic freeze-out behavior near √sNN ≈ 6 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe thermal response and critical scaling of the QCD phase diagram using temperature cumulants—complementary to conserved-charge fluctuations—and locate and characterize the chiral critical end point (CEP).

Method: Lattice-calibrated multi-charge Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton (EMD) model constrained by heavy-ion net-charge data; computation of temperature cumulants up to sixth order; analysis of cumulant ratios, critical exponents (e.g., δ), and freeze-out trajectories across collision energies.

Result: Near the CEP at (μ_B, T) ≈ (547, 112) MeV: (i) temperature variance is locally suppressed due to enhanced heat capacity; (ii) 3rd–6th-order cumulants exhibit sign reversals on the high-T side, with onset shifting to lower μ_B as order increases; (iii) cumulant ratios confirm mean-field critical behavior; (iv) critical-isotherm exponent δ = 3 is satisfied; (v) freeze-out variance shows a local minimum near √s_NN ≈ 6 GeV, path-dependent in position and depth.

Conclusion: Temperature cumulants are sensitive probes of criticality and thermal response in the QCD phase diagram; their pattern—especially variance suppression and ordered sign reversals—provides robust, complementary evidence for the CEP and its mean-field universality class, independent of conserved-charge observables.

Abstract: We investigate temperature cumulants from second to sixth order across the QCD phase diagram using a lattice-calibrated multi-charge Einstein--Maxwells--dilaton model under heavy-ion charge constraints. Near the model's critical end point at $(\mu_B,T)\simeq(547,112)$~MeV, the enhanced heat capacity produces a local suppression of the temperature variance. The higher-order cumulants develop sign reversals on the high-temperature side of the transition, with the onset moving to lower baryon chemical potentials as the order increases. Ratios of different cumulant orders provide a consistency check of the expected mean-field critical behavior. At fixed chemical potentials, their limits on both sides of the critical point agree with the predictions for the critical-isotherm exponent $\delta=3$. The freeze-out analysis reveals nonmonotonic collision-energy dependence that need not be accompanied by sign reversals. On a representative trajectory, the variance reaches a local minimum near $\sqrt{s_{NN}}\simeq6$~GeV, with its position and depth depending on the freeze-out path. Temperature cumulants probe the thermal response and its critical scaling, providing information complementary to conserved-charge fluctuations for exploring the QCD phase diagram.

</details>


### [52] [Axions from Betelgeuse: Probing Axion Couplings with $^{23}\rm{Na}$ and $^{55}\rm{Mn}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34540)
*Nicholas A. M. Revel,Baldeep Grewal,Anupam Ray,Aaron C. Vincent*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper searches for axion-like particles (ALPs) produced via nuclear de-excitation in Betelgeuse using INTEGRAL-ISGRI and NuSTAR X-ray data, setting new 95% CL upper limits on the product of ALP-photon and ALP-proton couplings (|g_aγ g_ap|), with strongest constraints from the 23Na line and improved robustness from the 55Mn line.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe ALPs — well-motivated dark matter and BSM physics candidates — via stellar nuclear transitions in a nearby red supergiant (Betelgeuse), leveraging its high core temperature and favorable isotopic abundances (23Na, 55Mn) to enhance ALP production from thermally excited nuclear states.

Method: Calculated ALP production rates from thermal population and de-excitation of 23Na (440.2 keV) and 55Mn (124 keV) nuclear levels in Betelgeuse’s core; modeled ALP propagation, conversion in Galactic magnetic field, and two-body decay to photons; performed spectral analysis of INTEGRAL-ISGRI (23Na, 55Mn lines) and NuSTAR (55Mn line) data to search for excess X-ray lines; derived coupling limits via profile likelihood ratio test under background-only hypothesis.

Result: No significant photon excess found. Derived 95% CL upper limits: for magnetic conversion, |g_aγ g_ap| < 4.1×10⁻²¹ GeV⁻¹ (23Na, m_a < 10⁻¹⁰ eV); for decay, |g_aγ g_ap| < 3.0×10⁻²² GeV⁻¹ (23Na, m_a ≈ 400 keV) and < 2.9×10⁻¹⁹ GeV⁻¹ (55Mn, m_a ≈ 83 keV), with mass-dependent scaling (m_a⁻³/² at low masses, m_a⁻² below ~1 keV for 23Na). 23Na yields strongest sensitivity; 55Mn provides greater astrophysical robustness.

Conclusion: The analysis sets world-leading constraints on ALP-nucleon–photon coupling combinations from stellar nuclear transitions, demonstrating Betelgeuse as a powerful natural ALP laboratory; highlights trade-off between sensitivity (higher-E transition) and model independence (lower-E, more robust transition), guiding future ALP searches with next-generation X-ray observatories.

Abstract: We investigate axion-like particle (ALP) production in the interior of Betelgeuse via nuclear de-excitation of thermally populated excited states of $^{23}$Na and $^{55}$Mn. As these ALPs propagate to Earth, they can produce photons either through their conversion in the Galactic magnetic field or through their decay. Using INTEGRAL-ISGRI and NuSTAR observations, we search for these photon signals, find no significant excess, and derive 95% CL exclusion limits on the ALP couplings. For magnetic-field conversion of ALPs, the $^{23}\mathrm{Na}$ ($^{55}\mathrm{Mn}$) line observed with INTEGRAL-ISGRI constrains $|g_{a\gamma}g_{ap}| < 4.1\times10^{-21}$ ($6.9\times10^{-19}$) $\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$ for $m_a < 10^{-10} \mathrm{eV}$. For ALP decays, the $^{23}\mathrm{Na}$ ($^{55}\mathrm{Mn}$) line observed with INTEGRAL-ISGRI (NuSTAR) constrains $|g_{a\gamma}g_{ap}|$ down to $3.0\times10^{-22}$ ($2.9\times10^{-19}$) $\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$ at $m_a \simeq 400$ ($83$) keV, weakening in both cases as $m_a^{-3/2}$ for lower ALP masses, and as $m_a^{-2}$ for $^{23}$Na below $m_a \sim 1$ keV, with sensitivity extending to $m_a \simeq 440.2$ ($124$) keV. Although our $^{23}\mathrm{Na}$-based limits are the strongest for both channels, their larger transition energy makes them more sensitive to the precise evolutionary stage of Betelgeuse, whereas the $^{55}\mathrm{Mn}$-based limits are considerably more robust against such uncertainties.

</details>


### [53] [Nucleon tomography from a calibrated diquark spectator model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34602)
*Jakub Terlecki,Shohini Bhattacharya,Krzysztof Cichy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates twist-2 vector generalized parton distributions (GPDs) H^q and E^q in a spectator diquark model (scalar + axial-vector), deriving for the first time an analytic expression for the axial-vector contribution to the helicity-flip GPD E^q; it introduces a momentum-transfer-dependent quasi-hard cutoff, calibrates model parameters using proton-only and combined proton+neutron observables (EM moments, radii, gravitational form factors), and computes valence PDFs and impact-parameter distributions—revealing flavor-dependent transverse distortions under nucleon transverse polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified, phenomenologically constrained description of nucleon structure in both longitudinal-momentum (via PDFs/GPDs) and transverse-position (via impact-parameter distributions) space, particularly resolving the poorly constrained helicity-flip GPD E^q and its axial-vector diquark contribution.

Method: Spectator diquark model with scalar and axial-vector diquarks; analytic computation of twist-2 vector GPDs H^q and E^q; introduction of a momentum-transfer-dependent quasi-hard cutoff for transverse-momentum regularization; two-parameter calibrations—(1) proton-only and (2) proton+neutron—using electromagnetic moments, charge/magnetic radii, and gravitational form factor constraints; extraction of zero-skewness GPDs H^{u,d}(x,0,t), E^{u,d}(x,0,t), then valence PDFs and impact-parameter distributions.

Result: First explicit analytic expression for axial-vector-diquark contribution to E^q; compatible parameter sets from both calibrations, with neutron data reducing uncertainties and tightening flavor constraints; computed valence-quark PDFs consistent with data; impact-parameter distributions show central localization of high-x quarks and flavor-dependent Sivers-type distortion under transverse nucleon polarization.

Conclusion: The model delivers a self-consistent, unified description of valence-quark longitudinal-momentum and transverse-spatial structure in the nucleon, with improved constraints on flavor separation and transverse spin-orbit correlations via inclusion of neutron observables.

Abstract: Generalized parton distributions (GPDs) provide a unified description of the longitudinal-momentum and transverse spatial structure of the nucleon. We calculate the twist-2 vector GPDs $H^{q}$ and $E^{q}$ in a spectator model containing both scalar and axial-vector diquark configurations. A central new result is the explicit analytic expression for the axial-vector-diquark contribution to the helicity-flip GPD $E^{q}$.
