To regulate the transverse-momentum integrations while retaining a physically sensible dependence on the momentum transfer, we introduce a momentum-transfer-dependent quasi-hard cutoff. The model parameters are determined through two complementary calibrations: one using proton observables only and another simultaneously including proton and neutron observables. Electromagnetic moments and radii, together with selected constraints on gravitational form factors, determine the quark and diquark masses, the relative scalar and axial-vector contributions, and the transverse-momentum scale. The two calibrations yield compatible parameter ranges, while the inclusion of neutron observables reduces the parameter uncertainties and more strongly constrains the flavor structure. From the resulting zero-skewness GPDs $H^{u,d}(x,0,t)$ and $E^{u,d}(x,0,t)$, we calculate the valence-quark PDFs and the impact-parameter distributions of unpolarized up and down quarks in both unpolarized and transversely polarized nucleons. The transverse distributions exhibit the expected localization of large-$x$ quarks near the transverse center of momentum and a flavor-dependent distortion when the nucleon is transversely polarized. Our results provide a unified phenomenological description of the longitudinal-momentum and transverse-position structure of valence quarks in the nucleon.

</details>


### [54] [Two-Pion Exchange Paradigm in Hadron-Hadron Interactions from Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34655)
*Tetsuo Hatsuda*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the HAL QCD method in (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD near the physical pion mass to study hadron–hadron interactions, finding attractive nucleon–flavor-singlet hadron potentials (e.g., φ–N, J/ψ–N, η_c–N) and lattice evidence for two-pion exchange dominance in the long-range region where one-pion exchange is forbidden.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate nonperturbative hadron–hadron interactions—particularly universal long-distance phenomena like two-pion exchange—in QCD near the physical pion mass, where chiral dynamics are realistic.

Method: HAL QCD method applied to (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD simulations at nearly physical pion mass (m_π ≃ 146 MeV); extraction of energy-independent hadronic potentials from Nambu–Bethe–Salpeter wave functions.

Result: Attractive potentials observed for φ–N, J/ψ–N, and η_c–N across all distances; clear lattice signatures of two-pion exchange dominance in the long-range regime where one-pion exchange is absent.

Conclusion: The HAL QCD method successfully isolates universal long-range dynamics—specifically two-pion exchange—from first-principles QCD, validating its capability to probe nonperturbative, chiral-sensitive hadronic forces.

Abstract: We study hadron-hadron interactions in (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD near the physical pion mass ($m_\pi \simeq 146\mathrm{MeV}$) using the HAL QCD method, with particular emphasis on two-pion exchange (TPE). The nucleon--flavor-singlet hadron potentials such as $\phi$-$N$, $J/\psi$-$N$, and $\eta_c$-$N$ are found to be attractive at all distances. Moreover, in the long-range region where one-pion exchange is forbidden, we observe lattice signatures consistent with TPE dominance. These results demonstrate that the HAL QCD method can extract universal long-distance phenomena, such as TPE, directly from nonperturbative QCD dynamics.

</details>


### [55] [Fermion Mass and Mixing Patterns from Fusion Algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34720)
*Jacques Distler,Can Kilic,Sanjay Mathai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using noninvertible symmetries—specifically fusion algebras—to generate hierarchical fermion mass textures in the Standard Model; it identifies FR⁸,⁶₈ as the best-fitting rank-8 fusion algebra for Yukawa matrices, though none reproduce both fermion masses and CKM/PMNS mixing angles simultaneously, while ensuring RG stability of textures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the large hierarchy of fermion masses in the Standard Model via symmetry principles—specifically noninvertible (fusion) symmetries—that constrain Yukawa coupling textures without relying on ad hoc assumptions or specific UV models.

Method: Uses fusion algebras to assign field labels that suppress composite operators in the Lagrangian; interprets UV operator hierarchies as IR Yukawa textures; performs Monte Carlo scan over Wilson coefficients to fit observed up/down-quark and charged-lepton mass matrices; evaluates predictions for CKM and PMNS matrices; checks RG stability of resulting textures.

Result: The rank-8 fusion algebra FR⁸,⁶₈ yields the best-fit texture for all three Yukawa matrices; however, no studied fusion algebra simultaneously reproduces measured fermion masses and quark/neutrino mixing angles; all derived textures are stable under RG evolution to low energies.

Conclusion: Fusion algebras offer a novel, symmetry-based mechanism to generate and stabilize fermion mass hierarchies, but current constructions fall short of fully explaining flavor mixing; they represent a promising direction for UV-complete flavor model building beyond conventional global or gauge symmetries.

Abstract: The large hierarchy of fermion masses in the Standard Model has long posed a challenge to be explained. One approach is to try to invoke a symmetry principle to dictate the structure ("texture") of the matrices of fermion Yukawa couplings to the Higgs field. Here we invoke noninvertible symmetries, specifically, fusion algebras, to provide such textures. In particular, the structure of the fusion-algebra can be used to assign labels to each field, which determines how suppressed any given composite operator is as a term in the Lagrangian. In UV completions, this can be interpreted as specifying a hierarchy of operators that in the IR effective theory become the Yukawa couplings to the Higgs field. Remaining agnostic of a specific UV model, we perform a Monte Carlo numerical scan over unknown Wilson coefficients, and we identify the rank-8 fusion algebra $\rm{FR}^{8,6}_8$ as the one providing the best fit for the textures of the up-quark, down-quark and charged-lepton Yukawa matrices. We also study the predictions for the CKM and PMNS mixing matrices, with the conclusion that none of the fusion algebras we study provide an accurate fit for the masses and mixing angles simultaneously. The fusion algebra structure ensures that the textures that we find are stable under RG running and persist down to low energies.

</details>


### [56] ["Inevitable'' nonstrange tetraquark states and $ρρ$ interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34741)
*Yanyue Pan,Yuying Hao,Shirui Zhao,Hongxia Huang,Jialun Ping*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates ρρ interactions with quantum numbers IJ=00, 02, 11, 20, 22 using the quark delocalization color screening model, finding strong attraction and bound-state candidates—especially IJ=02 (binding >100 MeV)—suggesting a molecular tetraquark; similarities to ΔΔ interaction and analogy to the d* dibaryon support its experimental relevance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify viable nonstrange tetraquark candidates via systematic investigation of ρρ interactions across multiple isospin–spin channels, motivated by theoretical expectations of 'inevitable' exotic hadrons and analogies to known dibaryons like d*.

Method: Quark delocalization color screening model (QDCSM), a microscopic quark-model framework incorporating interquark dynamics, cluster structure, and color screening to compute ρρ potentials, binding energies, and intercluster distances.

Result: Strong attractive ρρ potentials leading to bound states for all studied channels; largest binding energies (>100 MeV) for IJ = 00 and 02; smallest (~few MeV) for IJ = 20; intercluster distances consistent with molecular (loosely bound) structure; ρρ and ΔΔ interactions exhibit qualitative similarities.

Conclusion: The IJ = 02 ρρ state is the most promising 'inevitable' nonstrange tetraquark candidate due to its large binding energy, molecular character, and analogy to the experimentally confirmed d* dibaryon—warranting dedicated experimental searches.

Abstract: Investigation of the interaction between two $\rho$ mesons with $IJ=00, 02, 11, 20$ and $22$ is conducted systematically in the framework of the quark delocalization color screening model. The results show that there is a strong attractive potential between two $\rho$ mesons, which makes it easier for these two mesons to form bound states. The largest binding energies occur for $IJ=02$ and $IJ=00$ with values over a hundred MeV, while the $IJ=20$ state is the least bound and has a binding energy of only a few MeV. Moreover, the results of intercluster distances favor a molecular interpretation of these states. The interaction analysis reveals that the $\rho\rho$ and $\Delta\Delta$ interactions share certain similarities. Drawing inspiration from the $d^*$ dibaryon prediction and from decay-property analyses, we suggest that the $\rho\rho$ with $IJ=02$ may be the most promising candidate of the ``inevitable'' nonstrange tetraquark state, which deserves experimental search.

</details>


### [57] [The Pion Parton Distribution Puzzle in the High x Region](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34798)
*Misak Sargsian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a spectral function-based theoretical approach to describe the partonic structure of hadrons, applying it to calculate the pion's valence-parton distribution function (PDF); results show that the pion’s x-weighted PDF peak and height constrain its analytic behavior near x→1, supporting a highly virtual qq̄ cluster dominated by the Feynman mechanism with minimal residual mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend a previously developed spectral function approach—successfully applied to nucleon PDFs and revealing pion-cloud effects—to the pion itself, aiming to understand its soft partonic structure and explain empirical features of its valence PDF, especially the (1−x) behavior at x→1.

Method: Uses spectral function representation for valence quarks in a mean-field approximation; models the pion as a valence q̄q cluster embedded in a residual field; employs phenomenological light-front wave functions for both cluster and residual field; fits model parameters (qq̄ virtuality and residual mass) to empirical pion xPDF data at initial scale Q₀.

Result: Fits indicate very small residual mass and high virtuality of the qq̄ cluster, implying dominance of the Feynman mechanism; successfully reproduces the observed ∼(1−x) scaling of the valence xPDF as x→1; establishes that the height and peak position of xPDF uniquely determine the analytic form of the valence PDF near x=1.

Conclusion: The spectral function approach provides a consistent, parameter-light description of pion valence structure, revealing that its PDF is governed by soft, highly asymmetric qq̄ dynamics with negligible hard contributions—contrasting nucleon structure—and that low-x′ global features encode essential high-x asymptotics.

Abstract: We developed a new theoretical approach in the description of partonic structure of hadrons based on spectral function representation of valence quarks. In this approach massless valence quarks are considered in the mean field of residual hadron. This approach initially was applied to the calculation of parton distribution function~(PDF) of nucleons that resulted in an observation of pion-cloud structure of nucleon. In this report we present our calculation of pion PDF in the valence region within the same approach. In this approach we describe the soft partonic structure of pion through the valence $q\bar q$ cluster, embedded in the residual field of the pion. The pion PDF is calculated using phenomenological Light-Front wave functions for the $q\bar q$ cluster and the residual field. Our result indicates that the peak position of the x weighted valence PDF~(xPDF) depends on parameters characterizing the virtuality of the $q\bar q$ cluster and the mass of the residual system. These parameters are evaluated by fitting the calculated PDF to the height and the peak position of empirical pion xPDF at starting $Q_0$. They indicate that unlike the nucleon case, very little residual mass is needed in the pion to describe existing pion PDFs in the valence region. They also require the $q\bar q$-cluster to be highly virtual with the interacting quark carrying almost all of the momentum of the $q\bar q$ cluster. This picture is consistent with the dominance of the Feynman mechanism leaving little room for the hard component in the PDF. The calculation practically describes the $\sim (1-x)$ behavior observed recently at $x\to 1$ limit. Our non-trivial observation is that the height and the peak position of xPDF define the analytic behavior of valence PDF at $x\to 1$ limit.

</details>


### [58] [Analysis of $S$-wave $ωπ$ dynamics behavior in multi-body $B_{\ell4}$ and $B_{\ell6}$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34840)
*Yan-Ting Yang,Hai-Bing Fu,Xue Zheng,Jian-Qi Chen,Sheng-Bo Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates transition form factors for B→b₁(1235)ℓν using QCD light-cone sum rules with a transverse twist-2 LCDA modeled via light-cone harmonic oscillator; it finds the b₁(1235)’s finite width reduces the Bℓ4 branching ratio by ~2/3 compared to treating it as stable, and predicts the six-body Bℓ6 decay including ω→3π.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve longstanding systematic deviations caused by neglecting the sizable decay width of the b₁(1235) resonance in theoretical predictions, and to probe its internal structure via multi-body semileptonic B decays.

Method: QCD light-cone sum rules with a right-handed chiral current correlation function; transverse twist-2 LCDA of b₁(1235) constructed using the light-cone harmonic oscillator model; inclusion of b₁→ωπ resonant line shape and ω→π⁺π⁻π⁰ decay for full Bℓ4 and Bℓ6 observables.

Result: TFFs V₀,₁,₂(0) and A(0) computed at q²=0; TFF ratios r_V = 1.530₋₀.₀₁₆⁺⁰.₀₁₃ and r₂ = 0.553₋₀.₀₁₅⁺⁰.₀₁₂; Bℓ4 branching ratio reduced to ~1/3 of the narrow-resonance approximation; predicted Bℓ6 branching ratio incorporating full decay chain.

Conclusion: Finite-width effects of b₁(1235) are crucial—neglecting them leads to large overestimation of Bℓ4 rates; the calculated TFFs and branching ratios provide benchmark predictions for experimental tests (e.g., Belle II) and structural studies of axial-vector mesons.

Abstract: The $b_1(1235)$ state has a sizable decay width, which have longstanding puzzle in treating as a final state in theoretical calculations may lead to systematic deviations in the predicted observables. To address this issue and further explore the internal structure of this resonance, we study the multi-body semileptonic decays $B_{\ell4}$ and $B_{\ell6}$. Within the approximation where the $b_1(1235)$ resonance dominates, we study the $B_{\ell4}$ decay $B\to b_1(1235)(\to\omega\pi)\ell^+\nu_\ell$ in the QCD light-cone sum rules framework. The corresponding transition form factors (TFFs) are calculated by constructing a correlation function with a right-handed chiral current. As the main nonperturbative input for the TFFs calculation, the transverse twist-2 light-cone distribution amplitude (LCDA) of the $b_1(1235)$ resonance is constructed using the light-cone harmonic oscillator model, and the resulting LCDA exhibits a single-peak behavior. At the large-recoil region, we obtain the TFFs $V_{0, 1, 2}(0)$ and $A(0)$, together with the corresponding TFFs ratios $r_V = 1.530_{-0.016}^{+0.013}$ and $r_2 = 0.553_{-0.015}^{+0.012}$. We then further study the complete $B_{\ell4}$ decay by incorporating the $b_1(1235)\to\omega\pi$ resonant line shape and obtain the corresponding observables. The results show that the branching ratio of the $B_{\ell4}$ decay is about $1/3$ of that obtained when $b_1(1235)$ is treated as a final state. We also account for the subsequent decay $\omega \to \pi^+\pi^-\pi^0$ and further predicted the branching ratio of the $B_{\ell6}$ decay $B\to b_1(1235)(\to\omega(\to \pi^+\pi^-\pi^0)\pi)\ell^+\nu_\ell$. These results may provide useful references for future experimental measurements and further theoretical studies.

</details>


### [59] [Light top-flavoured dark matter in dark SMEFT: one-loop matching and phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34880)
*Meng-Chao Gao,Xin-Qiang Li,Ya-Dong Yang,Xing-Bo Yuan,Xin Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified effective field theory framework (DSMEFT → DLEFT → non-relativistic EFT) to analyze light top-flavoured dark matter (< EW scale) coupling preferentially to third-generation quarks, and confronts it with Belle II's B→Kνν excess, rare meson decays, direct/indirect detection, and thermal relic constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the Belle II excess in B⁺→K⁺νν̄ as DM production while maintaining consistency with complementary probes (rare decays, direct/indirect detection, relic density), using a theoretically consistent EFT chain tailored for light, flavour-specific DM.

Method: One-loop matching from DSMEFT to DLEFT at the EW scale for all operators coupling scalar/fermionic/vector DM or ALPs exclusively to third-generation quarks; QCD evolution down to low scales; subsequent matching to non-relativistic DM-nucleus scattering EFT; comprehensive phenomenological analysis across collider, direct, indirect, and cosmological observables.

Result: Only tree-level DSMEFT operators can explain the Belle II excess with O(1) couplings at Λ = 1 TeV; B⁰→K*⁰+inv is the dominant constraint over much of the favoured parameter space; low-mass region constrained by K⁺→π⁺+inv, high-mass by Bₛ→inv, B⁺→π⁺+inv, and Υ(1S)→inv; CRESST-III/DarkSide-50 exclude parts of the favoured band for m_DM ≳ 1–2 GeV for scalar-current operators; relic abundance and CMB/Fermi-LAT indirect limits are also quantified.

Conclusion: A consistent, flavour-focused EFT framework tightly constrains light top-flavoured DM as an explanation of the B→Kνν̄ anomaly, with strong complementarity among observables—no single operator evades all bounds, and viable parameter space is highly restricted.

Abstract: We study light top-flavoured dark matter (DM) below the electroweak (EW) scale within the dark Standard Model effective field theory (DSMEFT). We perform the one-loop matching of the DSMEFT onto the dark low-energy effective field theory (DLEFT) at the EW scale for all relevant operators that couple scalar, fermionic or vector DM, as well as axion-like particles, exclusively to third-generation quarks. After QCD evolution to the relevant low-energy scales, we match the DLEFT onto the non-relativistic EFT for DM-nucleus scattering. This EFT chain provides a unified framework for phenomenological studies of rare $B$- and $K$-meson decays with missing energy, direct and indirect detection, and the thermal relic abundance. Interpreting the Belle II excess in $B^+\to K^+\nu\bar\nu$ as a decay into DM, we find that only the DSMEFT operators contributing at tree level can accommodate it with $O(1)$ couplings for a new-physics scale of $1\,\mathrm{TeV}$. The decay $B^0\to K^{*0}+\mathrm{inv}$ provides the strongest constraint over large portions of the Belle II-favoured parameter space, while $K^+\to\pi^++\mathrm{inv}$ can dominate at low and $B_s\to\mathrm{inv}$, $B^+\to\pi^++\mathrm{inv}$ and $\Upsilon(1S)\to\mathrm{inv}$ become important at high DM masses, respectively. Direct-detection searches by CRESST-III and DarkSide-50 are competitive for operators involving scalar quark currents and, depending on the operator, exclude the Belle~II-favoured band for $m_{\mathrm{DM}}\gtrsim1$-$2\,\mathrm{GeV}$. We also perform detailed analyses of the thermal relic abundance and the indirect-detection constraints from the CMB and Fermi-LAT.

</details>


### [60] [Explaining the universal Hagedorn temperature in string models of hadrons with fixed endpoints or baryon junctions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34882)
*Yuki Fujimoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives mass formulas and asymptotic density-of-states expressions for string models of hadrons with one fixed heavy endpoint or a baryon junction; it shows that all such models share the same Hagedorn temperature as ordinary open strings—explaining its phenomenological universality—and that the baryon-junction and quark-diquark pictures yield identical asymptotic density scaling (up to normalization), though differ in Regge slope predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a microscopic explanation for the recently observed phenomenological universality of the Hagedorn temperature across different hadronic systems, and to compare competing string-based baryon models (baryon-junction vs. quark-diquark) via their thermodynamic and spectral predictions.

Method: Analytical derivation of mass spectra and asymptotic densities of states for relativistic string models: (i) open strings with one massive (fixed) endpoint (heavy-light mesons), (ii) Y-shaped strings with a baryon junction, and (iii) effective quark-diquark strings; use of saddle-point and Laplace-method asymptotics to extract Hagedorn temperatures and leading-order density behavior.

Result: All three string configurations—fixed-endpoint mesons, baryon-junction baryons, and quark-diquark baryons—exhibit identical Hagedorn temperatures (T_H = 1/(2π√α′)) as standard open strings, independent of endpoint mass or topology; their densities of states scale asymptotically as ρ(M) ∼ M^a exp(M/T_H), with identical exponent a but differing overall normalizations; baryon-junction model predicts steeper Regge slopes than observed, while quark-diquark aligns better.

Conclusion: The Hagedorn temperature is topologically and dynamically robust across diverse stringy hadron models, rooted in universal UV string tension rather than specific boundary conditions or junction dynamics; thus its universality is explained microscopically. However, Regge trajectory data favor the quark-diquark over the baryon-junction picture for baryons.

Abstract: We derive the mass formulas and more accurate asymptotic expressions for the densities of states for string models of hadrons with one fixed heavy endpoint or a baryon junction. We show that strings with one fixed endpoint have the same Hagedorn temperature as ordinary open strings at the same tension and number of polarizations, despite the different Regge slopes of heavy-light and light mesons. This provides a microscopic explanation for the universality of the Hagedorn temperature recently found phenomenologically. We also find that the baryon-junction and quark-diquark pictures share this Hagedorn temperature and yield the same asymptotic mass dependence of the density of states, differing only by an overall normalization. The observed baryon mass spectrum provides little discrimination between the two pictures, although the baryon-junction model has a larger discrepancy with the observed Regge slopes.

</details>


### [61] [Exact flavor conservation at the quark level in causal clusters: strange particles, protons and light nuclei in small collision systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34947)
*Paolo Castorina,Helmut Satz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends a strangeness conservation model to light flavors (up/down quarks) in pp and p--Pb collisions, using exact flavor conservation in causally connected clusters; with strangeness fixed from prior work, a single parameter — the light-to-strange quark abundance ratio r = 3.76 ± 0.38 — successfully describes yields of protons, deuterons, helium-4, and hypertriton across multiplicities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain observed suppression patterns of light-flavor hadrons and light nuclei in small collision systems (pp, p--Pb) beyond strangeness-only models, by incorporating exact up/down quark number conservation in hadronization clusters.

Method: Extends the strangeness-exact conservation framework to include exact conservation of up and down quark numbers within the same causally connected cluster; treats baryon/nucleus suppression as determined solely by their light quark content; fits data using one free parameter r (light-to-strange quark abundance ratio), while fixing strangeness sector from prior analysis and accounting for finite nuclear size.

Result: Achieves excellent agreement with experimental multiplicities of protons, deuterons, He-4, and hypertriton in pp and p--Pb collisions; extracts r = 3.76 ± 0.38, implying strange quarks are ~0.27 times as abundant as up or down quarks individually — consistent with string fragmentation expectations.

Conclusion: Light-flavor hadron and light-nuclei production in small systems is governed by exact flavor conservation at the quark level within localized clusters, unifying suppression mechanisms across strangeness and light sectors; the success of a single-parameter fit supports the physical relevance of the cluster-based, flavor-conserving hadronization picture.

Abstract: The suppression of strange hadrons in pp and p--Pb collisions at the LHC, including the $\phi$ meson, has recently been described by the exact conservation of strangeness imposed on the quarks of a causally connected cluster before hadronization. Here we extend this approach to the light flavors. The numbers of up and down quarks and antiquarks are conserved in the same cluster, and the suppression of a baryon or a nucleus depends only on how many light quarks of each flavor it contains. With the strangeness sector fixed by the previous analysis, a single constant, the ratio $r$ of light to strange quark abundances, determines the multiplicity dependence of protons, deuterons, He and hypertriton in pp and p--Pb collisions. Including the finite size of the nuclei, one obtains a very good fit of the data for $r=3.76\pm0.38$, i.e.\ strange quarks about 0.27 times as abundant as light quarks per flavor, close to the value known from string fragmentation

</details>


### [62] [Dilepton azimuthal anisotropy from coherent $J/ψ$ photoproduction in hadronic Pb-Pb collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35149)
*Xueli Hua,Xin Wu,Shuai Yang,Wangmei Zha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using coherent J/ψ photoproduction as a geometry reference to mitigate nonflow and autocorrelation biases in hadronic event-plane estimators in Pb–Pb collisions at 5.36 TeV, calculating the dilepton anisotropy coefficient A₂ relative to the reaction plane within an equivalent-photon + Glauber + vector-meson-dominance framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hadron-based event-plane estimators suffer from nonflow and autocorrelation, especially at low multiplicity; coherent J/ψ photoproduction provides a clean, geometry-driven reference via photon polarization–reaction plane correlation.

Method: Computes second-order dilepton coefficient A₂ relative to the reaction plane using an equivalent-photon approximation combined with generalized vector-meson dominance and a nuclear Glauber model; includes modeling of nuclear diffraction and two-source interference; compares two treatments of coherent scattering in the nuclear overlap region.

Result: A₂ exhibits strong transverse-momentum dependence but weak rapidity dependence; suppressing coherent contribution from the nuclear overlap region reduces total yield but enhances integrated A₂ (i.e., stronger polarization–reaction-plane correlation), most significantly in central/semicentral collisions; joint A₂ and coherent yield measurements constrain coherence loss due to hadronic interactions.

Conclusion: Coherent J/ψ photoproduction offers a robust, complementary reaction-plane reference for flow studies; azimuthal correlations of decay leptons can extend collectivity investigations to small systems, provided sufficient tagged yields.

Abstract: Hadron-based event-plane estimators can be affected by nonflow and autocorrelation biases, especially at low multiplicity. Coherent $J/\psi$ photoproduction offers a complementary geometry reference through the correlation between quasireal-photon polarization and the reaction plane. We calculate the second-order dilepton coefficient $A_2$ relative to the reaction plane in hadronic Pb--Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=5.36$ TeV within an equivalent-photon and generalized vector-meson-dominance Glauber framework. Nuclear diffraction and two-source interference produce a pronounced transverse-momentum dependence, while the rapidity dependence is weak. We also compare two treatments of coherent scattering in the overlap region of the two colliding nuclei. Suppressing the contribution from this region reduces the yield and strengthens the integrated polarization--reaction-plane correlation, with the largest differences in central and semicentral collisions and smaller differences toward peripheral collisions. Joint measurements of the coherent yield and $A_2$ can constrain how hadronic interactions disrupt nuclear coherence and clarify the photoproduction mechanism. With sufficient tagged yields, azimuthal correlations involving decay leptons from photoproduced $J/\psi$ mesons could complement studies of collectivity, with potential future applications to small systems.

</details>


### [63] [Probing the Collins effect at photon-photon colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35285)
*Simone Anedda,Carlo Flore,Francesco Murgia,Cristian Pisano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes studying the Collins effect via azimuthal distributions of back-to-back unpolarized hadrons in γγ → h₁h₂X, with estimates showing measurable asymmetries (10–15%) at FCC and LHC ultra-peripheral collisions, enabling tests of quark fragmentation models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the Collins effect — a spin-dependent hadronization mechanism — in an unpolarized γγ collision environment, where access to transversity and Collins functions is otherwise challenging; and to discriminate among competing theoretical models of quark fragmentation.

Method: Analytical calculation of azimuthal asymmetries in the production of almost back-to-back unpolarized hadrons (mainly π±) around the jet thrust-axis in γγ → h₁h₂X; includes kinematic modeling for quasi-real photons from lepton–lepton or heavy-ion ultra-peripheral collisions; numerical estimation using realistic FCC and LHC UPC configurations.

Result: Predicted Collins-type asymmetries reach 10–15% in accessible kinematic regions at FCC and LHC UPC setups, significantly above experimental sensitivity thresholds.

Conclusion: The proposed observable provides a clean, unpolarized-channel probe of the Collins function and transverse-momentum-dependent fragmentation, offering model-discriminatory power for TMD QCD studies.

Abstract: We propose to study the Collins effect in the production of almost back-to-back unpolarized hadrons (mainly charged pions) by looking at their azimuthal distributions around the jet thrust-axis in the process $\gamma\gamma \to h_1h_2X$. The quasi-real photons in the initial state can be emitted either by two scattering leptons or two charged heavy nuclei in ultra-peripheral collisions. We provide numerical estimates for kinematic configurations in principle accessible at the Future Circular Collider and at the planned experiments on ultra-peripheral collisions at the LHC. We find that the corresponding asymmetries can be sizable, even up to around 10-15%. The proposed measurements would thus shed light on the fragmentation mechanism of quarks into hadrons and, in principle, would allow one to distinguish among different existing models for the Collins functions.

</details>


### [64] [Revisiting supernova constraints on dark gauge bosons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35379)
*Zun Wang,Yongchao Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits supernova constraints on three light gauge bosons—dark photon, U(1)_{B-L}, and U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ}—using improved nucleon bremsstrahlung calculations (one-pion exchange with full momentum/mass dependence) and realistic geometrical treatment of decay/absorption in the supernova core; it derives updated limits from SN1987A cooling and low-energy supernovae (LESNe), yielding tighter mass and coupling bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve upon previous supernova bounds by incorporating more accurate nuclear physics (full momentum/mass dependence in nucleon bremsstrahlung) and realistic geometrical effects (decay and absorption within the core), thereby obtaining more robust and stringent constraints on light gauge bosons relevant to dark sector models.

Method: Re-evaluation of nucleon bremsstrahlung production using one-pion exchange approximation with full momentum and mass dependence; inclusion of geometrical effects for in-medium decay and absorption; application of SN1987A neutrino-cooling constraint and LESNe energy-deposition constraint using the SFHo-18.8 18.8 M⊙ progenitor profile.

Result: SN1987A cooling excludes masses up to ~230 MeV (dark photon), ~280 MeV (U(1)_{B-L}), and ~300 MeV (U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ}); LESNe constraints extend to ~450 MeV, ~440 MeV, and ~600 MeV respectively; coupling limits: ε < 5.8×10⁻¹² (dark photon), g_BL < 2.9×10⁻¹⁶ (U(1)_{B-L}), g_μτ < 6.4×10⁻¹² (U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ}).

Conclusion: The refined analysis yields significantly improved and more reliable bounds on light gauge bosons, especially for U(1)_{B-L} and U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ}, demonstrating the importance of consistent nuclear physics and geometric modeling in supernova-based particle constraints.

Abstract: We revisit the supernova constraints on three well-motivated light gauge bosons: the dark photon, the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson, and the $U(1)_{L_\mu-L_\tau}$ gauge boson. For the dark photon and the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson, we re-evaluate the nucleon bremsstrahlung process within the one-pion exchange approximation, retaining the full momentum and mass dependence. The geometrical dependence of the decay and absorption of these gauge bosons inside the supernova core is also taken into account. Updated bounds on these models are derived based on the cooling of SN1987A and energy deposition constraints from low-energy supernovae (LESNe). Taking the SFHo-18.8 $18.8\,M_\odot$ profile, the SN1987A cooling constraints reach masses of about $230$ MeV, $280$ MeV, and $300$ MeV for the dark photon, the $U(1)_{B-L}$ boson, and the $U(1)_{L_\mu-L_\tau}$ boson, while the corresponding LESN limits extend to about $450$ MeV, $440$ MeV, and $600$ MeV, respectively. The kinetic mixing parameter $\epsilon$ for dark photon, the gauge coupling $g_{BL}$ in the $U(1)_{B-L}$ model, and the gauge coupling $g_{\mu\tau}$ for the $U(1)_{L_\mu-L_\tau}$ model are constrained down to $5.8\times10^{-12}$, $2.9\times10^{-16}$, and $6.4\times10^{-12}$, respectively.

</details>


### [65] [Probing the singlet-triplet scotogenic model with lepton flavor violation, gravitational waves and dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35444)
*Shi-Yu Li,Jun-Nan Lu,Yang Zhang,Gui-Jun Ding*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the singlet-triplet scotogenic model, linking neutrino masses, lepton-flavor violation (LFV), first-order electroweak phase transition (FOEWPT), gravitational-wave (GW) signals, and scalar dark matter. It derives general one-loop Wilson coefficients for LFV processes, computes observables like μ→eγ and μ–e conversion, identifies λ₅ as a key coupling connecting neutrino mass, LFV, and DM detection, studies finite-temperature effective potential and GW spectra from FOEWPT, and finds a benchmark point satisfying current experimental constraints while yielding detectable GW signals at LISA, BBO, and U-DECIGO.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify explanations of neutrino mass generation, dark matter, baryogenesis (via FOEWPT), and LFV within a single, testable extension of the Standard Model—the singlet–triplet scotogenic model—while identifying interconnections among its parameters and experimental signatures.

Method: Analytical computation of one-loop penguin and box Wilson coefficients for LFV processes with generic scalar/fermion mediators; numerical evaluation of LFV branching ratios and coherent μ–e conversion; finite-temperature effective potential analysis to determine conditions for a strong first-order electroweak phase transition; calculation of resulting gravitational-wave spectra (peak frequency, amplitude); parameter-space scan constrained by current LFV (MEG, SINDRUM), DM direct detection (XENONnT), and theoretical bounds (vacuum stability, perturbativity).

Result: λ₅ governs correlations among neutrino mass scale, LFV rates, and DM-nucleon scattering; λ₁^Ω and μ₁ control FOEWPT strength and GW signal amplitude; viable parameter space exists where all constraints are simultaneously satisfied; a benchmark point yields h²Ω_GW ~ 10⁻¹² at f ~ 1–10 mHz, detectable by LISA/BBO/U-DECIGO.

Conclusion: The singlet–triplet scotogenic model provides a coherent, multi-messenger framework where neutrino physics, LFV, scalar dark matter, and cosmological phase transitions are interlinked; future LFV, DM, and GW experiments can jointly probe and constrain its parameter space, offering a path toward falsifiability.

Abstract: We perform a comprehensive phenomenological analysis of neutrino masses, lepton-flavor violation, gravitational-wave signature induced by the first-order electroweak phase transition, and scalar dark matter within the framework of the singlet--triplet scotogenic model. We derive general analytical expressions for the one-loop Wilson coefficients of the penguin and box contributions to lepton-flavor-violating processes, considering generic scalar and fermion mediators. These general results are then applied to the singlet--triplet scotogenic model to calculate the branching ratios of $\mu\to e\gamma$ and $\mu\to e\overline{e}e$, as well as the coherent $\mu$--$e$ conversion rate. We systematically investigate the lepton-flavor-violation phenomenology and the constraints from current and future experiments. It is found that the coupling $\lambda_5$ plays a key role in connecting neutrino masses, lepton flavor violation, and dark matter direct detection. We also study the finite-temperature effective potential and the gravitational-wave signatures of the resulting first-order phase transition. By analyzing the thermal history of the model, we identify the viable parameter space allowing for a first-order electroweak phase transition in the early Universe and find that the portal parameters $\lambda_1^\Omega$ and $\mu_1$ play a crucial role in determining the transition strength. The corresponding stochastic gravitational-wave spectra can be probed by future gravitational-wave observatories. As a representative example, we identify a benchmark point that simultaneously satisfies the current constraints from lepton-flavor-violation and dark-matter experiments and generates a stochastic gravitational-wave spectrum that may be probed by the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna, the Big Bang Observer, and the Ultimate Deci-hertz Interferometer Gravitational-wave Observatory.

</details>


### [66] [Midrapidity dip and limiting fragmentation of charged hadrons from RHIC to LHC energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35456)
*Neeraj,Md. Kaosar Ali Mondal,Amal Sarkar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the observed midrapidity depression in charged-hadron pseudorapidity density (dN/dη) in central heavy-ion collisions reflects true structure in rapidity density (dN/dy) or is merely an artifact of the y→η Jacobian transformation. Using a data-driven y→η kernel from measured identified-hadron spectra — avoiding event generators — the authors test the null hypothesis of no dip in dN/dy. They find no statistically significant evidence for a dip in dN/dy across RHIC and LHC energies; p-values remain ≥ pMinAll. Upper limits on dip depth (at 95% CL) are quantified. Energy sum rule constraints, calibrated on RHIC, yield LHC/RHIC energy ratios consistent with unity within uncertainties — neither confirming nor excluding limiting fragmentation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the experimentally observed midrapidity depression in dN/dη is physical (i.e., a real dip in dN/dy) or kinematic (arising solely from the y→η mapping), without relying on model-dependent Jacobians or event generators.

Method: Constructs an empirical y→η transformation kernel directly from measured identified-hadron transverse momentum spectra; applies it to test the null hypothesis of a flat (dip-free) dN/dy using statistical inference (p-values from pseudo-experiments); performs generator closure tests; imposes an energy sum rule calibrated on RHIC data to constrain dN/dy shape at LHC; assesses systematic uncertainties from RHIC covariance and kernel variations.

Result: No statistically significant dip in dN/dy is required: p ≥ pMinAll across all energies (19.6 GeV–5.36 TeV). For ≤1-unit-wide dips, 95% CL upper limits on depth are quantified (e.g., dminLHClo–dminLHChi % at LHC). Energy sum rule fits give LHC/RHIC dN/dy integrals consistent with unity within 95% intervals — though mild tensions (~pullLoEF–pullHiEF σ) appear at 2.76/5.02 TeV. Systematic variation yields ratio bounds sSystAllLo–sSystAllHi; unity is excluded only at systUnionAbs confidence level.

Conclusion: The midrapidity depression in dN/dη does not necessitate a corresponding dip in dN/dy; limiting fragmentation remains neither established nor excluded by current data.

Abstract: The charged-hadron pseudorapidity density $\dndeta$ in central heavy-ion collisions has a midrapidity depression that deepens with collision energy. Whether it reflects structure in the rapidity density $\dndy$ or only the kinematics of the $y\to\eta$ transformation has mostly been argued with effective or model-based Jacobians. We test this without an event generator, with a $y\to\eta$ kernel built from measured identified-hadron spectra. In central Au+Au and Pb+Pb collisions from $\snn=19.6$~GeV to 5.36~TeV the data do not require a dip in $\dndy$: the decisive null-hypothesis family gives $p\ge\pMinAll$ in all variants. For dips up to one rapidity unit wide, the depth giving $p<0.05$ in half the pseudo-experiments is \rng{\dminLHClo}{\dminLHChi}\% at the LHC and \rng{\dminRHIClo}{\dminRHIChi}\% at 62.4--200~GeV. Generator closure tests show a false dip at top RHIC energy caused by the forward region. As the LHC data barely constrain the fragmentation region, an energy sum rule calibrated on RHIC data, with the RHIC shape of that region, gives best-fit LHC/RHIC ratios of \rng{\sLoE}{\sHiE}, \rng{\sLoF}{\sHiF} and \rng{\sLoG}{\sHiG} at 2.76, 5.02 and 5.36~TeV for three shape families, with 95\% intervals over the families of \rng{\sNFLoE}{\sNFHiE}, \rng{\sNFLoF}{\sNFHiF} and \sNFG{} that include unity, although at 2.76 and 5.02~TeV the fits prefer \rng{\pullLoEF}{\pullHiEF}$\,\sigma$ more energy than calibrated. Over plausible RHIC covariances and kernels the best-fit ratios span \rng{\sSystAllLo}{\sSystAllHi}, and single variants exclude unity at \systUnionAbs: limiting fragmentation is neither established nor excluded.

</details>


### [67] [Neutrino Phenomenology and Leptogenesis from Non-Invertible Fusion Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35587)
*Pulakesh Borah,Mrinal Kumar Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal type-I seesaw model with two right-handed neutrinos, governed by a non-invertible Z₂-gauged Z₅ fusion symmetry (Z⁵_IN), yielding predictive neutrino mass textures without flavons; it favors inverted ordering (m₃ = 0), predicts near-maximal δ_CP and a testable 0νββ effective mass, and successfully realizes thermal leptogenesis consistent with the observed baryon asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a minimal, predictive neutrino mass model that avoids ad hoc flavon fields while linking neutrino phenomenology (mass ordering, CP violation, 0νββ) and baryogenesis via leptogenesis, using novel non-invertible fusion symmetries.

Method: Built a two-right-handed-neutrino type-I seesaw model incorporating a second Higgs doublet and auxiliary Z₂ parity, constrained by a non-invertible Z₂-gauged Z₅ fusion symmetry (Z⁵_IN); performed global χ² fit to neutrino oscillation data; computed effective Majorana mass m_ββ; analyzed thermal leptogenesis via Boltzmann equations including flavor effects and CP asymmetries from RH neutrino decays.

Result: Best-fit solution corresponds to inverted neutrino mass ordering with m₃ = 0; Dirac CP phase δ_CP ≈ 270° (nearly maximal); predicted m_ββ ≈ 15–25 meV (testable in next-generation 0νββ experiments); successful thermal leptogenesis reproducing observed baryon-to-photon ratio η_B ≈ 6.1 × 10⁻¹⁰.

Conclusion: Non-invertible fusion symmetries provide a theoretically motivated, minimally extended framework capable of simultaneously explaining neutrino masses/mixings, CP violation, 0νββ observables, and the baryon asymmetry—establishing a robust link between low-energy neutrino physics and high-scale leptogenesis.

Abstract: We constructed a minimal type-I seesaw model with two right-handed neutrinos, in which the flavor structure is determined by a non-invertible \(\mathbb{Z}_2\)-gauged \(\mathbb{Z}_5\) fusion symmetry~($\mathbb{Z}^{IN}_5$). The framework, incorporating a second Higgs doublet and an auxiliary \(\mathbb{Z}_2\) parity, yields predictive neutrino mass textures without introducing flavon fields. A fit to neutrino data selects the inverted mass ordering with \(m_3=0\) and predicts a nearly maximal Dirac CP phase, together with a characteristic prediction for the effective Majorana mass governing neutrinoless double-beta decay($0\nu\beta\beta$). We further investigate thermal leptogenesis and show that the framework can reproduce the observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe. Our results establish a connection between predictive neutrino phenomenology and leptogenesis within a minimal framework based on non-invertible fusion symmetry.

</details>


### [68] [Threshold effects of inelastic neutrino-dark-matter scattering in AGN spikes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35620)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Van Que Tran*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates inelastic neutrino-dark matter scattering in active galactic nuclei (AGN), showing that it produces a distinctive spectral distortion—deficit above and pile-up below a threshold energy E_th—due to irrecoverable energy loss. Using IceCube data from NGC 1068 and other AGN, it sets competitive upper limits on the optical depth τ₀ for both narrow and broad energy-transfer kernels, and demonstrates that vector-mediator models with adiabatic DM spikes can yield constraints rivaling lab experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe dark matter (DM) spikes around supermassive black holes using high-energy astrophysical neutrinos, particularly by exploiting inelastic scattering νχ₁ → νχ₂—which introduces an irreducible energy threshold E_th absent in elastic scattering—and thus breaks the attenuation-regeneration degeneracy that obscures DM signatures.

Method: Solved the full neutrino transport cascade including repeated inelastic scattering, secondary neutrinos from χ₂ decay, and realistic energy-transfer kernels (narrow: ε = 0.01; broad: ε = 0.36); performed unbinned likelihood analysis on IceCube public data (NGC 1068, CGCG 420-015, TXS 0506+056); marginalized over intrinsic power-law spectral index and normalization; assessed impact of intrinsic cutoffs and DM velocity dispersion.

Result: Observed a robust spectral feature: deficit above E_th and excess below it; derived 90% CL upper limits: τ₀ < 26–39 (narrow kernel, E_th = 0.24–3.0 TeV) and τ₀ < 20–26 (broad kernel, E_th = 0.24–2.4 TeV); intrinsic cutoffs weaken limits by factors 2.0–7.4; combined multi-source analysis extends E_th reach; vector-mediator model with adiabatic spike yields coupling constraints competitive with laboratory bounds for broad kernels.

Conclusion: Inelastic neutrino-DM scattering generates a unique, observable spectral imprint in AGN neutrino spectra, enabling stringent, astrophysically grounded constraints on DM interactions—particularly for broad energy-transfer scenarios—complementing and in some cases surpassing terrestrial experimental sensitivity.

Abstract: High-energy neutrinos from active galactic nuclei provide a powerful probe of dark matter (DM) spikes surrounding supermassive black holes. While elastic neutrino-DM scattering mediated by a light particle produces little net attenuation due to regeneration, inelastic scattering, $\nu\chi_1\to\nu\chi_2$, introduces a nonzero minimum energy loss, $E_{\rm th}$. We show that this threshold prevents the near-cancellation between attenuation and regeneration, producing a distinctive spectral deficit above $E_{\rm th}$ accompanied by a pile-up below it. Solving the full neutrino cascade, including repeated scattering and secondary neutrinos from excited-state decays, we demonstrate that this feature persists for both narrow and broad energy-transfer kernels. Using public IceCube data for NGC 1068 and an unbinned likelihood analysis with a freely fitted intrinsic power-law spectrum, we derive upper limits on the asymptotic optical depth of $\tau_0<26-39$ for a narrow kernel (kernel width $\epsilon=0.01$) over $E_{\rm th}=0.24-3.0$ TeV and $\tau_0<20-26$ for a broad kernel ($\epsilon=0.36$) over $E_{\rm th}=0.24-2.4$ TeV, at the nominal $90\%$ confidence level. An intrinsic high-energy cutoff weakens the narrow-kernel limits by factors of $2.2-7.4$ and the broad-kernel limits by factors of $2.0-5.0$. We further investigate DM velocity effects and combine NGC 1068 with CGCG 420-015 and TXS 0506+056 to extend the threshold reach. In a vector-mediator realization, an optimistic adiabatic DM spike can yield constraints on the neutrino coupling competitive with laboratory bounds for broad energy-transfer kernels.

</details>


### [69] [Automated two-loop fermion-bubble contributions at high multiplicity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35655)
*Dario Kermanschah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes finite, gauge-invariant two-loop QCD corrections with $N_f$ quark flavors for $q\bar q \to n\gamma^{(*)}$ ($n=2$–$8$), using local UV renormalisation, IR factorisation, analytic loop-energy integration, and automated threshold subtraction; results are obtained via adaptive multi-channel Monte Carlo and interfaced with parton distributions up to five photons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide precise, infrared- and ultraviolet-finite virtual corrections for multi-photon production in hadronic collisions, essential for high-precision tests of the Standard Model and background modeling at colliders.

Method: Gauge-invariant helicity-summed two-loop computation; local UV renormalisation; infrared factorisation; analytic loop-energy integration; automated threshold subtraction; adaptive multi-channel Monte Carlo sampling over loop momenta, helicities, and identical-particle permutations.

Result: Finite integrals over real spatial momenta for $n = 2$ to $8$ photons; quantified generation and evaluation costs across multiplicities; direct integration with parton distribution functions yielding inclusive and differential virtual corrections up to $n = 5$.

Conclusion: A robust, automatable framework is established for computing multi-leg two-loop QCD amplitudes with massless external states, enabling precision predictions for multi-photon final states in $q\bar q$ annihilation.

Abstract: We compute the gauge-invariant, helicity-summed two-loop $N_f$ contribution for $q\bar q\to n\gamma^{(*)}$, $n=2,\ldots,8$. Local ultraviolet renormalisation, infrared factorisation, analytic loop-energy integration and a new automated threshold subtraction yield finite integrals over real spatial momenta. Adaptive multi-channel Monte Carlo samples loop momenta, helicities and identical-particle permutations. We quantify generation and evaluation costs across multiplicities. The same representation is integrated directly with parton distributions through five photons, yielding inclusive and differential virtual corrections.

</details>


### [70] [Valence-quark-core and meson-cloud effects on electromagnetic form factors of $Δ$ baryons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35705)
*Yutao Fan,Shuai Gui,Shixing Liu,Yongxin Guo,Xuyang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes electromagnetic form factors and properties of Δ baryons using the perturbative chiral quark model (PCQM), with quark wavefunctions fixed from proton charge data; it finds that charged Δ states are dominated by the three-quark core, while Δ⁰ is entirely meson-cloud–driven, yielding a nonzero magnetic moment, negative charge radius, and sizable magnetic radius — all parameter-free and consistent with lattice QCD and phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide consistent, parameter-free predictions for the electromagnetic structure of Δ baryons — especially the neutral Δ⁰, whose quark-core contribution vanishes — and to quantify the role of meson-cloud fluctuations in their electromagnetic observables.

Method: Perturbative chiral quark model (PCQM) with quark wavefunctions constrained by proton charge form factor data; electromagnetic form factors and radii computed including both three-quark core and meson-cloud contributions.

Result: Agreement with lattice-QCD and other phenomenological results; for Δ⁺⁺, Δ⁺, Δ⁻: observables dominated by three-quark core with small meson-cloud corrections; for Δ⁰: vanishing leading-order quark-core contribution → meson cloud fully determines response, yielding nonzero magnetic moment, negative charge radius, and sizable magnetic radius.

Conclusion: The PCQM provides robust, parameter-free predictions for Δ baryon electromagnetic structure; highlights essential role of meson-cloud dynamics, especially for neutral Δ⁰ where it solely governs electromagnetic observables.

Abstract: Electromagnetic form factors and electromagnetic properties of $\Delta$ baryons are studied in the perturbative chiral quark model (PCQM) using quark wavefunctions predetermined from proton charge form factor data. Our predictions agree reasonably with lattice-QCD and other phenomenological results. For charged $\Delta^{++}$, $\Delta^{+}$, and $\Delta^{-}$, observables are dominated by the three-quark core, with small meson-cloud corrections superimposed. For neutral $\Delta^{0}$, the leading-order quark core contribution vanishes, so meson-cloud corrections fully determine its electromagnetic response. The PCQM gives a nonzero magnetic moment, a negative charge radius, and a sizable magnetic radius for $\Delta^{0}$. This work provides consistent, parameter-free predictions for $\Delta$ baryon structure and highlights the role of meson cloud fluctuations.

</details>


### [71] [Lepton polarization and sensitivity to the form factors in $Λ\to p\,\ell^-\barν_\ell$ with lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35717)
*Qazi Maaz Us Salam,Ishtiaq Ahmed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes lepton polarization observables in Λ→pℓ⁻νℓ decays using lattice QCD form factors, showing how longitudinal polarization determines g₁/f₁, forward-backward asymmetries probe weak magnetism, and Rₘₑ tests lepton flavor universality—providing SM benchmarks and probes for lattice QCD validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide precise Standard Model predictions for lepton polarization observables in semileptonic Λ hyperon decay, enabling tests of SM validity, lepton flavor universality, and lattice QCD form factor calculations—especially given discrepancies between lattice QCD and older QCD sum-rule results.

Method: Computes longitudinal, transverse, and normal lepton polarizations, forward-backward asymmetries per spin state, and the lepton flavor universality ratio R_μe using lattice-QCD-derived Λ→p form factors; compares with QCD sum-rule form factors; analyzes q² dependence and sensitivity to g₁/f₁, weak magnetism, second-class currents, and electromagnetic final-state interactions.

Result: Longitudinal polarization directly determines g₁/f₁; electron and negative-helicity muon forward-backward asymmetries constrain weak magnetism; positive-helicity muon asymmetry is kinematically fixed; R_μe is nearly insensitive to g₁/f₁ and weak magnetism but its q² shape fixes muon longitudinal polarization; normal polarization arises from EM FSI and serves as SM baseline for T-violation searches; lattice vs. sum-rule inputs yield markedly different predictions.

Conclusion: The computed observables—especially A_FB(e), A_FB(μ⁻), and R_μe—are clean probes of hadronic structure and SM dynamics; their experimental measurement at BESIII, STCF, and LHCb will test both the Standard Model and the accuracy of lattice-QCD form factors for baryonic transitions.

Abstract: We investigate the polarization of the charged lepton in the semileptonic hyperon decay $\Lambda\to p\,\ell^-\bar\nu_\ell$ ($\ell=e,\mu$) within the Standard Model, using the form factors obtained from lattice QCD. We compute the longitudinal, transverse and normal polarization of the lepton, the forward-backward asymmetry of each lepton spin state, and the lepton flavor universality ratio $R_{\mu e}$. We show that the longitudinal polarization determines the ratio $g_1/f_1$ of the axial-vector to vector couplings, and that the forward-backward asymmetries of the electron and of negative-helicity muons determine the weak magnetism. The asymmetry of positive-helicity muons is fixed by kinematics up to a small dependence on the second-class form factors, whereas $R_{\mu e}$ is nearly independent of $g_1/f_1$ and the weak magnetism. For comparison, we also use the QCD sum-rule form factors, whose $g_1/f_1$ and weak magnetism are opposite in sign to experiment and lattice QCD, and find very different values for the observables that depend on them. Moreover, the $q^2$ dependence of $R_{\mu e}$ fixes the longitudinal polarization of the muon, which can thus be measured from the decay rates, without measuring the muon spin. The normal polarization is generated by the electromagnetic final-state interaction and provides the Standard Model reference for searches for time-reversal violation. The electron forward-backward asymmetry and $R_{\mu e}$ can be measured at BESIII and the proposed Super Tau-Charm Facility, and $R_{\mu e}$ also at LHCb. These measurements can test the Standard Model and the lattice-QCD form factors.

</details>


### [72] [The neutrino fog for inelastic dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35789)
*C. S. Amarasinghe*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how inelastic dark matter scattering alters the neutrino fog—i.e., irreducible neutrino background—in xenon-based direct detection experiments, revealing regions where the fog vanishes or reappears with exposure, and showing that reduced neutrino rate uncertainties can paradoxically raise the neutrino floor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how inelastic dark matter scattering modifies the neutrino fog—the fundamental sensitivity limit imposed by coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering—in liquid xenon detectors.

Method: Analytical and numerical characterization of recoil spectra under inelastic scattering kinematics; statistical treatment of neutrino fog onset including multiple uncertain backgrounds; evaluation of discovery limits and neutrino floor dependence on neutrino rate uncertainties.

Result: Inelastic scattering reshapes the recoil spectrum, leading to parameter regions where the neutrino fog disappears entirely for future xenon experiments, or reappears at larger exposures; background normalization uncertainties cause discontinuous jumps in fog onset versus dark matter mass/splitting, while discovery limits remain smooth; lower neutrino rate uncertainties can unintuitively increase the derived neutrino floor due to fog opacity considerations.

Conclusion: The neutrino fog for inelastic dark matter is highly nontrivial and parameter-dependent; conventional assumptions about monotonic fog behavior and floor reduction with improved neutrino modeling do not hold, necessitating tailored sensitivity forecasts for inelastic models.

Abstract: We characterize the behavior of the neutrino fog for inelastic dark matter scattering on xenon. The inelastic scattering kinematics reshape the recoil spectrum of the signal and produce a qualitatively different neutrino fog landscape. In some regions of the inelastic parameter space the fog is completely absent for future xenon experiments at any scale, while in other regions the fog is dispelled only to reappear at larger exposures. We find that multiple background sources with uncertain normalizations can make the onset of the fog jump discontinuously with the dark matter mass and mass splitting, while the discovery limit varies smoothly. We also describe a mechanism by which reduced uncertainties on neutrino rates may have the unintuitive effect of raising neutrino floors derived from the fog opacity.

</details>


### [73] [Holographic Approaches to Hadron Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35818)
*Luigi Cappiello,Anton Rebhan,Shigeki Sugimoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a comprehensive review of holographic approaches to QCD and hadron structure, covering both top-down (e.g., probe flavor branes in the Witten model) and bottom-up (e.g., minimal AdS₅-based) frameworks, with emphasis on meson, baryon, and glueball phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge string theory and low-energy QCD by leveraging gauge/gravity duality, enabling analytic and semi-analytic studies of strongly coupled hadronic physics inaccessible via perturbative methods.

Method: Review and comparative analysis of top-down holographic QCD (derived from D-brane constructions in string theory, specifically probe flavor branes in the Witten model) and bottom-up models (phenomenological 5D anti-de Sitter space constructions), focusing on their implementation and predictive frameworks for hadron spectra and properties.

Result: Systematic exposition of key predictions—mass spectra, decay constants, form factors, and Regge trajectories—for mesons, baryons, and glueballs; highlights successes and limitations of each approach in reproducing QCD phenomenology.

Conclusion: Holographic QCD offers a powerful non-perturbative framework for modeling hadron structure; top-down models provide theoretical consistency with string theory, while bottom-up models offer greater flexibility and phenomenological fit, suggesting complementary roles in advancing our understanding of confinement and chiral dynamics.

Abstract: We give an introduction to and an overview of holographic approaches to QCD and hadron structure. After an introduction to the string-theoretic foundations of holographic QCD, we review the predictions of top-down and bottom-up models for the phenomenology of mesons, baryons, and glueballs. We focus on the top-down model following from probe flavor branes in the Witten model of low-energy Yang-Mills theory and, in the more phenomenological approach of bottom-up models, on minimal models based on five-dimensional anti-de Sitter space.

</details>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [74] [Observational constraints on a non-minimally coupled scalar field in non-metricity gravity with an extra degree of freedom](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33146)
*Saddam Hussain,Ghulam Murtaza,Avik De*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores non-minimally coupled scalar fields in non-metricity gravity with a non-coincident connection, revealing an extra dynamical degree of freedom that enables viable cosmic evolution—including extended matter domination and stable late-time de Sitter expansion—consistent with DESI BAO, SN Ia, and cosmic chronometer data, and confirmed via autonomous dynamical-system analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore cosmological dynamics beyond the coincident gauge in symmetric teleparallel (non-metricity) gravity, where a non-trivial connection introduces new degrees of freedom that may resolve or alleviate tensions in standard cosmology while preserving background viability.

Method: Formulates field equations for a non-minimally coupled scalar field in flat FLRW spacetime within non-metricity gravity using a non-coincident affine connection; constructs two models (power-law and exponential coupling + exponential potential); performs Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) fitting against DESI BAO, two SN Ia compilations, and cosmic chronometers; complements with autonomous dynamical-system analysis to classify critical points and stability.

Result: Both models yield extended matter-dominated eras followed by stable late-time de Sitter attractors; best-fit parameters are statistically consistent with ΛCDM; dynamical analysis reveals a physically viable sequence of stiff-fluid → matter-domination → de Sitter critical points, confirming thermal history compatibility.

Conclusion: Non-minimal scalar–non-metricity couplings with non-coincident connections provide cosmologically viable alternatives to ΛCDM, supported jointly by observational constraints and phase-space stability—demonstrating that non-metricity can meaningfully enrich scalar-tensor cosmology beyond metric-affine frameworks.

Abstract: We investigate a non-minimally coupled scalar field in non-metricity gravity, adopting a non-trivial connection class in spatially flat FLRW spacetimes. In contrast to the coincident-gauge choice, this connection class activates an additional dynamical degree of freedom (DoF) that modifies the background evolution. We study two representative models, defined by power-law and exponential coupling functions with the exponential potential for scalar field. Confronting both models with recent combined datasets - DESI BAO, two independent supernova compilations, and cosmic chronometers - we find that each reproduces an extended matter-dominated era followed by a stable late-time de-Sitter attractor, with inferred parameters consistent with the standard $\Lambda$CDM model. A complementary autonomous dynamical-system analysis identifies a sequence of critical points describing successive stiff-fluid, transient matter-dominated, and stable de-Sitter phases, ensuring a viable thermal history of the universe. The observational and dynamical results are mutually consistent and support the cosmological viability of the proposed models.

</details>


### [75] [A unified dark sector from a single canonical scalar field in Bohmian Wheeler-DeWitt cosmology with a cuscuton-inspired potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33187)
*Anant Badal,Rikpratik Sengupta,Kaushik Bhattacharya*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper quantizes a FLRW minisuperspace with a cuscuton-inspired hyperbolic scalar potential, uses the de Broglie–Bohm interpretation of the Wheeler–DeWitt wave function to derive cosmological histories, and shows that for large mass-to-Hubble ratio κ ≫ 1, the model yields an effective dust + vacuum background consistent with ΛCDM at O(1/κ) accuracy, with quantum zero-point energy contributing a dust-like density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a quantum cosmological model where a single scalar field with a cuscuton-type superpotential generates both dark energy (de Sitter vacuum) and cold dark matter-like behavior (dust-like zero-point energy), without phenomenological fitting, bridging quantum gravity effects and late-time cosmic acceleration.

Method: Canonical quantization of a spatially flat FLRW minisuperspace with a canonical scalar field governed by a hyperbolic potential derived from a cuscuton-type superpotential; analysis of the Wheeler–DeWitt equation in the de Broglie–Bohm interpretation; adiabatic Gaussian approximation for large κ; full numerical integration of the Wheeler–DeWitt equation; extraction of Bohmian trajectories and averaged energy densities.

Result: For κ ≫ 1: (i) adiabatic Gaussian wave packet yields classical condensate with a^{-3} redshifting excitation density; (ii) quantum zero-point energy at packet center behaves as dust (ρ ∝ a^{-3}) and matches numerical results at 10^{-3} level; (iii) quantum corrections to Hubble rate scale as inverse cell action; (iv) background evolution matches ΛCDM distance measures with D_V deviation ~1.3/κ; (v) dark energy has w = −1 exactly.

Conclusion: A single-field quantum cosmological model with cuscuton-inspired potential naturally produces simultaneous dark energy (exact de Sitter) and emergent dust-like behavior from zero-point energy, offering a unified quantum-origin explanation for ΛCDM components without fine-tuning or data fitting—validating the de Broglie–Bohm approach for extracting semiclassical cosmology from the Wheeler–DeWitt framework.

Abstract: We quantize a spatially flat FLRW minisuperspace containing one canonical scalar field with the hyperbolic potential generated by a cuscuton-type superpotential, and extract cosmological histories from the Wheeler-DeWitt wave function in the de Broglie-Bohm interpretation. For the canonical potential parameter, related to the original cuscuton couplings, below an explicit critical value the potential has a unique nondegenerate minimum with positive height, and the ratio $\kappa$ of the scalar mass to the de Sitter Hubble rate at the minimum diverges as $\kappa^2\simeq 3b/\delta$, where $\delta$ is the relative distance to criticality. For the original hyperbolic potential, $\kappa\leq 3/\sqrt{2}$, whereas its cuscuton-inspired generalization allows a large hierarchy between scalar oscillation and expansion rates. For $\kappa\gg1$, small excitation and sufficiently large cell action, the reduced wave equation has an adiabatic Gaussian solution describing a de Sitter vacuum, a classical oscillating condensate whose averaged excitation density redshifts as $a^{-3}$, and, at the packet centre, a leading quantum density equal to the zero-point energy of the effective homogeneous oscillator divided by the physical cell volume, which is also dust-like. Full numerical solutions of the Wheeler-DeWitt equation support the averaged zero-point law at the reported $10^{-3}$ level, reproduce its dependence on the position of the Bohmian particle inside the packet, show that quantum corrections to the present Hubble rate converge as the inverse of the cell action, and support a single-field dust-plus-vacuum description at the homogeneous background level when $\kappa\gg1$. The classical histories approach the $\Lambda$CDM distance relations with a deviation of $1.3/\kappa$ in $D_V$. The dark energy component has $w=-1$ by construction, and the model is not fitted to data.

</details>


### [76] [Black holes from a nonlocal equation of state: horizon geometry, singularity structure and thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33222)
*Hishan Farfán-Bachiloglu,C. R. Muniz,Francisco Tello-Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a Yukawa-type density profile from a nonlocal equation of state for an anisotropic fluid in Kerr–Schild geometry, yielding black hole solutions with finite-range 'hair', exact horizon classification, strong-curvature singularities, integrable curvature scalars at the origin for vanishing central mass, and rich thermodynamic structure including defect-based topology and Lambert-function–governed heat-capacity divergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how nonlocal equations of state for anisotropic fluids in relativistic gravity can generate physically viable black hole solutions with regularity properties, controlled singularity behavior, and novel thermodynamic features—beyond standard general relativity and nonlinear electrodynamics.

Method: Analytical derivation in static, spherically symmetric Kerr–Schild spacetimes; classification of horizons via metric root analysis; computation of surface gravities, curvature invariants (Ricci scalar, Kretschmann scalar), and singularity strength (Królak and Tipler criteria); thermodynamic analysis including first law, Smarr relation, heat capacity, and topological defect characterization using winding numbers.

Result: A unique Yukawa density profile emerges; exact horizon classification (horizonless, Schwarzschild-like, two-horizon, extremal); inner surface gravity > outer in two-horizon regime; global monopole/Kantowski–Sachs limits for zero central mass; integrable curvature scalars despite central divergence; power-law generalization yields incomplete gamma–based solutions interpolating between singular and de Sitter cores; closed-form thermodynamic quantities and Lambert-W–governed heat-capacity divergence; thermodynamic topology reveals winding-defect transitions.

Conclusion: Nonlocal fluid closure in Kerr–Schild geometry yields a consistent, analytically tractable framework for black holes with finite-range hair, controlled singularity structure, and rich thermodynamic phase behavior—including topological defect transitions—without requiring exotic matter models like nonlinear electrodynamics.

Abstract: We show that a nonlocal equation of state for an anisotropic fluid reduces to a linear equation for the enclosed matter and uniquely selects a Yukawa-type density profile in a static, spherically symmetric Kerr--Schild geometry. The resulting neutral solution has finite-range hair, exponentially screened at large distances, with the asymptotic mass as its only conserved charge, and admits an exact horizon classification: horizonless configurations, Schwarzschild-like black holes, two-horizon geometries with a Cauchy horizon, and an extremal configuration whose mass and radius are obtained in closed form. We prove that the magnitude of the inner surface gravity exceeds that of the outer horizon throughout the two-horizon regime. When the central mass vanishes, the geometry approaches a global monopole in the static branch and a Kantowski--Sachs-type region in the nonstatic branch. The singularity satisfies Królak's strong-curvature criterion and is Tipler-strong along non-radial geodesics in the spacelike branch. For vanishing central mass, the Ricci scalar and the square root of the Kretschmann scalar are locally integrable with respect to the invariant spacetime volume, despite diverging at the origin. A power-law generalization of the nonlocal closure yields solutions expressed through incomplete gamma functions, interpolating between the singular core and a regular black hole with a de~Sitter core, without invoking nonlinear electrodynamics. We obtain closed-form surface gravities, an extended first-law identity and a Smarr relation, and locate the heat-capacity divergence using the lower branch of the Lambert function. Finally, thermodynamic topology distinguishes a Schwarzschild-like regime with one defect from an extremal regime with two opposite-winding defects that merge at the maximum Hawking temperature.

</details>


### [77] [Hyperboloidal Initial Data with Prescribed Asymptotic Charges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33266)
*Károly Csukás,János Östör,István Rácz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces a novel method to construct hyperboloidal initial data for Einstein's equations with direct analytic control over asymptotic charges (e.g., Bondi energy and angular momentum) using a parabolic-hyperbolic constraint formulation and a Fuchsian-inspired spectral solver; demonstrates feasibility by constructing data for a spinning black hole with specified Bondi charges starting from Schwarzschild seed data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precise, analytic control over asymptotic gravitational charges—such as Bondi energy and angular momentum—in hyperboloidal initial data, circumventing the usual difficulty of indirectly inferring such quantities only after solving the full constraint equations.

Method: Employs the parabolic-hyperbolic formulation of the Einstein constraint equations combined with a Fuchsian-analysis-inspired spectral numerical solver; treats asymptotic degrees of freedom as free parameters that directly determine infinite towers of asymptotic charges, thereby prescribing the solution’s asymptotics *a priori*.

Result: Explicitly constructs hyperboloidal initial data for a spinning black hole with Bondi energy $E_B = 2$ and angular momentum $J_B = 1/2$, derived from seed data on a hyperboloidal slice of Schwarzschild spacetime ($M=1$).

Conclusion: The method establishes a robust framework for engineering hyperboloidal initial data with pre-specified asymptotic physical charges, enhancing control and interpretability in numerical relativity and gravitational-wave modeling.

Abstract: A novel method is introduced for constructing hyperboloidal initial data with direct analytic control over asymptotic charges prior to solving the Einstein constraint equations. Using the parabolic-hyperbolic formulation of the constraints together with a Fuchsian-analysis-inspired spectral solver, the asymptotic degrees of freedom, which determine infinite towers of asymptotic charges, including the Bondi energy and angular momentum, are used to prescribe the asymptotic behavior of solutions. As a proof of concept, we explicitly construct hyperboloidal initial data for a spinning black hole with Bondi energy $E_B = 2$ and angular momentum $J_B = 1/2$, starting from seed data induced on a hyperboloidal slice of the non-rotating Schwarzschild spacetime with mass $M=1$.

</details>


### [78] [Impact of nonlinear electrodynamics on quasinormal modes of rotating black strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33309)
*F. S. Mirekhtiary,I. Sakalli*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes quasinormal modes of rotating anti-de Sitter black strings coupled to a power-Maxwell field with negative exponent s, revealing charge- and rotation-dependent spectral features including exceptional points, non-oscillatory decay, hypergeometric spectra at critical charge, modified dispersion scaling, and absence of superradiance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how charge (via power-Maxwell field with negative exponent s) and rotation affect the quasinormal mode spectrum of AdS black strings—particularly deviations from neutral or static limits, emergence of exceptional points, and validity of near-horizon approximations.

Method: Combines three complementary numerical techniques—Horowitz-Hubeny series expansion, Chebyshev collocation, and time-domain evolution—each implemented in two independent codes; cross-validated against the planar Schwarzschild-AdS limit; analytically leverages rotational boost symmetry to relate rotating and static spectra.
