Result: Identifies a critical reduced charge q̂_c ≈ 0.72626 where the least-damped mode pair coalesces on the imaginary axis (exceptional point), leading to purely damped ringdown; finds exact hypergeometric spectrum at the branch endpoint; derives modified asymptotic mode dispersion k^{1/(1−4s)} replacing k^{−1/5} for neutral branes; shows rotation extends the exceptional point into a line in (q, a)-space, adds a purely damped axisymmetric tower, and suppresses superradiance; demonstrates failure of prior near-horizon estimates to capture these effects.

Conclusion: Charge and rotation qualitatively reshape the quasinormal spectrum of AdS black strings in ways invisible to standard approximations: exceptional dynamics, non-oscillatory decay, exact solvability at criticality, altered high-k scaling, and robust absence of superradiance—highlighting the necessity of global spectral analysis.

Abstract: We study massless scalar perturbations of rotating anti-de Sitter black strings sourced by a power-Maxwell field with negative exponent $s$. The charge enters the metric function as $-Qr^{p}$ with $p=2/(1-2s)$, and the case $s=-1/2$ gives a linear term with an algebraic horizon. Since rotation is a local boost of the static string, the rotating spectrum follows exactly from the static one evaluated at a frequency-dependent momentum. We compute the spectrum with the Horowitz-Hubeny series, Chebyshev collocation and time-domain evolution, each implemented in two independent codes and checked against the planar Schwarzschild-AdS limit. The charge acts in two ways. At the reduced charge $\hat q_c=0.72626$ the least damped pair of modes meets on the imaginary axis, so more strongly charged strings ring down without oscillation, and at the endpoint of the branch the spectrum is exactly hypergeometric. At large transverse momentum the distance of the modes from the light cone grows as $k^{1/(1-4s)}$, replacing the $k^{-1/5}$ law of the neutral brane. Rotation continues the exceptional point into a line in the plane of charge and rotation, adds a purely damped axisymmetric tower, and never produces superradiant growth. A near-horizon estimate used earlier for this geometry misses all of these effects.

</details>


### [79] [Neutron Star--Primordial Black Hole Binaries: Dynamical Tides, Resonance Locking, and Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33314)
*Isaac Legred,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how dynamical tides between a neutron star and a low-mass primordial black hole—unlike standard compact binaries—can drive inspiral or outspiral, mediated by gravitational-wave emission when neutron star oscillation modes stay resonantly locked to the orbit; such effects may yield detectable signals in pulsar timing or gravitational-wave data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand anomalous orbital evolution in neutron star–primordial black hole binaries, where conventional binary dynamics break down due to resonant dynamical tides—a regime unexplored in typical compact binary studies.

Method: Analytical modeling of resonant tidal coupling between neutron star fluid modes and orbital motion, incorporating gravitational-wave backreaction while enforcing mode-orbit resonance locking.

Result: Dynamical tides can either accelerate inspiral or induce sustained outspiral depending on orbital configuration and spin–orbit alignment; gravitational-wave emission regulates the energy exchange, leading to potentially observable deviations in orbital period derivatives (pulsar timing) or phase evolution (gravitational-wave chirp).

Conclusion: Resonant dynamical tides in neutron star–primordial black hole binaries introduce qualitatively new orbital evolution pathways, offering novel probes of both neutron star interiors and primordial black hole populations via multi-messenger timing and gravitational-wave observations.

Abstract: The orbital evolution of a neutron star and a much lower-mass primordial black hole can differ strikingly from that of typical compact binaries. Dynamical tides, analogous to those in the Solar System's giant planet-moon systems, can accelerate inspiral or induce outspiral, depending on the orbital configuration. We show how gravitational-wave emission mediates this evolution when neutron star modes remain locked in resonance with the orbit. These effects may leave observable signatures in pulsar timing or gravitational waves.

</details>


### [80] [Constraining Ultralight Dark Matter in Dynamical Dark Energy Cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33332)
*Muhammad Awais,Hafiz Inam Ullah,Rafael C. Nunes,Paula S. Ferreira,Tonatiuh Matos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies scalar-field dark matter (SFDM) within a dynamical dark energy cosmology (beyond ΛCDM), constraining it with CMB (Planck), BAO (DESI DR2), and SNe Ia (Pantheon+) data; it finds SFDM suppresses small-scale matter fluctuations in a mass-dependent way, yields a lower bound log₁₀(m_ϕ/eV) > −24.04, and does not alter the evidence for evolving (including phantom) dark energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend SFDM analysis beyond the standard ΛCDM model and assess its compatibility with and impact on dynamical dark energy scenarios, particularly whether SFDM alters inferences about dark energy evolution or phantom crossing.

Method: Consistent implementation of SFDM in a dynamical dark energy cosmology at background and linear perturbation levels; Bayesian parameter inference using Planck CMB, DESI DR2 BAO, and Pantheon+ supernova data.

Result: Lighter SFDM masses cause stronger scale-dependent suppression of matter power and larger CMB temperature spectrum modifications; heavier masses recover CDM behavior; joint analysis gives 95% lower limit log₁₀(m_ϕ/eV) > −24.04; SFDM substitution does not qualitatively change dynamical dark energy constraints or evidence for low-redshift phantom crossing.

Conclusion: SFDM remains viable in dynamical dark energy frameworks, with tight mass constraints from combined probes, and does not undermine the inferred dynamics or phantom nature of dark energy.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological implications of scalar-field dark matter (SFDM) in a dynamical dark energy framework, extending previous analyses that considered SFDM within the standard $\Lambda$CDM cosmological model. We consistently implement the scenario at both the background and linear perturbation levels. We perform Bayesian parameter inference using Planck CMB measurements, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation (BAO) data, and Pantheon+ Type Ia supernovae to constrain the model parameter space. Our theoretical predictions show that lighter scalar-field masses produce increasingly strong scale-dependent suppression of matter fluctuations and more pronounced modifications to the CMB temperature power spectrum, whereas heavier masses progressively approach the cold dark matter limit. For the SFDM mass, we obtain a one-sided 95\% lower limit of $\log_{10}(m_\phi/{\rm eV})>-24.04$ from our main joint analysis. We find that replacing the standard cold dark matter fluid with SFDM does not qualitatively alter the constraints on dynamical dark energy or the evidence for its evolution, including a phantom crossing at low redshifts.

</details>


### [81] [Accurate pulsar timing array likelihoods: better low-rank covariance for gravitational wave background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33444)
*Heling Deng,Ken D. Olum,Rutger van Haasteren*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a new low-rank covariance construction for modeling the gravitational wave background in pulsar timing array analyses, improving spectral fidelity and inference accuracy over conventional diagonal Fourier-basis models, without increasing computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional Fourier-basis modeling with diagonal covariance at ~0.1 yr⁻¹ frequency spacing is computationally efficient but insensitive to sub-bin spectral features, leading to biased parameter inference.

Method: The authors construct a low-rank covariance matrix by maximizing the ensemble-averaged log-likelihood over realizations from the true model—yielding a generally non-diagonal, spectrum-aware basis-domain covariance.

Result: On two mock datasets, the new model achieves higher likelihood accuracy and more faithful recovery of fine-scale spectral features compared to the standard Fourier-diagonal approach, with comparable computational cost.

Conclusion: The proposed ensemble-averaged log-likelihood optimization yields a superior low-rank approximation that preserves spectral structure and improves inference reliability while maintaining efficiency.

Abstract: In pulsar timing array analyses, the stochastic process describing the gravitational wave background is typically modeled using a Fourier basis at discrete frequencies with an interval $\sim 0.1~\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$, with a diagonal Fourier-domain covariance determined by the power spectral density at the corresponding frequencies. Although this rank-reduced modeling significantly accelerates likelihood evaluation and provides a good approximation in many scenarios, it can lead to biased parameter inference because it is insensitive to spectral features on scales smaller than $\sim 0.1~\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$. We introduce a better construction of the low-rank covariance for low-frequency processes by maximizing the log-likelihood averaged over an ensemble of realizations drawn from the true model likelihood. In this approach, the basis-domain covariance matrix is sensitive to the entire model spectrum and is generally non-diagonal. Using two mock datasets, we demonstrate improved likelihood accuracy and more faithful recovery of spectral features compared to the conventional Fourier-diagonal model. Benchmark tests show that this new model requires a computational cost comparable to that of the conventional approach.

</details>


### [82] [Acoustic Schwarzschild-like black hole and analogous Hawking radiation in Einstein-bumblebee gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33481)
*Chikun Ding,Yuebing Zhou,Yu Shi,Yuehua Xiao,Jiliang Jing*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an acoustic Schwarzschild-like black hole in Einstein-bumblebee theory using Gross-Pitaevskii theory, identifies dual gravitational and acoustic horizons, computes Hawking and dumb hole temperatures, analyzes grey-body factors and energy emission spectra for scalar particles, and highlights experimentally testable opposite parameter dependences on ξ and ℓ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore analog gravity and Lorentz violation effects by constructing a controllable acoustic black hole in Einstein-bumblebee theory — a framework incorporating spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking — and to identify observable signatures distinguishing gravitational from acoustic horizons.

Method: Uses Gross-Pitaevskii theory to model a Bose-Einstein condensate in a Schwarzschild-like background spacetime within Einstein-bumblebee gravity; derives effective acoustic metric, locates event and acoustic horizons analytically; computes Hawking temperatures via surface gravity; solves Klein-Gordon equation for scalar fields to obtain grey-body factors and energy emission rates.

Result: Finds two types of horizons: gravitational (r_h = 2M) and acoustic (r_ac− ∈ (2M, 4M], r_ac ≥ 4M), dependent on tuning parameter ξ and Lorentz-violating coupling ℓ; Hawking temperature T_h depends only on ℓ, while dumb hole temperature T_ac depends on both ℓ and ξ, peaking at T_acm = T_h/8; grey-body factor increases with ξ but decreases with ℓ at low frequencies, approaches unity at high frequencies; energy emission rate exhibits Planck-like spectrum with peak frequency ω_m, and shows opposite ξ/ℓ dependence — increasing with ξ, decreasing with ℓ.

Conclusion: The acoustic black hole in Einstein-bumblebee theory provides a theoretically consistent analog system where the opposing parametric dependencies of emission properties on ξ and ℓ serve as a distinctive, experimentally falsifiable signature — potentially enabling observational discrimination between fundamental Lorentz violation and standard GR predictions in astrophysical black hole analogs.

Abstract: We derive an acoustic Schwarzschild-like black hole and study its analogous Hawking radiation in Einstein-bumblebee theory by using Gross-Pitaevskii theory in the background of Schwarzschild-like spacetime. This black hole have two kinds of event horizons: one is the gravitational horizon $r_h=2M$(black hole horizon), the others are the internal and external acoustic horizons $2M<r_{ac-}\leq4M,\;r_{ac}\geq4M$(dumb hole horizon) which depend on the tuning parameter $\xi$ and Lorentz violation coupling constant $\ell$. The corresponding Hawking temperature $T_h$ depends only on $\ell$, however $T_{ac}$ depends on $\ell$ and $\xi$. The dumb hole temperature $T_{ac}$ has a maximum $T_{acm}=T_h/8$. When considering the scalar particles radiating from the dumb hole, we find that in low frequency, the grey-body factor increases with the tuning parameter $\xi$, but decreases with $\ell$. In the high frequency, it approaches to $1$. Its energy emission rate is similar to the Planck's black spectrum where there has a maximum at a given peak frequency $\omega_m$. The energy emission rate also increases with the tuning parameter $\xi$, but decreases with $\ell$. This opposite dependence relationship is the most experimentally valuable feature and can directly benchmark against observations of real astrophysical black holes.

</details>


### [83] [Gravitational Waves from Ringing Dyonic Black Holes and Estimation of Parameters in Einstein-Bumblebee-Maxwell Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33569)
*Sikha Gogoi,Umananda Dev Goswami*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of dyonic Reissner-Nordström-like black holes in Einstein-bumblebee-Maxwell gravity, using 6th-order Padé-WKB and time-domain simulations; it assesses QNM detectability and parameter estimation with LISA for supermassive black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe Lorentz violation via black hole quasinormal modes in a modified gravity theory (Einstein-bumblebee-Maxwell) that introduces a bumblebee field coupling to gravity and electromagnetism, and to assess observational prospects with LISA.

Method: Computes QNMs using the 6th-order Padé-averaged WKB approximation; validates results via time-domain integration of scalar and gravitational perturbations; performs Fisher matrix analysis to estimate parameter measurability (BH mass, Lorentz-violating bumblebee parameter) for LISA observations.

Result: QNM frequencies and damping rates depend sensitively on the bumblebee Lorentz-violating parameter; time-domain ringdown confirms dominant QNM; Fisher analysis shows high SNR and tight constraints on BH mass and bumblebee parameter for supermassive BHs (≥10⁶ M⊙) at z ≲ 1 and dL = 3 Gpc with LISA.

Conclusion: Dyonic RN-like black holes in Einstein-bumblebee-Maxwell gravity exhibit Lorentz-violation–sensitive QNMs, and LISA can robustly detect and constrain both BH mass and the bumblebee parameter for nearby supermassive black holes, offering a novel test of low-energy Lorentz violation in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the quasinormal mode (QNM) frequencies of dyonic Reissner-Nordström (RN)-like black holes associated with scalar and gravitational perturbations in the framework of Einstein-bumblebee-Maxwell gravity theory. The QNMs are calculated using the 6th-order Padé averaged WKB approximation method. We analyse the dependence of oscillation frequencies and damping rate of the BHs for the free model parameters. To further verify the QNM spectrum, we study the time evolution of both the scalar and gravitational perturbations around the BHs and extract the dominant QNM from the resulting ringdown signal. Moreover, we try to estimate the BH parameters using Fisher analysis method for the future gravitational wave detector named Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA). We found that for supermassive BHs with mass around $10^6$ -- $10^8 M_\odot$ and at lower redshifts, the signal-to-noise ratio for the detection of a QNM signal by the LISA is high. Further, for a representative source at a luminosity distance of $3$ Gpc, the BH mass at the detector reference frame and the Lorentz violating parameter are well constrained for supermassive BHs with mass $\ge 10^6.$

</details>


### [84] [A no-go theorem for closed timelike curves in globally null-foliated spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33706)
*Hongsheng Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new causality criterion based on the existence of a globally defined null gradient function, proving that such a function forbids closed timelike curves (CTCs); it applies this to Kundt and Robinson–Trautman spacetimes and clarifies the geometric role of parameters in known CTC constructions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a new, elementary causality criterion independent of energy conditions, time functions, or topology—specifically, one grounded solely in the null eikonal equation—to address cases where classical criteria (e.g., time function theorem) are inapplicable.

Method: Uses differential geometry and Lorentzian causality theory; proves a key lemma that a null covector cannot be orthogonal to a timelike vector, implying strict monotonicity of any null-gradient function along timelike curves; applies the null eikonal equation ∇_a f ∇^a f = 0 globally; analyzes Kundt, Robinson–Trautman, Misner, Ori, and Ahmed spacetimes via their adapted null coordinates and geometric decompositions.

Result: 1) A spacetime with a globally defined function f satisfying g^{ab}(∇_a f)(∇_b f) = 0 is CTC-free; 2) This condition is logically independent of stable causality; 3) No Kundt or Robinson–Trautman spacetime admits CTCs where the null coordinate is globally single-valued, regardless of Λ or rotation one-form; 4) Misner/Ori/Ahmed CTCs arise solely from compact null directions becoming timelike—the cosmological constant, expansion, and shear are irrelevant to CTC formation in those models.

Conclusion: The null eikonal criterion provides a powerful, geometrically transparent, and broadly applicable tool for ruling out CTCs without invoking energy conditions or global topological assumptions; it reveals that certain widely studied CTC mechanisms reduce to simple causal transitions of null directions, exposing previously hidden structural simplicity.

Abstract: The study of closed timelike curves has proceeded one spacetime at a time, and the criteria available form three families, the time function criteria, the energy condition criteria, and the topological criteria. We initiate a fourth family of criteria, elementary in form and resting on a single scalar equation rather than any energy condition. We prove that a spacetime admitting a globally defined function whose gradient is everywhere null cannot contain a closed timelike curve, and that the hypothesis is logically independent of stable causality rather than weaker than it. The proof turns on one algebraic fact, that a null covector is never orthogonal to a timelike vector, so the function grows strictly along every timelike curve and cannot close. Because the null eikonal equation alone decides the criterion, we can use it where the classical time function theorem is silent. We establish for the first time that no Kundt spacetime and no Robinson--Trautman spacetime can contain a closed timelike curve on any region where the null coordinate is globally single valued, whatever the cosmological constant and the rotation one form, which settles a question left open for gyratonic waves. We further show that the Misner, Ori and Ahmed constructions reduce to the statement that a compact null direction has turned timelike, and that the cosmological constant, the expansion and the shear are spectators in all of them.

</details>


### [85] [Forecasting Strongly Lensed Binary Black Hole Rates from the Stochastic Gravitational-Wave Background with TianQin](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33751)
*Zheng-Cheng Liang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a framework using the TianQin space-based gravitational-wave detector to infer stellar-mass binary black hole (BBH) population properties from the millihertz stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB), and propagates those constraints to forecast strongly lensed BBH merger rates detectable by ground-based detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a quantitative link between millihertz SGWB measurements and predictions of strongly lensed BBH mergers observable by ground-based detectors, enabling population-level probes of BBH cosmic merger history and galaxy-scale strong lensing.

Method: Bayesian inference of BBH population parameters from a one-year mock TianQin SGWB observation; propagation of the posterior through a galaxy-scale strong lensing model incorporating image-level selection effects and system magnification threshold (μ_sys ≥ 2); distinction between lensed events with ≥1 detected image and fully associated two-image lens systems.

Result: Median predicted rates: R_event^det = 5.50×10⁻⁴ yr⁻¹ (≥1 detected image), R_sys^det = 2.54×10⁻⁴ yr⁻¹ (associated two-image systems), F_sys^det = 3.35×10⁻⁴ (detectable system fraction); posterior uncertainties reduced by 4.0–6.5% compared to prior-only forecasts.

Conclusion: SGWB-informed BBH population constraints from TianQin significantly tighten forecasts of strongly lensed BBH mergers for ground-based detectors, demonstrating a viable pathway to synergistic multi-band gravitational-wave astrophysics.

Abstract: The millihertz stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) provides a population-level probe of stellar-mass binary black holes (BBHs) and their cosmic merger history. We develop a framework using TianQin alone to propagate SGWB-informed BBH population constraints into forecasts of strongly lensed BBH mergers detectable by ground-based gravitational-wave detectors. The framework distinguishes between lensed events with at least one detected image and lens systems in which both images are detected and successfully associated. Using a one-year mock observation, we infer the BBH population and propagate the resulting posterior through a galaxy-scale lensing model with image-level selection. For the adopted system-magnification threshold, \(\mu_{\rm sys}\ge 2\), the median predicted rate of lensed events with at least one detected image is \(R_{\rm event}^{\rm det}=5.50\times10^{-4}\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}\), while the median rate of associated two-image lens systems is \(R_{\rm sys}^{\rm det}=2.54\times10^{-4}\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}\); the median detectable system fraction is \(\mathcal{F}_{\rm sys}^{\rm det}=3.35\times10^{-4}\). Compared with the prior-only propagation, the pooled 16th--84th percentile widths decrease by 6.5\% for the event rate, 6.4\% for the system rate, and 4.0\% for the system fraction. Our work establishes a quantitative link between millihertz SGWB inference and forecasts of strongly lensed BBH mergers for ground-based gravitational-wave observations.

</details>


### [86] [Massive Scalar Quasinormal Modes and Quasibound States of Charged Black Holes with Logarithmic corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33883)
*Prosenjit Paul,Rickmoy Samanta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how logarithmic deformations of the Reissner-Nordström black-hole metric—arising from nonlinear electrodynamics or dark-matter models—affect quasinormal modes (QNMs) and quasibound states (QBSs) of a massive scalar field; it employs multiple analytical and numerical methods (WKB, global matching, Leaver, time-domain), validates consistency across them, quantifies approximation errors, and demonstrates QNM–QBS orthogonality in a relativistic bilinear framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how long-range metric deformations—specifically logarithmic deviations from RN geometry, motivated by nonlinear electrodynamics and perfect-fluid dark-matter models—affect the dynamics (oscillation, trapping, decay) of massive scalar fields around black holes, particularly beyond standard Schwarzschild or RN paradigms.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of scalar perturbations on a logarithmically deformed RN metric: (i) first- and sixth-order WKB for QNMs; (ii) global matching and generalized Leaver method (adapted to logarithmic asymptotics) for both QNMs and QBSs; (iii) time-domain evolution for validation; (iv) Padé and Gamow tunneling approximations for binding energy and damping; (v) relativistic bilinear current formalism with horizon regularization and complex radial contour for orthogonality verification.

Result: Small scalar mass shifts and heightens the effective potential barrier, reducing curvature and weakening QNM damping at fixed multipole/overtone; all QNM methods agree within validity ranges; global matching and Leaver yield closely aligned QBS spectra; Padé gives ~0.04% binding-energy error, Gamow underestimates damping by ~25%; increased scalar mass or nonlinear coupling enhances binding but reduces QBS lifetime due to greater horizon absorption; QNM–QBS orthogonality is rigorously demonstrated via a conserved bilinear current.

Conclusion: Logarithmic long-range deformations significantly alter scalar-field dynamics near black holes, with robust, cross-validated predictions for QNMs and QBSs; the study establishes methodological benchmarks for non-asymptotically-flat or slowly decaying geometries and provides a framework for testing exotic matter models through gravitational-wave or bosonic cloud signatures.

Abstract: We study how long-range deformations in a black-hole metric affect the oscillation, trapping, and decay of a neutral, minimally coupled massive scalar field. We choose a logarithmic deformation of Reissner-Nordström (RN) sourced by nonlinear electrodynamics. It provides an exact solution of Einstein gravity and admits a finite completion. The same geometry also arises in a phenomenological perfect-fluid dark-matter model. In this setup, we analyze both quasinormal modes (QNMs), which describe relaxation, and quasibound states (QBSs), which describe trapped modes with small horizon absorption. We first show that a small scalar mass moves the potential barrier outward, raises it, and reduces its curvature. First-order WKB then predicts weaker damping at every fixed finite multipole and overtone. We then compare several approaches, namely sixth-order WKB, global matching, a generalized Leaver method (adapted to the logarithmic geometry) and time-domain evolution. All methods yield consistent QNM estimates within their ranges of validity. Global matching and Leaver also agree closely for QBSs. Further analytic estimates with a reference trapped state show that a local Padé approximation gives a binding-energy error of about $0.04\%$, while leading Gamow tunnelling underestimates damping by about $25\%$. Across the sampled QBSs, increasing the scalar mass or nonlinear coupling strengthens binding but shortens the lifetime through greater horizon absorption. Finally, using an appropriate horizon regularization and a common complex radial contour, we demonstrate orthogonality of an independently computed QNM-QBS pair using a suitable relativistic bilinear framework.

</details>


### [87] [Gravitational Radiation Coupled to a real massive scalar Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33928)
*Yue Ma,Xianghao Wu,Weidong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the long-term behavior of small-data global solutions to the Einstein--Klein--Gordon system with a positive-mass scalar field in wave gauge, proving an optimal $t^{-1}$ decay rate for the linearized trace of the metric perturbation and establishing a quantitative observability estimate linking initial Klein-Gordon energy to that leading-order coefficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the asymptotic stability and decay structure of coupled gravitational and massive scalar field dynamics in general relativity, particularly in wave gauge where gauge-dependent features affect pointwise decay.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of small-data global solutions using wave gauge formalism; derivation of optimal pointwise decay rates via energy estimates and linearization; proof of a one-point observability inequality connecting initial data to asymptotic coefficients.

Result: Optimal $\hat t^{-1} \simeq t^{-1}$ decay rate for the linearized trace component of the metric perturbation; a quantitative one-point observability estimate showing control of initial Klein-Gordon energy by the leading-order asymptotic coefficient.

Conclusion: The Einstein--Klein--Gordon system with positive scalar mass exhibits sharp $t^{-1}$ decay for the trace mode in wave gauge, and the initial scalar field energy is fully encoded in this leading asymptotic coefficient—demonstrating strong coupling between geometry and matter at null infinity.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of small-data global solutions to the Einstein--Klein--Gordon system with a positive-mass real scalar field in wave gauge. We show that the linearized trace component of the metric perturbation admits a $\hat t^{-1}\simeq t^{-1}$ optimal decreasing rate. Moreover, a quantitative one-point observability estimate is established: the initial Klein-Gordon energy is controlled by this leading order coefficient.

</details>


### [88] [Comment on "Effect of perfect fluid dark matter..." [Phys. Dark Univ. 34 (2021) 100891]](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34019)
*Ming-Hsun Li,Kwei-Chou Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This comment critiques the physical interpretation of a black hole spacetime metric used by Shaymatov et al., clarifying that it represents a dual-component system (cold dark matter + phantom field), not a single-component perfect fluid dark matter with w = −1, which would contradict gravitational attraction requirements for dark matter halos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct the misinterpretation of a black hole spacetime metric as a single-component Perfect Fluid Dark Matter (PFDM) model, and to prevent its misuse in extreme astrophysical contexts.

Method: Conceptual and theoretical analysis of the metric's origin (Li & Yang, 2012), its physical decomposition into cold dark matter and phantom field components, and examination of equation-of-state consistency with gravitational clustering requirements.

Result: Demonstration that assigning a total equation of state w_total = −1 to the metric is physically inconsistent with dark matter halo formation; clarification of distinct parameter regimes in the original dual-component model.

Conclusion: The metric cannot be interpreted as a viable single-component PFDM model; proper treatment requires retaining its dual-component nature—cold dark matter plus phantom field—and respecting their separate physical roles and parameter constraints.

Abstract: This Comment clarifies the physical interpretation of the black hole spacetime metric employed by Shaymatov \textit{et al.} [Phys. Dark Univ. 34 (2021) 100891]. We point out that the metric, originally derived in Li and Yang (2012), describes a specific dual-component system consisting of cold dark matter and a phantom field background. Treating this geometry as a single-component ``Perfect Fluid Dark Matter'' (PFDM) with a total equation of state $w_{\mathrm{total}} = -1$ is physically incompatible with the gravitational attraction required for dark matter halos. Furthermore, we clarify the distinct parameter regimes of the original model to prevent potential misapplications in extreme astrophysical scenarios.

</details>


### [89] [Repetitive Magnetic Penrose Process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34164)
*Ke Wang,Xin-Yun Hu,Xiao-Xiong Zeng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the nonlinear iterative repetitive Penrose process to the magnetic Penrose process in the Kerr spacetime with a Wald magnetic field, showing that charge accumulation via charged particle decay leads to monotonic black hole charging, which eventually neutralizes magnetic energy gain and terminates the magnetic process, reverting it to the standard mechanical Penrose process and capping efficiency below 100%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the magnetic Penrose process can sustain repeated, super-efficient energy extraction (>100%) as suggested by single-event analyses, and to understand the role of black hole charge dynamics in limiting such extraction.

Method: Analytical relativistic astrophysics: employs Kerr metric with Wald magnetic field; treats electromagnetic field and particle charge as test fields/charges; derives optimal equatorial energy extraction conditions; models iterative particle decay with charge conservation and feedback-driven black hole charging; tracks evolution of monopole voltage and its sign reversal.

Result: Charging feedback causes black hole charge Q to asymptote to ~Q_Wald/2; monopole voltage V ∝ −Q crosses zero, nullifying magnetic contribution; magnetic Penrose process terminates, reducing to mechanical Penrose process; energy extraction efficiency is capped below 100%, invalidating sustained super-efficiency.

Conclusion: The repetitive magnetic Penrose process is self-limiting due to charge accumulation and consequent magnetic neutralization; thus, the theoretically predicted >100% efficiency from a single magnetic Penrose event cannot be sustained iteratively.

Abstract: The nonlinear iterative repetitive Penrose process established recently by Ruffini et al.\ is generalized to the magnetic Penrose process. The spacetime background is the Kerr metric, the external magnetic field is described by the Wald solution, the electromagnetic field is treated as a test field, and the charge is treated as a test charge. We analyze the optimal conditions for maximum energy extraction on the equatorial plane and find that under these conditions the core mechanism of the iterative process is charging feedback. After charging, the black hole charge is approximately half of the Wald charge. When the neutral parent particle decays, charge conservation forces the infalling fragment to carry charge while the other fragment carries the opposite charge, so the black hole charge accumulates monotonically. The monopole voltage of the Wald solution decreases linearly with the charge $Q$ and crosses zero to reverse sign at a certain point, so the magnetic gain is gradually neutralized by the charge. The repetitive magnetic Penrose process is therefore forced to terminate and eventually falls back to the mechanical Penrose process. The energy extraction efficiency $>100\%$ predicted by the single magnetic Penrose process will therefore be prevented.

</details>


### [90] [Finite-Distance Imaging of Relativistic Equilibrium Tori around Genuine Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34331)
*Hao-Peng Yan,Xiang-Qian Li,Xiao-Jun Yue*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes finite-distance images of pressure-supported tori around Kerr–Bertotti–Robinson black holes using GR ray tracing with gray emission/absorption, analyzing how magnetic field strength, torus filling, viewing inclination, self-absorption, and black hole spin affect image brightness, size, contrast, and asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how electromagnetic field strength, black hole spin, viewing geometry, and radiative transfer jointly shape observable features (brightness, size, asymmetry) of magnetized accretion tori at finite distance — beyond idealized infinite-distance or optically thin approximations.

Method: General relativistic ray tracing in Kerr–Bertotti–Robinson spacetime; gray (frequency-independent) emission and absorption; torus modeled as stationary, pressure-supported fluid bounded by constant-specific-angular-momentum equilibrium surface; observer fixed at r = 50M.

Result: Integrated brightness and apparent size increase strongly with external magnetic field; higher filling boosts brightness 5.9× but expands image size only 10%; larger inclination enhances lateral brightness contrast; stronger self-absorption coexists with higher absolute brightness due to coupled scaling; prograde torus brightness is nearly spin-insensitive (±2%), while retrograde dims ~7× from a = 0.5 to a = 0.95 due to equilibrium contraction.

Conclusion: Finite-distance torus images are shaped by the interplay of magnetically modified equilibrium structure, frame-dragging–dependent fluid motion, and optical depth — making brightness, scale, and asymmetry highly sensitive to spin orientation, field strength, and viewing angle.

Abstract: We calculate finite-distance images of stationary, pressure-supported tori around genuine, electrically neutral Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson black holes using general relativistic ray tracing with gray emission and absorption. The emitting volume is bounded by a constant-specific-angular-momentum equilibrium free surface, and the observer is placed at a radius of $50M$. Along the investigated external-field sequence, integrated brightness and apparent size both increase strongly as the field modifies the equilibrium source and photon propagation. Higher torus filling has a different effect: in a representative sequence, brightness grows by a factor of $5.9$ while the intensity-weighted image size increases by only $10\%$. Increasing viewing inclination enhances lateral brightness contrast. Stronger self-absorption can coexist with higher absolute brightness because emission and absorption scale together in the adopted gray prescription. At common pressure center and filling, increasing the dimensionless spin from $0.5$ to $0.95$ dims the retrograde sequence by a factor of about $7$ as its equilibrium contracts, whereas prograde brightness changes by only $2\%$. These contrasting responses show how the equilibrium boundary, fluid motion, and optical depth jointly shape the brightness, scale, and asymmetry of finite-distance torus images.

</details>


### [91] [Topological analysis of novel charged-AdS black hole solutions embedded in dark matter halos with quintessence field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34334)
*Muhammad Yasir,Hafiza Bushra Mujahid,Faisal Javed,Shahid Qaisar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a charged AdS black hole embedded in a NGC 4649-type dark matter halo and a quintessence field with variable equation of state; it derives modified black hole solutions, analyzes horizon structure and asymptotics, and investigates thermodynamics—including topology, criticality, and stability—using Duan-φ-mapping and generalized free energy, revealing quintessence’s decisive role in phase behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how realistic astrophysical environments—specifically galactic-scale dark matter halos (NGC 4649-type) and dynamical quintessence—affect the geometry, horizon structure, and thermodynamic phase behavior of charged AdS black holes, beyond standard vacuum or cosmological-constant-only models.

Method: Solves modified Einstein field equations incorporating electric charge, negative cosmological constant, spherically symmetric NGC 4649 dark matter density profile, and quintessence with variable equation-of-state parameter ω_q; computes metric function and horizon radii; applies Duan-φ-mapping topological theory and generalized free energy formalism to analyze thermodynamic topology, critical points, and stability.

Result: Dark matter enhances gravitational attraction (reducing outer horizon radius), while quintessence induces repulsion and modifies asymptotics; thermodynamic analysis reveals ω_q-dependent critical behavior, phase transitions, and topological defects (e.g., vortices) in the order parameter space; fractional shifts in ω_q systematically alter critical temperature, specific heat divergence, and stability windows.

Conclusion: The interplay among charge, AdS curvature, dark matter halo, and dynamical quintessence yields rich geometric and thermodynamic phenomenology; quintessence is not merely a background but a tunable control parameter for black hole criticality and phase structure, suggesting observational signatures in strong-gravity regimes influenced by dark sector physics.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the properties of a charged anti-de Sitter (AdS) black hole in the presence of a NGC 4649-type dark matter halo and a surrounding quintessence field. Starting from the modified Einstein field equations (EFEs), we construct the corresponding black hole solutions by incorporating the effects of electric charge, cosmological constant, dark matter distribution, and quintessence characterized by a variable equation of state. We analyze the resulting spacetime geometry through the metric function and horizon structure, showing that the dark matter halo strengthens the gravitational attraction, whereas the quintessence field provides a repulsive contribution that substantially alters the asymptotic behavior of the spacetime. Furthermore, the thermodynamic properties are investigated by applying the Duan-\(\phi\)-mapping topological theory together with the generalized free energy method. This framework allows us to examine the topological structure, critical behavior, and stability conditions of the black hole system in a consistent manner. We further study the fractional shifts relative to the reference value of \(\omega_q\) and observe that variations in \(\omega_q\) produce systematic changes which highlight the important role of the quintessence field in determining the phase structure and critical behavior of the black hole system.

</details>


### [92] [Relativistic diffusing particles, entropy and varying cosmological constants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34341)
*Z. Haba*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores Einstein equations with a non-conserved energy-momentum tensor, incorporating spacetime-dependent Newton constant G and cosmological term Λ; it links energy-momentum non-conservation to thermodynamic quantities (temperature, entropy) and models diffusing massive/massless particles in an expanding FLRW universe, deriving time-dependent barotropic equations of state and asymptotic behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate gravitational dynamics when energy-momentum is not covariantly conserved—motivated by physical scenarios like particle diffusion—and to relate such non-conservation to spacetime variation of fundamental constants (G, Λ) and thermodynamics.

Method: Covariant analysis of modified Einstein equations with $\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu} = 8\pi G T^{\mu\nu} - g^{\mu\nu}\Lambda + \Sigma^{\mu\nu}$; derivation of constraint from $\nabla_\mu \tilde{T}^{\mu\nu} = 0$; thermodynamic interpretation via first law; kinetic modeling of diffusing particles in FLRW background; analytic solution for massless relativistic gas in flat expanding universe.

Result: A consistency relation linking $\nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}$ to $\partial_\mu G$, $\partial_\mu \Lambda$, and $\Sigma^{\mu\nu}$; identification of $\nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}$ with thermodynamic fluxes involving $T$ and $S$; emergence of time-dependent linear barotropic EOS $P_t = w_t \rho_t$ with $0 < w_t \leq 1/3$; asymptotic transition from massive to massless diffusing gas; explicit solutions for $\rho(t)$, $S(t)$, and phase-space distribution in the massless soluble model.

Conclusion: Energy-momentum non-conservation arises naturally from diffusion processes and induces effective spacetime dependence of $G$ and $\Lambda$; the framework unifies gravitational, thermodynamic, and kinetic descriptions, yielding physically viable cosmological evolution with emergent barotropic behavior and well-defined entropy dynamics.

Abstract: We discuss Einstein equations with a non-conserved energy-momentum $T^{\mu\nu}$. The energy-momentum tensor $\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}$ on the right hand side of Einstein equations is expressed in the form $8\pi GT^{\mu\nu}-g^{\mu\nu}\Lambda+\Sigma^{\mu\nu}$ where $G$ is the Newton constant, $\Lambda$ is the cosmological term and $T^{\mu\nu}$ is the energy-momentum of an ideal fluid. The remaining part $\Sigma^{\mu\nu}$ describes the viscosity and a heat flow. We discuss space-time-dependent Newton ``constant" $G$ and the cosmological term $\Lambda$. The covariant conservation law for $\tilde{T}^{\mu\nu}$ imposes a relation between the energy-momentum (non)conservation and the variation of $G$ and $\Lambda$. The covariant derivative $\nabla_{\mu}T^{\mu\nu}$ can be related to the first law of thermodynamics and expressed by the temperature $T$ and the entropy $S$. The energy-momentum non-conservation in our models is a consequence of a diffusion of its constituting particles. We describe a gas of diffusing massive particles in a homogeneous isotropic space-time as a system with a time-dependent linear barotropic equation of state $P_{t}=w_{t}\rho_{t}$ with $0<w_{t}\leq \frac{1}{3}$. The gas of massive diffusing particles in an expanding universe tends in a limit of infinite time to the gas of diffusing massless particles. We obtain results on the energy density, entropy and phase space dstribution in a soluble model of massless relativistic particles in a spatially flat expanding universe.

</details>


### [93] [Geometric criticality and regular quasibound spectra of axial Proca fields in Schwarzschild spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34528)
*Antonio Sousa Ribeiro,Francisco Artur Pinheiro Alves Junior*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the relationship between a geometric critical point in the odd-parity Proca potential and the global quasibound spectrum in Schwarzschild spacetime, deriving exact fold-scaling laws near critical coupling and demonstrating that spectral branches remain nondegenerate despite geometric potential degeneracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how local geometric features (e.g., merging extrema) of the Proca axial potential influence the global quasibound mode spectrum—specifically whether such potential criticality induces spectral degeneracy or merely governs scaling behavior.

Method: Analytical derivation of fold-scaling relations near the critical coupling $\mu_c$ using normalization and perturbation in $\delta = 1 - (M\mu)^2/(M\mu_c)^2$; numerical validation via continued-fraction and Riccati shooting methods for axial quasibound modes with $\ell=1,2,3$.

Result: Exact scalings: stationary-point separation $\propto \delta^{1/2}$, barrier-well depth $\propto \delta^{3/2}$, curvature $\propto \delta^{1/2}$; quasibound frequencies show regular continuation and near-linear behavior across $\mu_c$; radial operator is affine in $\delta$, supporting analyticity; no spectral degeneracy observed despite geometric criticality.

Conclusion: Geometric criticality of the Proca axial potential (merging extrema at $r=6M$) governs universal fold-scaling but does not induce spectral degeneracy—quasibound branches remain distinct and analytically continuable, establishing a clear separation between potential geometry and spectral structure.

Abstract: We investigate how a local geometric critical point of the odd-parity Proca potential relates to the global quasibound spectrum in Schwarzschild spacetime. After normalization by $\mu^2$, the axial potential $V_{\rm ax}=(1-2M/r)[\ell(\ell+1)/r^2+\mu^2]$ has the same radial form as the timelike-geodesic effective potential. Its outer minimum and inner maximum merge at $r=6M$, the Schwarzschild innermost stable circular orbit, when $(M\mu_c)^2=\ell(\ell+1)/12$. With $\delta=1-(M\mu)^2/(M\mu_c)^2$, we derive exact fold scalings: stationary-point separation $\propto\delta^{1/2}$, barrier--well depth $\propto\delta^{3/2}$, and curvature magnitudes $\propto\delta^{1/2}$. Continued-fraction calculations for the first three axial quasibound branches with $\ell=1,2,3$, cross-checked for selected modes by independent Riccati shooting, show regular frequency continuation across the threshold and approximately linear near-critical behavior. At fixed radius the radial operator is affine in $\delta$, supporting a conditional simple-root analytic interpretation. The main result is the explicit distinction between geometric potential criticality and spectral degeneracy for the branches examined.

</details>


### [94] [Existence of static axisymmetric solutions to the Einstein equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34555)
*Philippe G. LeFloch,Luc Rozoy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Constructs smooth static Einstein metrics with positive cosmological constant on a 3-ball minus two disjoint open balls, modeling two small, comparable black holes with controlled horizon areas and separation; solves the reduced Einstein equation via weighted Hölder estimates and obstruction cancellation by axial position variation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct explicit, physically meaningful static solutions of the Einstein equations with positive cosmological constant that describe multiple black holes (two horizons) in a compact setting, overcoming analytical difficulties arising from shrinking horizons and nonlinear coupling.

Method: Uses weighted Hölder space analysis to control the linearized static Einstein operator near degenerating horizons; constructs an approximate solution by gluing Kottler (Schwarzschild–de Sitter) collars to de Sitter space; removes obstructions via variation of two axial positions; enforces the contracted Bianchi identity and employs an energy estimate for an auxiliary Killing vector field induced by the circle symmetry.

Result: Existence of smooth, circle-invariant static Einstein metrics with positive cosmological constant on a compact 3-manifold with three boundary components (two inner black hole horizons and one outer cosmological horizon); horizon areas scale linearly with individual masses, inter-horizon separation scales with cube root of total mass; solution is unique up to isometry and rescaling of the lapse when one horizon’s intrinsic metric is prescribed.

Conclusion: A family of multi-black-hole static solutions to the Einstein equations with Λ > 0 exists in dimension three under small-mass and scale-separation assumptions; the construction demonstrates robustness of elliptic PDE methods in geometric relativity with degenerating geometries and provides a rigorous bridge between localized black hole models and global de Sitter-like structure.

Abstract: We construct smooth Riemannian metrics and scalar potentials that are invariant under a circle action and satisfy the static Einstein equations with positive cosmological constant. The underlying compact three-manifold is a ball with two disjoint open balls removed. The potential (or lapse function) is positive in the interior and vanishes at the boundary, consisting of three non-degenerate horizons. The areas of the two inner boundary components are controlled by two arbitrary parameters, namely the masses of the black holes, which are taken to be sufficiently small and comparable. Each inner horizon radius scales linearly with the individual mass parameters, while the separation of their reference centers scales as the cube root of the total mass. These two scales are assumed to be sufficiently small at different rates, whereas the cosmological length remains fixed. The main analytical challenge is to control the linearized equation and the nonlinear remainder simultaneously near the shrinking horizons, between the centers and in the outer region. Weighted Hölder estimates quantify the growth of the inverse and show that the error of an approximation joining Kottler collars to de Sitter space is small enough to close a contraction argument. Namely, we solve a reduced elliptic Einstein equation that is endowed with two obstruction tensors; varying the two axial positions removes their coefficients. The contracted Bianchi identity and an energy estimate for an auxiliary vector field (Killing field) introduced by the projection to vanish. Finally, for a fixed cosmological constant, the intrinsic metric of one of the two boundary components is sufficient to determine a static solution with simply connected interior and non-vanishing boundary gradient, up to isometry and multiplication of the potential by a positive constant.

</details>


### [95] [Exact spinning wormholes in five dimensions from Toda integrability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34672)
*Masato Nozawa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a new exact rotating wormhole solution in five-dimensional Einstein-phantom-scalar gravity, generalizing the Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole; it leverages an integrable $A_1 \oplus A_2$ Toda structure to obtain closed-form solutions, enforce regularity and asymptotic flatness, and rigorously establish key geometric and causal properties—including geodesic completeness, absence of horizons/singularities, stable causality, uniqueness of the throat, angular momentum bounds, and ergosurface existence—while recovering extremal Myers-Perry black hole geometry in a scalar-vanishing limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and rigorously characterize a physically viable rotating wormhole solution in higher-dimensional gravity coupled to a phantom scalar field—addressing open questions on regularity, causality, uniqueness, and limiting behavior—beyond static or lower-dimensional analogs.

Method: Imposes cohomogeneity-one symmetry (equal angular momenta) on the 5D Einstein-phantom-scalar system; reduces radial field equations to an integrable $A_1 \oplus A_2$ Toda system; solves it in closed form; analytically enforces asymptotic flatness, regularity at the throat, and global causal conditions; derives geometric quantities (ergosurface, throat area, angular momentum bound) from the explicit metric and scalar field.

Result: A three-parameter family of exact, geodesically complete, horizonless, singularity-free, stably causal rotating wormhole solutions in 5D; proof of unique wormhole throat; sharp upper bound $J \leq \sqrt{3} \, A_{\text{throat}}/(8\pi)$ for equal angular momenta; existence and uniqueness of an ergosurface asymmetric between asymptotic ends; recovery of extremal Myers-Perry black hole exterior in the vanishing-scalar limit.

Conclusion: The integrable Toda structure provides a powerful analytical framework for constructing and controlling nontrivial rotating wormhole geometries in higher dimensions; the resulting solutions are fully regular and satisfy all standard physical viability criteria, offering a concrete alternative to black holes with distinct global and causal features.

Abstract: We construct a new exact solution to the five-dimensional Einstein-phantom-scalar system that describes a rotating wormhole connecting two asymptotically flat regions. The solution represents a rotating generalization of the Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole with equal angular momenta. Under the cohomogeneity-one ansatz, we demonstrate that the radial equations reduce to an integrable $A_1\oplus A_2$ Toda system, from which the general local solution can be obtained in closed form. This integrable structure enables us to implement the regularity and asymptotic-flatness conditions analytically, thereby singling out a three-parameter family of solutions characterized by the mass, the equal angular momenta, and the scalar charge. The resulting spacetimes are geodesically complete, horizonless and stably causal, and contain no curvature singularities. The Toda structure further allows us to prove the uniqueness of the wormhole throat and to derive a sharp upper bound on the angular momentum for a fixed throat area. We also establish the existence and uniqueness of an ergosurface with respect to the stationary frame that is nonrotating at one asymptotic end, while the opposite end rotates relative to this frame. In a suitable scaling limit, the scalar field vanishes and the solution approaches the exterior region of the extremal Myers-Perry black hole with equal angular momenta.

</details>


### [96] [The $C^0$-inextendibility of Kerr spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35012)
*Bobby Eka Gunara*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves C^0-inextendibility of full Kerr spacetimes (subextremal, extremal, and overextremal) with positive mass and nonzero angular momentum, showing that the ring singularity acts as a generic causal boundary due to unbounded proper time in fixed-endpoint causal homotopy classes—robust under conformal rescalings and causal cone widening.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, geometrically intrinsic obstruction to extending Kerr spacetimes across the ring singularity, independent of global causality assumptions or specific coordinate choices, thereby strengthening the understanding of strong cosmic censorship and singularity structure in rotating black holes.

Method: Uses causal homotopy theory and Lorentzian geometry; constructs fixed-endpoint causal homotopy classes where proper time diverges near the ring singularity; analyzes behavior under continuous conformal rescalings and causal cone deformations; derives sharp coordinate-norm bounds for stationary-axisymmetric perturbations; studies timelike geodesics approaching high-curvature regions.

Result: C^0-inextendibility is proven for all full Kerr realizations and their connected coverings across all mass–spin regimes (subextremal, extremal, overextremal); the obstruction is encoded in unbounded proper time in a causal homotopy class, persisting under arbitrary positive conformal rescalings and causal cone widenings; sharp coordinate bounds for perturbations are established; explicit complete timelike geodesics encountering arbitrarily large curvature near the ring are constructed.

Conclusion: The ring singularity in Kerr spacetimes is a genuine C^0-boundary—not an artifact of coordinates or low regularity—and its inextendibility is robust, topological-causal in nature, supporting the physical inevitability of singularities in generic rotating black hole interiors.

Abstract: We prove the $C^0$-inextendibility of the full Kerr realizations considered in this paper and all their connected covering spacetimes. The result holds for positive mass and nonzero rotation in the subextremal, extremal, and overextremal regimes. The obstruction at the singular ring is expressed by unbounded proper time in a fixed-endpoint causal homotopy class. It persists under every positive continuous conformal rescaling and under continuous widening of the Kerr causal cones, without any global causality assumption. We give sharp bounds in a fixed coordinate norm for stationary and axisymmetric perturbations and describe complete timelike geodesics that encounter arbitrarily large curvature near the ring.

</details>


### [97] [Quasinormal modes of thick branes in $f(R,T)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35096)
*Yu-Peng E,Hai-Long Jia,Chun-Chun Zhu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates tensor perturbations and quasinormal modes (QNMs) of thick branes in $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$ gravity, deriving a Schrödinger-like equation, identifying a double-barrier effective potential dependent on parameters $b$ and $\alpha$, and computing QNMs via time-domain and direct integration methods—showing that $f(R,T)$ modifications significantly alter the potential and QNM spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how $f(R,T)$ gravity modifications—specifically $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$—affect gravitational stability and dynamics of thick branes via tensor perturbations and quasinormal modes, potentially yielding observable imprints of modified gravity in braneworld scenarios.

Method: Derivation of the Schrödinger-like equation for tensor (gravitational) perturbations around thick brane backgrounds in $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$ gravity; construction and analysis of the effective potential; computation of transmission spectra; extraction of quasinormal modes using both time-domain evolution and direct integration of the perturbation equation.

Result: The effective potential exhibits a parameter-dependent double-barrier structure ($b$ controls barrier width, $\alpha$ controls height); resonance peaks in transmission spectra align with real parts of QNMs; low-lying QNMs are robustly extracted by both numerical methods, while higher overtones suffer from strong damping and numerical contamination.

Conclusion: Modifications from $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$ gravity substantially reshape the effective potential and quasinormal spectrum of thick branes, suggesting that braneworld QNMs could serve as probes for $f(R,T)$ gravity effects.

Abstract: We study tensor perturbations and quasinormal modes (QNMs) of thick branes in $f(R,T)=R+\alpha T$ gravity. Starting from the background thick brane solutions, we derive the Schrödinger-like equation for gravitational perturbations and construct the corresponding effective potential. The effective potential exhibits a double-barrier structure whose properties depend on the model parameters. We analyze transmission spectra and find good agreement between resonance peaks and the real parts of the quasinormal frequencies. The parameters $b$ and $\alpha$ mainly control the width and height of the potential barriers, respectively. QNMs are extracted using both time-domain evolution and direct integration methods, showing consistent results for low-lying modes. Higher overtones become increasingly difficult to extract due to stronger damping and numerical contamination. Our results indicate that $f(R,T)$ corrections significantly affect both the effective potential and the quasinormal spectrum, providing a possible imprint of modified gravity in braneworld perturbations.

</details>


### [98] [An asymptotically conical regular black hole in four-dimensional nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35248)
*Jia-Zhou Liu,Shao-Wen Wei,Yu-Xiao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an asymptotically conical regular black hole in 4D nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity, using a nonlinear deformation of a linear reduced function to obtain an exact solution with a de Sitter core and finite curvature invariants; thermodynamics—including entropy with rational and logarithmic contributions—is consistently derived via Hamiltonian and Wald formalisms, and the first law holds for both the deformed and original solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically viable regular black hole that is asymptotically conical (rather than asymptotically flat or (A)dS), avoids curvature singularities, and admits well-defined thermodynamics within a higher-curvature gravity framework beyond general relativity.

Method: Starts from a linear reduced function yielding an asymptotically conical Schwarzschild-like solution in nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity; introduces a controlled nonlinear deformation preserving the leading asymptotic conical structure while generating an exact regular black hole with a de Sitter core; computes conserved charges, entropy, and thermodynamic potentials using both the Hamiltonian (canonical) and Wald (covariant phase space) formalisms.

Result: An exact, asymptotically conical, singularity-free black hole solution with finite curvature invariants and a de Sitter core; explicit expressions for mass, temperature, and entropy—showing rational plus logarithmic corrections in the regular model; verification that both the original and deformed solutions satisfy the first law of black hole thermodynamics.

Conclusion: Asymptotically conical regular black holes are realizable in nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity; their thermodynamics is robust across distinct formalisms and retains the first law, supporting the physical consistency of such exotic asymptotics and singularity resolution mechanisms.

Abstract: In this work, we construct an asymptotically conical regular black hole in four-dimensional nonpolynomial quasitopological gravity. Starting from a linear choice of the reduced function, which yields an asymptotically conical Schwarzschild-like solution, we introduce a nonlinear deformation that preserves the leading asymptotic cone while producing an exact black hole solution with a de Sitter core and finite curvature invariants. We further establish the corresponding black hole thermodynamics for both solutions within the Hamiltonian and Wald frameworks. The entropy contains rational and logarithmic terms in the regular model, and both solutions satisfy the first law of black hole thermodynamics.

</details>


### [99] [Bargmann-invariant witness of the graviton commutator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35253)
*Hiroki Matsui*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies the Bargmann invariant phase in matter-wave interferometry as a unique quantum-gravity signature, isolating a commutator phase that is state-independent, Newtonian-interaction-free, and unreplicable by any classical gravitational field—even Wigner-positive ones—thereby providing a rigorous test for gravity's quantum nature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To find an observable signature of quantum gravity that cannot be mimicked by any classical (even stochastic or branchwise-definite) gravitational field, overcoming limitations of decoherence-based tests.

Method: Analyzes the interferometric coherence factorization for matter linearly coupled to linearized gravity; isolates the Bargmann invariant phase as the sole carrier of the graviton commutator contribution; compares reproducibility under classical vs. quantum gravity assumptions, including Wigner-positive graviton states.

Result: The Bargmann phase exactly isolates a commutator term independent of the graviton state and Newtonian interactions; it is bounded by which-path decoherence and is fundamentally nonclassical; while undetectable for real gravity due to extreme weakness (~60 orders below current sensitivity), it is testable in trapped-ion analog experiments at order unity.

Conclusion: Decoherence alone cannot certify quantum gravity; the Bargmann invariant phase provides a necessary and sufficient operational signature—nonreproducible by any classical gravitational field—and establishes a feasible pathway to experimental verification via quantum-simulation analogs.

Abstract: We identify the phase of a Bargmann invariant of two matter-wave interferometers as a quantum gravity signature that cannot be reproduced by any branchwise-definite classical gravitational field. For matter coupled linearly to linearized gravity, the interferometric coherence factorizes exactly into a conservative Newtonian phase, the characteristic functional of the graviton state, and a commutator phase. For Wigner-positive graviton states, the first two are classically reproducible; therefore, decoherence alone cannot certify that gravity is quantized. The Bargmann phase isolates the commutator, which is independent of the graviton state and of all Newtonian interactions and is bounded by which-path decoherence. For gravity, this radiation-reaction effect is sixty orders of magnitude below experimental reach, but trapped-ion analogs can test it at order unity.

</details>


### [100] [From Specific Heat Bounds to Penrose-like Inequalities: A Unified Thermodynamic Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35264)
*Kai-Peng Lu,H. Lu,Run-Qiu Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies a local-to-global thermodynamic mechanism unifying several black hole inequalities (e.g., Penrose-like) under physically motivated assumptions, focusing on static, spherically symmetric and planar symmetric black holes in asymptotically flat and AdS spacetimes; it links local energy conditions (e.g., NEC) to global geometric bounds and holographically implies monotonicity of normalized thermodynamic quantities in the dual field theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify previously disconnected black hole inequalities—especially Penrose-like ones—under a coherent local thermodynamic framework grounded in physical principles (e.g., energy conditions), and to expose structural connections between local constraints and global geometric bounds.

Method: Analytical derivation of thermodynamic inequalities using explicit, physically motivated assumptions—including staticity, spherical or planar symmetry, asymptotic flatness or Schwarzschild-AdS boundary conditions—and application of the Null Energy Condition (NEC); incorporates holographic interpretation via AdS/CFT correspondence.

Result: A central integrated inequality is rigorously established for static planar symmetric asymptotically Schwarzschild-AdS black branes directly under the NEC; unified thermodynamic interpretations are provided for multiple Penrose-type inequalities in both asymptotically flat and AdS settings; holographically, these imply monotonic behavior of normalized thermodynamic quantities in the dual field theory.

Conclusion: The paper reveals a structural thermodynamic origin for diverse black hole inequalities, framing them as manifestations of local energy constraints propagating to global geometry; however, generalization beyond the considered symmetry classes remains open.

Abstract: Under explicit and physically motivated assumptions, we identify a local-to-global thermodynamic mechanism that organizes several previously disconnected inequalities on black holes. We first focus on asymptotically flat, static, and spherically-symmetric black holes, and provide a unified thermodynamic perspective on various Penrose-like inequalities. We also discuss the generalizations in asymptotically Schwarzschild anti-de Sitter black holes/branes. In holographic settings, these bounds imply monotonic behavior of normalized thermodynamic quantities in the dual field theory. Notably, one central integrated inequality can be established directly under the NEC in general static planar symmetric asymptotically Schwarzschild anti-de Sitter black branes. Our purpose is not to claim a fully general proof of the proposed local bounds, but to expose a unified structural perspective connecting local thermodynamic constraints to global geometric inequalities. Establishing the local bounds beyond the classes considered here remains an open problem.

</details>


### [101] [Gravitational-Wave Signatures and Periodic Orbits of Charged Particles Around a Five-Dimensional Rotating Black Hole: Comparison with the Kerr Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35386)
*Bahromjon Shokirov,Tursunali Xamidov,Chen Zhou,Sanjar Shaymatov,Chengxun Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates periodic orbits and gravitational wave emission from charged particles orbiting a 5D rotating Myers–Perry black hole in a uniform magnetic field, comparing results with the 4D Kerr case; it finds weaker binding, shallower potential wells, altered orbital frequencies, lower-amplitude and lower-frequency GW signals, and reduced LISA SNR for the 5D case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how higher-dimensional gravity (specifically D=5 Myers–Perry BHs) modifies charged-particle dynamics and gravitational-wave signatures in astrophysically relevant settings—especially under external magnetic fields—and to assess detectability by LISA relative to standard 4D Kerr predictions.

Method: Analytical derivation of equatorial circular and periodic orbits for charged particles around a D=5 rotating Myers–Perry BH in a uniform magnetic field; comparison with Kerr BH; numerical kludge method used to compute gravitational waveforms for extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs); computation of orbital frequencies, potential well depth, energy at ISCO, radiation reaction timescales, and LISA signal-to-noise ratios.

Result: Charged particles are less tightly bound to the D=5 BH (ISCO energy ~0.95–0.99 vs. 0.78–0.94 for Kerr); potential well is several times shallower; periodic orbits lie closer to the separatrix; magnetic field induces frequency inversions (Ω_φ < Ω_r) and looped trajectories; GW signals have longer periods, lower frequencies (~20% reduction), ~18% smaller amplitude; strongest spectral line at 7.9 mHz (vs. 9.9 mHz for Kerr) for βM = 0.02; radiation-reaction lifetimes range from <1 hour to a few days; LISA SNR is 7–31 for D=5 vs. 15–54 for Kerr.

Conclusion: Five-dimensional rotating black holes in magnetic fields yield qualitatively and quantitatively distinct orbital dynamics and gravitational-wave observables compared to their 4D Kerr counterparts—suggesting that EMRI observations with LISA could, in principle, constrain or distinguish higher-dimensional gravity models, though with reduced detectability due to lower SNR.

Abstract: We study periodic orbits of charged particles in the equatorial plane of a five-dimensional ($D=5$) rotating Myers--Perry black hole (BH) immersed in a uniform magnetic field, and the gravitational waves emitted by these orbits in an extreme mass-ratio inspiral. All results are compared with the Kerr BH in the uniform magnetic field. We find that a charged particle is much more weakly bound to the $D=5$ dimensional BH, i.e. its energy at the innermost stable circular orbit is close to unity, $0.95$--$0.99$, compared with $0.78$--$0.94$ for the Kerr BH, and the depth of the potential well is several times smaller. As a result, the periodic orbits of the $D=5$ dimensional BH lie much closer to the separatrix. The magnetic field can make the azimuthal frequency of the orbit smaller than the radial one and produces loops in the orbits far from the BH. Using the numerical kludge method, we compute the gravitational waveforms for a compact object of $10M_\odot$ orbiting a BH of $10^6M_\odot$. At the same magnetic field, the $D=5$ dimensional signal has a longer period, lower frequencies, and an amplitude smaller by about $18\%$. We show that for the magnetic parameter $\beta M=0.02$ the strongest spectral line is at $7.9$\,mHz, compared with $9.9$\,mHz for the Kerr BH. An estimate of the radiation reaction shows that the periodic orbits survive from less than an hour to a few days in the presence of an external magnetic field. Over these lifetimes, the signal-to-noise ratio for LISA is $7$--$31$ for the rotating BH in $D=5$ dimensions, compared to the Kerr BH where it is about $15$--$54$.

</details>


### [102] [Phase-Space Constraints on a General Class of Eckart-like Viscous Cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35405)
*David R. Moreno,J. Bayron Orjuela-Quintana,Yeinzon Rodriguez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes viscous dark matter cosmologies using dynamical systems methods, showing that viable background evolution closely mimics ΛCDM—requiring negligible viscosity at early times and converging to a cosmological constant-dominated attractor late-time—thus failing to resolve the H₀ tension; however, bulk viscosity introduces scale-dependent damping of matter perturbations, suppressing structure growth on sub-horizon scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether bulk viscosity in dark matter can reconcile cosmological tensions (e.g., H₀) or alter structure formation, while maintaining consistency with standard cosmological epochs (radiation, matter, dark-energy domination).

Method: Dynamical system analysis of Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker cosmologies with bulk-viscous dark matter in the Eckart formalism; parameterization of dimensionless viscous pressure as Ω_Π = −ξ̂₀ Ωₘˢ; classification of fixed points, stability analysis, and examination of physical viability (e.g., positivity of Ωₘ, reality of Ω_Π).

Result: Viable background evolution necessitates Ω_Π ≈ 0 during radiation and matter eras, and only admits Λ-dominated de Sitter as a stable late-time attractor; power-law models with s < 1 lead to unphysical (negative/complex) densities/pressures; sub-horizon linear perturbations exhibit viscosity-induced suppression of δₘ via enhanced Hubble friction.

Conclusion: Viscous dark matter cannot alleviate the H₀ tension at the background level since it closely tracks ΛCDM expansion, but it significantly modifies linear structure growth through dissipative drag—making it potentially testable via LSS observables (e.g., σ₈, fσ₈, weak lensing).

Abstract: We study the cosmological dynamics of a general class of viscous dark matter models using a dynamical system analysis. In the Eckart formalism, these viscous cosmologies are characterized by a dimensionless bulk viscous pressure $\Omega_\Pi \equiv \Omega_\Pi(\Omega_m)$, where $\Omega_m$ is the matter density parameter. Our analysis demonstrates that a viable early radiation era requires the complete absence of viscosity, a proper matter-dominated epoch demands a small viscous pressure, and the only physically admissible late-time attractor is the cosmological constant-dominated fixed point; consequently, the background expansion must closely track standard $\Lambda$CDM evolution. We validate these general results using the power-law parameterization $\Omega_\Pi = -\hat{\xi}_0 \Omega_m^s$, where $\hat{\xi}_0$ and $s$ are real parameters; for $s \geq 1$ and $\hat{\xi}_0 \ll 1$, the model reproduces the $\Lambda$CDM background, while for $s < 1$ the system encounters a past-time integration boundary where the dark matter density transitions into negative values and its viscous pressure evaluates to nonphysical complex numbers. Since the background expansion essentially duplicates $\Lambda$CDM, the model is unlikely to resolve the $H_0$ tension. However, large differences arise at the linear perturbative level; indeed, on sub-horizon scales, bulk viscosity causes a scale-dependent dissipative drag on the matter density fluctuations; this increases the Hubble friction, offering a channel to suppress structure growth.

</details>


### [103] [Decoupled asymmetric thin-shell wormholes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35407)
*A. López-Hormazábal,Hishan Farfán-Bachiloglu,Kazuharu Bamba,Francisco Tello-Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs reflection-asymmetric thin-shell wormholes by gluing a Schwarzschild exterior to a geometrically deformed interior (a scalar–nonlinear-electrodynamics black hole), with asymmetry encoded solely in the gravitational decoupling sector; it derives exact energy and stability conditions, shows deformation enhances stability by shifting the transition radius inward, extends the model to Kerr geometry with exact spin-dependent junction conditions, and estimates gravitational-wave signatures via a free-precession model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically viable, reflection-asymmetric thin-shell wormholes that avoid exotic matter requirements at the throat by leveraging gravitational decoupling and geometric deformation, while exploring stability, energy conditions, and observational signatures.
