Method: Junction condition formalism for thin shells; minimal geometric deformation via gravitational decoupling (MGD); analysis of null energy conditions (radial null energy, momentum flux); linear stability analysis using perturbative shell dynamics; exact matching for Kerr–deformed spacetimes; free-precession modeling for GW signal estimation.

Result: 1) Total radial null energy (shell + bulk) obeys an exact identity and becomes positive under sufficient deformation; 2) Momentum flux across shell equals bulk’s radial null-energy density — inclusion yields closed-form linear stability condition; 3) Conventional shell response requires bulk violation of radial null energy condition at throat, but deformation enlarges stable parameter space and shifts transition radius inside photon sphere; 4) Kerr extension yields exact spin-dependent junction solution fixing angular momentum partition and rotating-fluid shell behavior; 5) Free-precession model gives characteristic GW frequency scale tied to throat geometry.

Conclusion: Gravitational decoupling enables construction of stable, asymmetric wormholes without fine-tuned exotic matter at the throat; deformation improves stability and moves critical radii inward; the framework generalizes to rotating spacetimes with predictive GW phenomenology.

Abstract: We construct reflection-asymmetric thin-shell wormholes by joining a Schwarzschild exterior to its minimal geometric deformation, which shares the same mass and redshift function, so that the asymmetry is carried entirely by the gravitational-decoupling sector. The deformed side is a black hole sourced by a scalar field coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics, in its branch with a real scalar. We show that the total radial null energy of shell and bulk obeys an exact identity and becomes positive for a sufficiently strong deformation. The momentum flux across the shell equals the radial null-energy density of the bulk, and retaining it yields the linear stability condition in closed form. We prove that stability with a conventional shell response requires the bulk to violate the radial null energy condition at the throat; the deformation instead enlarges the stable domain and moves the transition radius inside the photon sphere. For a stationary extension built on Kerr, the junction is solved exactly in the spin, which fixes how the angular momentum is shared between shell and bulk and where the shell behaves as a rotating fluid. A free-precession model provides an estimate of the gravitational-wave signal of the throat.

</details>


### [104] [Quasibound modes of rotating acoustic black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35493)
*H. S. Vieira,V. B. Bezerra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a spherical-coordinate metric for rotating acoustic black holes and derives exact analytical quasibound state frequencies for scalar perturbations via the Klein–Gordon equation, linking analog horizon physics to experimental observability and gravitational black hole phenomena.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable exact analytical treatment of quasibound states in rotating acoustic black holes—key for understanding wave trapping, stability, and energy confinement—and to bridge theoretical predictions with laboratory realizations of horizon physics.

Method: Constructed a spherical-coordinate metric for rotating acoustic black holes; solved the massless Klein–Gordon equation exactly in that background; imposed physically appropriate boundary conditions (ingoing at effective horizon, exponentially decaying at infinity) to obtain quasibound state frequencies analytically.

Result: An exact closed-form analytical expression for the complex quasibound state frequencies (real part: oscillation frequency; negative imaginary part: decay rate) of scalar perturbations in the rotating acoustic black hole spacetime.

Conclusion: Exact quasibound state solutions are attainable in this acoustic black hole model, confirming their utility as stable, long-lived resonant modes; this strengthens the role of acoustic analogs in testing horizon-related phenomena experimentally and informs parallel studies in gravitational black hole physics.

Abstract: In this paper, we introduce a metric describing rotating acoustic black holes in spherical coordinates. This formulation allows us to obtain exact analytical solutions of the massless Klein--Gordon equation governing scalar perturbations in the corresponding background spacetime. By imposing the appropriate boundary conditions, we derive an exact analytical expression for the quasibound state frequencies of the system. Quasibound states play a central role in the dynamics of black hole analog systems, as they characterize long-lived resonant modes trapped in the vicinity of the effective horizon. Their investigation provides valuable insights into wave propagation, mode stability, and energy confinement in analog spacetimes. Moreover, because acoustic black holes offer experimentally accessible realizations of horizon physics, the study of quasibound states may establish a direct connection between theoretical predictions and laboratory observations, thereby contributing to a deeper understanding of phenomena that also arise in gravitational black hole environments.

</details>


### [105] [Gravitationally Induced Polarization-Angle Rotation in Pulsar-Black Hole Binaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35589)
*Jyotijwal Debnath,Amodio Carleo,Delphine Perrodin,Pratik Tarafdar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper models how gravitational light bending and parallel transport near a black hole companion distort the polarization profile of a radio pulsar, modifying the Rotating Vector Model predictions—especially the S-shaped position angle curve—via general relativistic effects including frame-dragging and spin-orbit coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how strong-field general relativistic effects—specifically gravitational light bending, frame dragging, and parallel transport—in a pulsar–black hole binary alter the observed polarization position angle (PPA), potentially enabling geometric probing of such systems once discovered.

Method: Combines the classical Rotating Vector Model (RVM) with numerical integration of null geodesics in Schwarzschild and Kerr spacetimes; incorporates parallel transport of the polarization vector along photon trajectories and accounts for orbital evolution due to relativistic spin-orbit coupling.

Result: Gravitational light bending significantly distorts the characteristic S-shaped PPA curve—especially near superior conjunction—and can reverse its sense of variation for supermassive BH systems; frame dragging adds an extra geometric shift, while parallel transport induces an additional polarization rotation; spin-orbit coupling modulates the orbital-phase dependence of the PPA.

Conclusion: Relativistic photon propagation strongly modifies both the shape and phase dependence of pulsar polarization profiles in BH binaries, offering a novel probe of binary geometry and spacetime structure when such systems are observed.

Abstract: Radio pulsars emit highly polarized radiation whose polarization properties are commonly described by the Rotating Vector Model (RVM). In this work, we investigate the effects of gravitational light bending and parallel transport on the polarization profile of a pulsar in a hypothetical binary system with a black hole (BH) companion. We combine the RVM with numerical integration of null geodesics to calculate the polarization direction along the photon trajectory and model the orbital-phase-dependent variation of the observed polarization position angle (PPA). We consider both non-spinning and spinning black hole companions and include the evolution of the orbital geometry due to relativistic spin-orbit coupling. For a non-spinning black hole, the change in the observed polarization direction arises from the geometric shift associated with gravitational light bending. For a spinning black hole, frame dragging introduces an additional geometric shift, while parallel transport produces an additional polarization rotation measured relative to our chosen reference construction. We investigate the dependence of these polarization changes on the binary parameters and the orientation of the pulsar spin. We find that gravitational light bending can significantly distort the characteristic S-shaped PPA profile, particularly near superior conjunction, and can reverse its overall sense of variation for the supermassive black hole system considered here. Relativistic spin-orbit evolution also affects the orbital-phase dependence of the polarization profile, particularly in the supermassive black hole system. These results show that relativistic photon propagation can modify both the shape and the phase dependence of pulsar polarization profiles and may provide a useful probe of the geometry of pulsar-BH binaries when such systems are discovered.

</details>


### [106] [Nonlinear Tides of Binary Neutron Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35602)
*Guangzhou Guo,Zhen Pan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates nonlinear tidal effects in neutron-star binary inspirals, showing they significantly shift the fundamental oscillation mode frequency and add ~1 radian of gravitational-wave phase shift late in inspiral—beyond linear predictions—making them detectable by next-generation GW observatories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how nonlinear tidal couplings between stellar oscillations and orbital dynamics affect gravitational wave signals from neutron-star binaries, especially where linear tidal theory fails.

Method: Combines high-resolution hydrodynamic simulations of inspiralling neutron-star binaries with a semi-analytic mode-coupling model to compute second-order quadrupolar f-mode frequency shifts.

Result: Nonlinear tides reduce the fundamental-mode frequency by ~10% in equal-mass binaries during late inspiral and induce an O(1) radian GW phase shift beyond the linear tidal response; simulations and analytic model agree in weak-tide regime but diverge at stronger fields.

Conclusion: Nonlinear tides produce observable imprints on GW waveforms, rendering them a viable target for next-generation detectors such as Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer.

Abstract: Nonlinear tides couple stellar oscillations and reshape the gravitational wave (GW) signal from inspiralling binaries. We present hydrodynamic simulations of nonlinear tides in inspiralling neutron-star binaries and, within a mode-coupling model, derive a semi-analytic quadrupolar fundamental mode-frequency shift through second order in the tidal strength. The two approaches agree in the perturbative regime, but diverge at stronger tidal fields, where perturbation theory breaks down. In the late inspiral, nonlinear tides reduce the fundamental-mode frequency by ${\sim}10\%$ for equal-mass binaries and add a GW phase shift of $\mathcal{O}(1)$ radian beyond the linear tidal response. These effects make nonlinear tides a promising target for next-generation GW detectors.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [107] [Spectroscopy of the SU(2) scalar-fermion-gauge system under the influence of the Brout-Englert-Higgs effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33611)
*Axel Maas,Sofie Martins,Georg Wieland*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies the ground state spectrum of an SU(2) scalar-fermion-gauge system on the lattice, finding mass-independent spectral features consistent with the Fröhlich-Morchio-Strocchi mechanism in a weakly coupled regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the Brout-Englert-Higgs effect shapes the non-perturbative spectrum of a dynamical scalar-fermion-gauge system, particularly whether fermion mass variations affect the physical spectrum beyond trivial shifts.

Method: Lattice field theory simulations using the HiRep code for an SU(2) gauge theory with a Higgs doublet and two degenerate vectorial fermion generations.

Result: The low-energy spectrum is insensitive to bare fermion mass (except for trivial mass-dependent shifts), indicating decoupling of fermion mass from the gauge-scalar dynamics.

Conclusion: The observed spectral insensitivity supports a weakly coupled interpretation where the Fröhlich-Morchio-Strocchi mechanism governs the realization of the Higgs phenomenon, distinct from strong-coupling or technicolor-like scenarios.

Abstract: We investigate the ground state spectrum of a dynamical scalar-fermion-gauge system with an SU(2) gauge group, a scalar doublet, and two degenerate generations of vectorial fermions under the influence of the Brout-Englert-Higgs effect. To this end, we employ lattice methods using the HiRep code. We study the dependence of the system on the (bare) fermion mass and find that the spectrum remains unaffected, up to trivial fermion mass effects. The results are in line with a weakly coupled theory exhibiting the Fröhlich-Morchio-Strocchi mechanism.

</details>


### [108] [Large-$N$ $π^+π^+$ scattering from twisted Eguchi--Kawai reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35176)
*Claudio Bonanno,Margarita García Pérez,Antonio González-Arroyo,Ken-Ichi Ishikawa,Masanori Okawa,Fernando Romero-López*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the π⁺π⁺ scattering length in large-N QCD using the twisted Eguchi–Kawai (TEK) lattice formulation, achieving controlled large-N, continuum, and chiral limits; results agree with NLO Chiral Perturbation Theory and S-matrix bootstrap bounds, and sub-leading 1/N corrections are extracted by combining TEK and standard lattice data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the π⁺π⁺ scattering length in the 't Hooft large-N limit of QCD — a regime where analytical control is limited and non-perturbative lattice validation is scarce — and to test consistency with chiral effective theory and fundamental S-matrix constraints.

Method: Uses the twisted Eguchi–Kawai (TEK) reduction to simulate large-N QCD on the lattice with N up to 1156; performs systematic extrapolations to the large-N, continuum (a → 0), and chiral (m_q → 0) limits; compares scattering length behavior with next-to-leading-order Chiral Perturbation Theory; combines TEK results with prior standard lattice data (N = 3–6) to isolate O(1/N) corrections.

Result: First determination of the π⁺π⁺ scattering length in the large-N ('t Hooft) limit; chiral dependence matches NLO χPT; low-energy coupling governing sub-leading quark-mass dependence satisfies known S-matrix bootstrap bounds; O(1/N) corrections to the scattering length are quantified.

Conclusion: Large-N QCD predictions for ππ scattering are quantitatively consistent with chiral effective theory and fundamental analyticity/unitarity bounds; the TEK approach enables reliable access to the large-N limit, and sub-leading 1/N effects are now empirically constrained.

Abstract: We present the first computation of the $\pi^+\pi^+$ scattering length in large-$N$ QCD in the 't Hooft limit, $N_{\scriptscriptstyle{\rm f}}/N\to 0$, using the twisted Eguchi--Kawai formulation of the lattice theory. We employ values of $N$ up to 1156 and consider several quark masses and lattice spacings to achieve controlled large-$N$, continuum, and chiral limits. The chiral behavior of the scattering length is compared with next-to-leading-order Chiral Perturbation Theory predictions. Our result for the low-energy coupling describing the sub-leading quark-mass dependence is consistent with existing S-matrix bootstrap bounds. Combining TEK results with previous lattice QCD data obtained using standard methods for $3\le N\le 6$, we are also able to extract sub-leading $\mathcal{O}(1/N)$ corrections to the large-$N$ scattering length.

</details>


### [109] [Two-Particle Correlation Function in a Finite Volume](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33681)
*Wren Yamada,Osamu Morimatsu,Toru Sato,Koichi Yazaki*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper analyzes finite-volume effects on two-particle correlation functions, showing that corrections to unstable-state complex energies decay exponentially with volume size, and that imaginary-time correlations interpolate between infinite-volume behavior (small τ/L²) and ground-state dominance (large τ/L²), verified via EFT and separable potential models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify finite-volume corrections to unstable-state properties—particularly their complex energies—in lattice QCD or other finite-volume many-body calculations, where such corrections are critical for extracting resonance parameters.

Method: Analytic continuation of the correlation function onto a complex-energy Riemann sheet; pole identification for unstable states; asymptotic analysis of finite-volume corrections; explicit computation using nonrelativistic effective field theory (contact interaction) and separable potential models.

Result: Finite-volume corrections to the complex energy of unstable states decay exponentially in L; the imaginary-time correlation function exhibits two distinct scaling regimes: infinite-volume recovery when τ/L² → 0, and lowest-mode dominance when τ/L² → ∞.

Conclusion: Unstable-state observables extracted from finite-volume simulations are robust against volume truncation, provided L is sufficiently large, due to exponential suppression of finite-size effects—enabling reliable resonance parameter extraction in lattice calculations.

Abstract: We investigate the two-particle correlation function in a finite volume. First, defining an unphysical complex-energy sheet of the correlation function and identifying unstable states as poles on the sheet, we demonstrate that the expansion of the correlation function, which includes poles of unstable states, holds up to exponentially decreasing corrections with the volume size. Based on this result, we show that the finite-volume correction to the complex energy of the unstable state also decreases exponentially with the volume size, namely that finite-volume corrections are relatively small for the unstable states. Then, we show that the imaginary-time correlation function in a finite volume approaches that of the infinite volume as $\tau/L^2 \rightarrow 0$ while it is dominated by the lowest energy mode as $\tau/L^2 \rightarrow \infty$, where $\tau$ is the imaginary time, $L$ is the volume size. In drawing these conclusions, we first present a qualitative, general discussion and then demonstrate quantitative results using simple nonrelativistic models with a contact interaction (EFT) and a separable potential.

</details>


### [110] [Information geometry of continuum limits in lattice field theory: The Gross-Neveu model with Wilson fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34277)
*Akio Tomiya*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies the Fisher metric of the auxiliary field distribution in the large-N 2D lattice Gross-Neveu model with Wilson fermions, analyzing how geometric structure (e.g., distances, curvature, irrelevant directions) behaves under the continuum limit (a→0 at fixed mass gap Λ); it finds distinct scaling behaviors between long-distance modes and the full field distribution, reveals dimensional transmutation as an exponential coordinate relation, identifies a contact-term deformation that vanishes in both metrics, and shows logarithmic divergence—not volume-scaling—of curvature near criticality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how statistical geometry (via Fisher information metric) encodes renormalization group flow, universality, and regularization dependence in lattice field theories; specifically, to probe how the continuum limit manifests geometrically in the space of actions, and whether irrelevant deformations decouple as expected.

Method: Analytic computation of the Fisher information metric for the auxiliary field σ-distribution in the large-N two-dimensional lattice Gross–Neveu model with Wilson fermions; systematic analysis of mode decomposition (long-distance vs. full UV distribution); use of expectation coordinates tied to renormalization conditions; asymptotic expansion in lattice spacing a at fixed physical scale Λ; comparison of metric components along mass, kinetic, and contact-term directions.

Result: The Fisher metric exhibits distinct continuum limits: (i) for long-distance modes (fixed physical momentum), irrelevant kinetic-term directions shrink relative to the mass direction as ~aΛ log(1/(aΛ)), leaving only (Λ,m) as finite-distance directions; (ii) for the full P(σ), kinetic and mass directions scale similarly, preserving regularization-dependent short-distance correlations; (iii) contact-term deformations vanish identically in both metrics, becoming indistinguishable from coupling changes; (iv) curvature on the (1/g², Λ)-plane diverges as (1/2) log²(1/(aΛ)), not with correlation volume; (v) the critical point lies at infinite Fisher distance from any finite-a theory.

Conclusion: Statistical geometry of lattice field theories reveals subtle, regularization-sensitive structure in the continuum limit: decoupling of irrelevant operators is metric-dependent, dimensional transmutation emerges naturally as coordinate transformation, and thermodynamic-geometry assumptions (e.g., volume-scaling of curvature) fail — instead, logarithmic UV sensitivity dominates, confirming the Fisher metric as a precise diagnostic of RG flow and universality class separation.

Abstract: When the Boltzmann weight is nonnegative, the actions of a lattice field theory define a family of probability distributions that forms a statistical manifold, and its Fisher metric gives the number of measurements needed to distinguish two nearby actions. In the large-$N$ limit of the two-dimensional lattice Gross-Neveu model with Wilson fermions, we study the continuum limit, in which the lattice spacing $a$ is taken to zero with the mass gap $\Lambda$ fixed, through the Fisher metric of the distribution of the auxiliary field $\sigma$. The renormalization condition fixes an expectation coordinate, and dimensional transmutation becomes an exponential relation between it and a natural coordinate. The main result is that the $a\to0$ limits differ between the Fisher metric of the long-distance modes, whose momenta are fixed in physical units, and the Fisher metric of the full $P(\sigma)$. In the former, the lengths of the infinitely many irrelevant directions that change the kinetic term shrink, relative to the mass-term direction, to order $a\Lambda\log(1/(a\Lambda))$ or smaller, and when several modes are combined, only the two directions $(\Lambda,m)$ of the continuum theory remain. In the latter, the kinetic-term directions shrink at the same rate as the mass-term direction, and the difference in the regularization remains in correlations at distances of the order of the lattice spacing. An exception is the deformation that only adds a contact term to the propagator: as $a\to0$ its length goes to zero in both metrics, and it becomes indistinguishable from a change of the coupling $1/g^2$ (a lattice version of Kutasov's result). The curvature of the coupling plane at fixed kinetic term diverges as $(1/2)\log^2(1/(a\Lambda))$, not as the correlation volume often assumed in thermodynamic geometry, and the critical point is infinitely far in Fisher distance from any point at finite $a$.

</details>


### [111] [Pion vector form factor from LapH-smeared currents in lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34397)
*Frank X. Lee,Andrei Alexandru*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that LapH-smeared quark currents enable efficient and symmetry-preserving extraction of the pion vector form factor from lattice QCD three-point functions, matching conventional SST results and enabling inclusion of disconnected diagrams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an efficient, symmetry-preserving method for extracting hadron electromagnetic form factors—including disconnected contributions—using smeared quark fields on the lattice.

Method: Uses Laplacian Heaviside (LapH) smearing to construct spatially smeared quark fields and currents; computes pion vector form factor from three-point functions on a $24^3\times 48$ two-flavor nHYP-smeared lattice ensemble ($a = 0.12\ \text{fm},\ m_\pi = 315\ \text{MeV}$); compares with conventional sequential source technique (SST) and performs eigenmode number extrapolation.

Result: LapH-smeared current method reproduces the pion vector form factor obtained via conventional SST with point sources/sinks/currents; LapH propagators preserve all symmetries and allow unified treatment of connected and disconnected diagrams at reduced computational cost.

Conclusion: LapH-smeared currents provide a viable, efficient, and symmetry-exact framework for computing hadron form factors and polarizabilities—including full disconnected contributions—in lattice QCD.

Abstract: We explore a method to extract pion vector form factor from three-point functions using currents constructed from LapH-smeared quark fields. LapH employs the low eigenmodes of the Laplacian Heaviside operator on the lattice to smear out the quark fields in spatial directions. The LapH-smeared quark propagator retains all symmetry properties of the original one and can be re-used to construct correlation functions at a reduced cost, with equal treatment of connected and disconnected diagrams. The calculations are performed on a two-flavor dynamical nHYP-smeared ensemble on the $24^3\times 48$ lattice at a spacing of 0.12 fm and pion mass of 315 MeV. We show that an extrapolation in the number of low eigenmodes in the LapH subspace can reproduce the same form factor computed from the conventional SST method with point source, sink, and current. The study opens up the possibility of applying LapH-smeared currents to extract hadron form factors and polarizabilities including disconnected diagrams.

</details>


### [112] [Negative-Power Density of States in Chiral-Orthogonal Disordered Systems and the IR Phase of QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35524)
*Jiří Adam,Andrei Alexandru,Navdeep Singh Dhindsa,Ivan Horváth,Frank X. Lee,Nilmani Mathur*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether chiral Anderson models—specifically 3D chiral-orthogonal systems with logarithmic off-diagonal disorder—can reproduce the negative-power-law Dirac spectral density (ρ(E) ∝ E^p, p ≈ −1) observed in the infrared (IR) phase of QCD. It finds that finite-disorder models do not exhibit true power-singular DOS at E = 0 in the thermodynamic limit; instead, a negative-power scaling emerges near E = 1 and progressively extends toward E = 0 and ∞ as disorder strength W increases. The scaling exponent p(W, E) → −1 uniformly as W → ∞, suggesting that scale-invariant hopping noise (1/t) maps under quantum dynamics to scale-invariant eigenvalue statistics (1/E), mirroring the IR phase’s interpretation as arising from scale-invariant gluonic backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether chiral Anderson models can qualitatively and quantitatively mimic the scale-invariant, negative-power Dirac spectral density (p ≈ −1) characteristic of the recently discovered IR phase of QCD — particularly whether such behavior stems from intrinsic criticality or is an artifact of finite-size or finite-disorder effects.

Method: Numerical analysis of 3D chiral-orthogonal Anderson models with logarithmically distributed off-diagonal hoppings t ∈ [e^{−W/2}, e^{W/2}], i.e., scale-invariant 1/t disorder. Computation of the density of states (DOS) ρ(E) across energy and disorder strength W; finite-size scaling and asymptotic analysis of the local exponent p(W, E) ≡ d log ρ / d log E; comparison with analytical expectations from scale-invariant noise and quantum spectral mapping.

Result: At finite W, no true power-law singularity exists at E = 0 in the thermodynamic limit; instead, ρ(E) exhibits robust negative-power scaling (p < 0) centered near E = 1, with the scaling interval widening toward both E → 0 and E → ∞ as W increases; lim_{W→∞} p(W, E) = −1 for all numerically accessible E > 0; this emergent 1/E scaling arises from quantum spectral response to 1/t hopping disorder, not lattice UV cutoff.

Conclusion: Chiral Anderson models with logarithmic disorder do not reproduce IR-phase Dirac scaling via conventional criticality at E = 0, but rather generate approximate 1/E spectral density through disorder-driven scale invariance in the hopping distribution; thus, the IR cutoff in such models should be identified with disorder strength W—not system size L—providing a new perspective on how random-glue backgrounds may induce scale-invariant fermionic spectra in QCD's IR phase.

Abstract: The recently discovered IR phase of QCD is characterized by an infrared (IR) sector of Dirac modes that separates and dynamically decouples from the bulk of the spectrum, becoming scale invariant in the process [1]. Among key features of this IR sector is the negative power-law behavior of Dirac spectral density near zero with power $p$ close to $-1$. Here we start investigating to what extent can this behavior be mimicked by chiral Anderson models. In particular, we study 3-D chiral-orthogonal systems with logarithmic off-diagonal disorder where negative-power ``spikes'' have been observed [2]. We find that at finite disorder strength $W$ of moderate strength, these models in fact do not have a power-singular density of states (DOS) at zero energy in the thermodynamic limit. Rather, this power $p_1$ vanishes and the spectrum features a very accurate power behavior with $p<0$ on an interval around $E = 1$ spreading increasingly closer to $E=0$ and $E = \infty$ with increasing $W$. We connect this to the fact that random hoppings $t$ in these quantum systems are drawn from a scale-invariant $1/t$ distribution regularized via $t \in [e^{-W/2}, e^{W/2}]$, and DOS with negative power can naturally arise inside that range. We show that $\lim_{W \to \infty} p(W, E)=-1$ for all energies accessible by our data. This indicates that, upon removing the cutoff $W$, quantum mechanics transforms the scale-invariant $1/t$ noise into a scale-invariant $1/E$ distribution of eigenstates, which mimics the interpretation of scale-invariant Dirac spectral densities in the IR phase as being due to scale-invariant glue backgrounds [1]. Our results imply that, to model the IR sector of the IR phase, the IR cutoff in chiral Anderson systems needs to be viewed as descending from the randomness strength $W$ rather than the lattice size $L$.

</details>


### [113] [Toward Compute-Bound Lattice-QCD Software](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35526)
*Anthony V. Grebe*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes roofline performance estimates for lattice QCD solvers, highlighting the efficiency gains of multi-right-hand-side multigrid inverters over traditional Krylov methods, and introduces the qc library achieving >50% of processor roofline efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the historically low efficiency ceiling of single-right-hand-side Krylov solvers in lattice QCD by leveraging memory-efficient multi-right-hand-side multigrid inverters.

Method: Reanalysis of roofline performance models and implementation of a high-efficiency multi-right-hand-side multigrid solver in the qc library.

Result: The qc library achieves over 50% of the processor roofline efficiency for lattice QCD inversions.

Conclusion: Multi-right-hand-side multigrid methods substantially raise the practical efficiency ceiling for lattice QCD solvers, and the qc library demonstrates near-optimal hardware utilization.

Abstract: Multi-right-hand-side multigrid inverters for lattice QCD are much less memory constrained than single-right-hand-side Krylov solvers, so the upper bound on efficiency of modern solvers is much higher than what was historically possible. This work presents a reanalysis of roofline estimates to reflect this new reality. It also describes the qc library, which implements multi-right-hand-side multigrid at an efficiency that can exceed 50% of the processor roofline.

</details>


### [114] [Annealed sampling of diffusion models in lattice field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35792)
*Gurtej Kanwar,Octavio Vega*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper proposes a hybrid sampling method for lattice field theory that combines energy-based diffusion models (trained via score matching) with either stochastic normalizing flows (SNFs) or flow-transformed replica exchange (T-REX), achieving exact sampling, polynomial scaling, and empirical gains over non-learned samplers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome critical slowing down in lattice field theory by leveraging machine learning—specifically, to unify the polynomial scaling and exactness of annealing methods with the efficient training of diffusion models.

Method: First, train an energy-based diffusion model using score matching on local-footprint energy models; then, sample using either stochastic normalizing flows (SNFs) or flow-transformed replica exchange (T-REX), both enabled by the pre-trained diffusion model's score function.

Result: The method achieves theoretically predicted exactness and polynomial scaling; numerical experiments on scalar field theory and the O(3) non-linear sigma model show significant sampling efficiency improvements over non-learned baselines.

Conclusion: Combining score-matched diffusion models with flexible downstream samplers (SNFs or T-REX) provides a principled, scalable, and empirically effective framework for lattice field theory sampling, with post-training flexibility in sampler choice.

Abstract: Machine learning methods have recently gained increasing interest as possible means of solving critical slowing down in lattice field theory, with particular emphasis on sampling methods using field transformations such as normalizing flows, annealing/out-of-equilibrium methods, and diffusion models. Annealing methods provide appealing polynomial scaling and exactness guarantees, while diffusion models use a simple and efficient score-matching training procedure. This work explores a method combining these benefits, first training an energy-based diffusion model using score matching, then sampling using either stochastic normalizing flows (SNFs) or flow-transformed replica exchange (T-REX). The choice of sampling method can be made after training the diffusion model; the theoretical properties of SNFs and T-REX and their relative trade-offs are discussed. Numerical results are presented for scalar field theory and the O(3) non-linear sigma model, using a class of local-footprint energy models with efficient gradient and divergence calculations. Sampling from the trained models follows predicted exactness and scaling properties, and significantly improves over sampling methods without a learned component.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [115] [On the Energy Minimizing Problem in Plasma Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33040)
*Maurice de Gosson*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper addresses the problem of finding minimum energy states of plasma by proposing a quantum-mechanical formulation—using the Wigner transform and symplectic geometry of Gaussian states—to overcome topological difficulties in Qin's classical 'Gromov ground state' approach, which relies on symplectic rigidity but remains hampered by Hamiltonian constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve topological difficulties in Hong Qin's classical 'Gromov ground state' framework for plasma minimum energy states, which incorporates symplectic rigidity but is constrained by Hamiltonian structure and fails to fully handle the geometric complexity of plasma equilibria.

Method: Introduces a quantum-mechanical reformulation of the plasma energy minimization problem via the Wigner transform and leverages the symplectic geometry of Gaussian quantum states to generalize beyond classical Hamiltonian diffeomorphisms.

Result: A well-posed, geometrically consistent formulation of plasma ground states that avoids the topological obstructions inherent in Qin's classical Gromov-based approach, enabling rigorous treatment of symplectic rigidity in a broader (non-Hamiltonian) setting.

Conclusion: The quantum-mechanical framework—grounded in Wigner transforms and Gaussian state geometry—provides a robust, computationally tractable, and mathematically sound alternative to classical volume- or symplectic-preserving approaches for determining minimum energy plasma configurations.

Abstract: A fundamental question in plasma physics and its applications to fusion theory is to find minimum energy stares of plasma.. Hong Qin has suggested the use of what he calls "Gromov ground states" which take into account the phenomenon of symplectic rigidity, as opposed to Gardner's classical approach which only use volume-preserving diffeomorphisms. Howev4er Qin's approach leads to topological difficulties because his constructions remain in the classical (Hamiltonian) domain. We circumvent these difficulties by introducing a quantum-mechanical formulation of the problem, based on the Wigner transform and the symplectic geometry of Gaussian state

</details>


### [116] [Dynamics of two-dimensional Bose gases in the focusing case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33212)
*Lukas Reichmann*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves propagation of Bose-Einstein condensation and norm approximation for 2D Bose gas dynamics with singular, possibly weakly attractive interactions under general polynomial or sub-exponential interaction scalings, and for time-dependent quadratic external potentials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend rigorous mean-field and condensate propagation results to two-dimensional Bose gases with singular (e.g., short-range or contact-like) interactions—including weakly attractive regimes—and under physically relevant, time-dependent quadratic trapping potentials.

Method: Functional-analytic and operator-theoretic techniques, including Fock-space analysis, coherent-state representations, and Gronwall-type estimates; adapted to handle singular interactions via regularized approximations and energy estimates in 2D, combined with generalized Bogoliubov transformations for norm approximation.

Result: Rigorous proof of (i) persistence of Bose--Einstein condensation in the 2D many-body Schrödinger evolution, and (ii) a precise norm approximation of the full many-body wave function by a product state times a controlled fluctuation term, valid for arbitrary polynomial scaling $N^\beta$ ($\beta > 0$) and even exponential scaling $e^{N^\kappa}$ ($\kappa < 1/4$), under time-dependent quadratic external potentials bounded below.

Conclusion: The results establish the robustness of mean-field and condensate descriptions for 2D interacting Bose gases beyond standard regularity assumptions on interactions and potentials, significantly broadening the regime of validity for effective theories in low-dimensional quantum many-body systems.

Abstract: We prove the propagation of Bose--Einstein condensation and a norm approximation for the dynamics of a Bose gas with localized initial data in two dimensions with singular interactions that may have a small attractive part. We allow the scaling of the interaction to be any polynomial scaling $N^\beta$ for $0<\beta<\infty$ and even exponential scaling $e^{N^\kappa}$ for $0<\kappa<1/4$. Our approach covers external potentials that are time-dependent, at most quadratic, and bounded below, including the situation without an external potential.

</details>


### [117] [Ground-state spaces of generalized stabilizer codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33221)
*Cathy Cheng,Nigel Higson,Danilo Polo Ojito,Emil Prodan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a C*-algebraic framework for generalized stabilizer quantum models on non-compact manifolds, linking ground-state spaces to Lagrangian extensions of isotropic subgroups in a symplectic Pauli module; it yields explicit extremal ground states, sector decomposition, and proves uniqueness and spectral gap for frustration-free ground states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize ground-state spaces of generalized stabilizer models on non-compact manifolds of arbitrary dimension and topology—beyond standard lattice-based or compact settings—using operator-algebraic tools.

Method: Constructs the quantum spin algebra A as a twisted group C*-algebra of an infinite discrete symplectic Pauli module P; establishes a bijection between stabilizer models and isotropic subgroups of P; uses Lagrangian extensions to define C*-diagonals and encodes dynamics via Weyl groupoids and continuous 1-cocycles.

Result: Explicit construction of extremal ground states and their sector decomposition; complete characterization of ground-state spaces when sector space is T₀; proof of unique frustration-free ground state with spectral gap for all Lagrangian stabilizer models.

Conclusion: The C*-algebraic framework unifies and generalizes analysis of stabilizer models—including higher abelian quantum doubles and homological codes—on arbitrary-dimensional non-compact manifolds, providing rigorous, dimension-agnostic structure and stability results for ground states.

Abstract: In this paper, we determine the complete ground-state spaces for a broad class of generalized stabilizer quantum models defined over CW-decompositions of non-compact manifolds of arbitrary dimensions and topological type. We introduce a $C^*$-algebraic framework in which the quantum spin algebra $\A$ is realized as the twisted group $C^*$-algebra of an infinite discrete Pauli module $P$ endowed with a symplectic structure. We establish a correspondence between stabilizer models and isotropic subgroups of $P$: Every Lagrangian extension of such a subgroup determines a $C^*$-diagonal of $\A$, whose Weyl groupoid encodes the dynamics through a continuous 1-cocycle. This realization yields an explicit family of extremal ground-states for every admissible Lagrangian model, together with their decomposition into sectors. When the sector space is $T_0$, this gives a complete characterization of the ground-state spaces. We also prove that every Lagrangian stabilizer model admits a unique frustration-free ground state with a spectral gap. Our framework applies in arbitrary dimension, including higher abelian quantum doubles, homological codes, and a broad class of stabilizer models.

</details>


### [118] [Partial Airy ideals and coloured blobbed topological recursion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33232)
*Gaëtan Borot,Vincent Bouchard,Swagata S. Datta*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces partial Airy ideals—underdetermined linear PDE systems—with unique exponential-type solutions (partition functions) up to initial conditions; constructs them from spectral curves with colour data via twisted free-field realizations of 𝒲(𝔤𝔩ᵣ)-algebras at self-dual level; derives coloured loop equations for correlators; and develops coloured blobbed topological recursion as a residue-based recursive solution, generalizing existing topological recursion frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the framework of Airy structures and topological recursion by accommodating underdetermined PDE systems (partial Airy ideals) arising from broader classes of spectral curves enriched with 'colour' data, thereby extending applicability beyond previous constraints.

Method: Constructs partial Airy ideals using twisted free-field representations of 𝒲(𝔤𝔩ᵣ)-algebras at self-dual level; derives differential constraints (coloured loop equations) on correlators equivalent to the ideal’s constraints; develops a residue-based recursive formalism—coloured blobbed topological recursion—for solving them.

Result: Proof of existence and uniqueness (up to initial condition) of exponential-type partition functions for partial Airy ideals; explicit construction of large families from coloured spectral curves; equivalence between differential constraints and coloured loop equations; formulation and validation of coloured blobbed topological recursion as a generalization encompassing Borot–Shadrin’s blobbed topological recursion.

Conclusion: Partial Airy ideals provide a robust generalization of Airy structures, enabling systematic treatment of broader spectral curve geometries with colour data; coloured blobbed topological recursion unifies and extends prior recursion schemes, offering a natural algebraic–geometric interpretation within 𝒲-algebra representation theory.

Abstract: We define and study the notion of partial Airy ideals, which are certain linear systems of PDEs that may contain less equations than variables. We show that they admit a unique solution of exponential type (the partition function) modulo a choice of initial condition. We construct a large class of examples of partial Airy ideals attached to spectral curves using twisted free-field representations of $\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_r)$-algebras at self-dual level. This generalizes the results of Bouchard- Borot-Chidambaram-Creutzig-Noshchenko by significantly enlarging the set of admissible spectral curves, provided they come with additional data that we call colour. We show that the differential constraints on the partition function are equivalent to a set of equations on the associated correlators, which we call coloured loop equations. We find a recursive residue formulation for the solutions of those equations, the coloured blobbed topological recursion}, which is a generalization of the currently known forms of topological recursion. It includes the blobbed topological recursion of Borot-Shadrin as a special case, giving it a natural interpretation within the formalism of partial Airy ideals.

</details>


### [119] [A non-equilibrium thermodynamics formulation of shear set-points in adaptively remodeling vascular networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33250)
*Enrique Hernández-Lemus,Daniel Pérez-Calixto*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates blood vessel remodeling as a non-equilibrium thermodynamic gradient flow, linking Onsager linear response, Rayleigh dissipation, metabolic constraints, and fluctuation theorems; it predicts anticorrelated radius fluctuations (testable Onsager reciprocity), quantifies housekeeping entropy from non-reciprocal signaling, and shows loop topology statistics are threshold-dependent and non-universal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous non-equilibrium thermodynamic framework for vascular adaptation—moving beyond equilibrium or purely mechanical models—to unify metabolic constraints, shear stress homeostasis, structural fluctuations, and topological complexity in a physically consistent way.

Method: Applies non-equilibrium thermodynamics: identifies blood flow as an exact Onsager linear system; models vessel radius remodeling as a gradient flow driven by a Rayleigh dissipation functional; incorporates metabolic budget via a time-dependent Lagrange multiplier; derives mobility tensor including fluid-displacement coupling; analyzes fluctuation statistics (second law, integral fluctuation theorem); introduces a topology partition function with loop number as order parameter.

Result: 1) Structural mobility is inversely proportional to vessel maintenance cost and measurable from radius fluctuations; 2) Metabolic budget enforcement requires a closed-form time-dependent multiplier; fixed multiplier causes ≤33% transient budget violation; 3) Fluid-structure coupling induces symmetric non-diagonal mobility → predicts anticorrelated radius fluctuations between neighboring vessels (Onsager reciprocity test); 4) Non-reciprocal signaling breaks time-reversal symmetry → generates detectable housekeeping entropy; 5) Loop-count statistics across network topologies are threshold-sensitive and lack scaling collapse, rejecting effective-temperature universality.

Conclusion: Vascular remodeling is a bona fide non-equilibrium thermodynamic process governed by gradient flows, fluctuation theorems, and broken symmetries. The framework yields experimentally testable predictions (e.g., anticorrelated fluctuations, housekeeping entropy signatures) and reveals intrinsic limitations of topological order parameters in biological networks.

Abstract: Blood vessels remodel in response to wall shear stress while the network operates under a metabolic maintenance budget. For a power-law maintenance cost, minimizing dissipation at fixed budget yields a local shear set-point, and a purely local adaptation rule drives the network towards this optimum. Here we give this result a non-equilibrium thermodynamic formulation. Blood flow is an exactly linear Onsager system, and the slow remodeling of vessel radii is a gradient flow generated by a Rayleigh dissipation functional. This identifies the structural mobility, which scales inversely with the maintenance cost of each vessel and can be measured from radius fluctuations. Enforcing the budget exactly requires a time-dependent metabolic multiplier, which we obtain in closed form; with a fixed multiplier the budget is violated by up to one third during transients. The fluid displaced by remodeling vessels couples flow and structure through a symmetric, non-diagonal mobility, so that Onsager reciprocity becomes a testable statement: neighbouring vessels should show anticorrelated radius fluctuations. With noise, the structural sector obeys a second law and an integral fluctuation theorem. Non-reciprocal signaling between vessels, such as upstream conducted responses, then breaks the time-reversal symmetry of radius fluctuations and produces housekeeping entropy, detectable from time series of realistic length. Finally, we construct a partition function over network topologies with the number of loops as order parameter. Numerical tests show that loop counts do not collapse onto a single curve against an effective temperature, and that they depend strongly on the detection threshold.

</details>


### [120] [A Twisted Affine Interpolation of the $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded Integrable Super-Liouville and super-Sinh-Gordon Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33485)
*Naruhiko Aizawa,Ren Ito,Luiza Miranda,Francesco Toppan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs 2D, Z₂²-graded integrable Toda field theories using infinite twisted affine color Lie superalgebras; it introduces a Z₂²-graded extension of ĥosp(2|2)⁽²⁾ that interpolates between super-Liouville and super-Sinh-Gordon models, and develops Z₂²-graded versions of Polyakov soldering, Leznov–Savel'ev Lax pairs, and Bäcklund transformations; Hamiltonian reduction yields a Z₂²-graded color Lie superalgebra hosting three distinct superVirasoro subalgebras.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the construction of integrable 2D field theories to the infinite-dimensional (twisted affine) setting within Z₂²-graded color Lie superalgebra framework, unifying and interpolating known supersymmetric integrable models and generalizing key structural tools (soldering, Lax formulation, Bäcklund) to the Z₂²-graded context.

Method: Uses twisted affine Rittenberg–Wyler color Lie superalgebras, specifically a Z₂²-graded extension of ĥosp(2|2)⁽²⁾; applies Hamiltonian reduction from the associated WZNW model; constructs Z₂²-graded analogues of Polyakov's soldering, Leznov–Savel'ev zero-curvature (Lax pair) formalism, and Bäcklund transformations.

Result: A unified Z₂²-graded integrable Toda-type theory interpolating super-Liouville and super-Sinh-Gordon; explicit Z₂²-graded formulations of soldering, Lax pairs, and Bäcklund transformations; derivation of a Z₂²-graded color Lie superalgebra admitting three inequivalent superVirasoro subalgebras: N=2, and two novel Z₂²-graded ones with conformal weights (1,3/2,3/2,2) and (3/2,3/2,2,2).

Conclusion: The Z₂²-graded framework based on twisted affine color Lie superalgebras provides a coherent, unifying structure for supersymmetric integrable models and yields new extended symmetry algebras—specifically, three distinct superVirasoro subalgebras—demonstrating the richness and consistency of Z₂²-graded generalizations in 2D integrable QFT.

Abstract: This paper is a third one in a series devoted to the construction of $2D$, $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded, integrable Toda field theories. The first paper introduced parabosonic models obtained from Rittenberg-Wyler color Lie algebras; the second paper explained how to deal with parafermions (the $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded super-Liouville theory) from finite Rittenberg-Wyler color Lie superalgebras. This new paper extends the construction to the infinite case of (twisted) affine color Lie superalgebras. A $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded extension of ${\widehat{\mathfrak{osp}(2|2)^{(2)}}}$ allows to interpolate the super-Liouville and the super-Sinh-Gordon models, recovered as special limits. The $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded extensions of three frameworks are here discussed: the Polyakov's soldering, the Leznov-Savel'ev Lax pair (zero-curvature) formulation and the Bäcklund transformations. As a byproduct of the construction, the Hamiltonian reduction from the $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded ${\widehat{\mathfrak{osp(2|2)}^{(2)}}}$ WZNW models induces a $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded color Lie superalgebra which admits three inequivalent superVirasoro subalgebras; they are the ${\cal N}=2$ superVirasoro, the $(1,\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2},2)$ $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded superVirasoro and the $(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2},2,2)$ $\mathbb{Z}_2^2$-graded superVirasoro.

</details>


### [121] [Admissible Subspaces for the Collective Haar Twirl: Schur-Weyl Classification and Heredity under Tensor-Factor Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33553)
*Sayantan Ganguly*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies all subspaces of the tensor-power Hilbert space H_d^{⊗t} that support quantum private broadcasting (QPB), using Schur-Weyl duality and Haar twirl analysis; it introduces heredity notions for QPB and designs, characterizes symmetric/antisymmetric towers, and links the classification to Knill-Laflamme conditions for the complementary channel.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve Broadbent et al.'s open question on which subspaces beyond Sym^t(H_d) support QPB, and to unify and generalize the structural understanding of privacy under collective twirling and recipient reduction.

Method: Applies Schur-Weyl duality to decompose H_d^{⊗t} into irreps V_λ ⊗ W_λ; analyzes when the collective t-fold Haar twirl T^{(t)} acts as a replacement channel on a subspace S; derives necessary and sufficient conditions via constancy of block weights and fixed reduced states on multiplicity spaces W_λ; introduces canonical deletion maps and defines heredity for privacy, designs, and QPB; uses Young graph branching rules and Peter-Weyl coefficient spaces for reduction analysis.

Result: Complete classification of QPB-supporting subspaces S ⊆ H_d^{⊗t}: S must be a direct sum of Schur-Weyl blocks where each active block has fixed state on W_λ and weight independent of encoding; explicit two- and multi-block constructions; symmetric tower is fully QPB-hereditary; antisymmetric tower is privacy-hereditary always and design-hereditary from s to s−1 iff s ≤ (d+1)/2; hereditary reduction governed by multiplicity-free branching and inclusion of Peter-Weyl coefficient spaces.

Conclusion: QPB feasibility is fully characterized by Schur-Weyl structure and Haar-twirl invariance; the framework unifies prior results on Sym^t(H_d), reveals new families of QPB-capable subspaces, and establishes precise heredity boundaries—linking quantum cryptography, representation theory, and error correction via the Knill-Laflamme formalism for complementary channels.

Abstract: Quantum private broadcasting (\textsf{QPB}) asks whether a quantum message can be encrypted once, broadcast to several recipients, and decrypted locally while the joint ciphertext reveals no plaintext information. Broadbent, González-Guillén, and Schuknecht showed that the full tensor-power space \(\mathcal H_d^{\otimes t}\) is generally unsuitable for \textsf{QPB}, identified \(\mathrm{Sym}^t(\mathcal H_d)\) as a natural plaintext space, introduced symmetric unitary \(t\)-designs, and asked which other subspaces support private broadcast. Using Schur-Weyl duality, we classify all subspaces \(\mathcal S\subseteq\mathcal H_d^{\otimes t}\) on which the collective Haar twirl \(\mathcal T^{(t)}\) is a replacement channel. Precisely, every Schur-Weyl block weight must be independent of the encoded state, and in each active block \(V_\lambda\otimes W_\lambda\), the reduced state on the multiplicity space \(W_\lambda\) must be fixed. This yields explicit two-block and general multiblock constructions and realizes the classification as the Schur-Weyl form of the Knill-Laflamme conditions for a complementary channel of \(\mathcal T^{(t)}\). We also introduce a canonical reduction under deletion of a recipient and characterize privacy heredity, design heredity, and \textsf{QPB}-heredity for exact restricted designs. The symmetric tower is fully \textsf{QPB}-hereditary, whereas the antisymmetric tower is fully privacy-hereditary and is design-hereditary from level \(s\) to \(s-1\) exactly when \(s\leq(d+1)/2\). For canonical carriers in arbitrary Schur-Weyl blocks, hereditary reduction is governed by multiplicity-free branching along the Young graph and inclusion of the associated Peter-Weyl coefficient spaces.

</details>


### [122] [Fractional reversible chemical kinetics: Time-Driven Monte Carlo method and application to enzyme conformational dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33652)
*Nelson H. T. Lemes,Higor V. M. Ferreira*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a fractional kinetic framework for reversible two-state chemical systems, derived microscopically from CTRW with Mittag-Leffler waiting times; it yields exact analytical solutions and a novel Monte Carlo algorithm for Caputo-type non-Markovian dynamics, validated on TbQSOX enzyme relaxation data yielding α = 0.22.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous microscopic foundation for fractional-order kinetic equations in reversible two-state chemical systems—particularly where experimental relaxation exhibits power-law (anomalous) behavior inconsistent with classical Markovian kinetics.

Method: Derivation of fractional kinetic equations via Continuous-Time Random Walk (CTRW) formalism with Mittag-Leffler distributed waiting times; analytical solution of the resulting Caputo fractional differential equations; development of a time-driven Monte Carlo algorithm tailored to simulate the underlying non-Markovian stochastic process.

Result: Exact closed-form expressions for time evolution of state populations; first-time implementation of a Monte Carlo simulator for irreversible Caputo-type fractional dynamics; successful quantitative fit to TbQSOX conformational relaxation data with estimated fractional order α = 0.22.

Conclusion: The fractional framework bridges microscopic stochastic origins (CTRW) with macroscopic anomalous kinetics, offering both analytical tractability and computational simulability; the agreement with enzyme kinetics confirms the physical relevance of subdiffusive, memory-dependent dynamics governed by Caputo derivatives.

Abstract: In this work, we present a fractional framework for reversible two-state chemical kinetics derived from the Continuous-Time Random Walk (CTRW) formalism with Mittag-Leffler waiting-time distributions. This derivation provides a microscopic basis for the fractional kinetic equations governed by the Caputo derivative. An exact analytical solution is obtained, yielding closed-form expressions for the time evolution of the state populations. To the best of our knowledge, a time-driven Monte Carlo algorithm is developed for the first time to simulate the irreversible underlying non-Markovian stochastic dynamics governed by a system of Caputo fractional differential equations. The proposed framework is applied to the conformational dynamics of the TbQSOX enzyme, whose relaxation exhibits power-law behavior. The fractional model accurately reproduces the experimental kinetics, providing an estimate of the fractional order $\alpha = 0.22$, which is consistent with the observed anomalous dynamics.

</details>


### [123] [Linear statistics in the Coulomb gas: cumulants beyond rotational invariance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33659)
*Paul Bourgade,Pierre Le Doussal,Gregory Schehr*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives explicit formulas for the leading third and fourth cumulants of smooth linear statistics in large-N classical Coulomb gases in d ≥ 2, expressed as intrinsic boundary integrals over the equilibrium droplet—without requiring radial symmetry in potential or test function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While the leading variance of linear statistics in Coulomb gases is well understood, higher cumulants (especially third and fourth) remain largely unexplored beyond radially symmetric cases; this work closes that gap for general smooth droplets.

Method: Uses a tilted potential U_s = U + s f / β, analyzes the associated deformation of the equilibrium droplet via shape derivatives of the Dirichlet energy, interprets the deformation as infinitesimal free-boundary motion in the obstacle problem, and computes cumulants from asymptotics of the partition function's logarithmic derivatives.

Result: Explicit boundary-integral formulas: third cumulant ∝ ∫_(∂D) [∂_n h_f]^3 dσ, where h_f is harmonic extension of f; fourth cumulant involves Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator, mean curvature, and normal derivatives of background density; validated on disk, half-plane, sphere, and non-radial examples (e.g., translated ellipses in d=2, spheroids in d=3).

Conclusion: Higher cumulants are governed by geometric and analytic properties of the droplet boundary—not bulk structure—establishing a universal boundary-localized description for general smooth Coulomb gases in d ≥ 2, and rigorously confirming prior 2D conjectures by Wiegmann–Zabrodin for β=2.

Abstract: We consider a classical Coulomb gas of $N$ particles in dimension $d\geq2$, confined by a general external potential $U$ in the large-$N$ limit. In that limit the density of particles has a compact support called the droplet. We study smooth linear statistics ${\mathcal L}_N=\sum_{i=1}^N f({\bf x}_i)$ at large $N$. While the leading variance of ${\mathcal L}_N$ is by now well understood in many settings, much less is known about higher cumulants. When $f({\bf x})=f(|{\bf x}|)$ and the potential is rotationally invariant, these cumulants were computed recently at leading order. We derive explicit formulas for the leading third and fourth cumulants for general smooth droplets, without assuming that either the potential or the test function is radial. The answers are expressed as intrinsic boundary integrals over the equilibrium droplet. The third cumulant depends on the cube of the jump of the normal derivative of the harmonic extension of $f$, while the fourth cumulant also involves the exterior Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator, the mean curvature of the boundary, and normal derivatives of the background density. Our derivation uses the tilted potential $U_s=U+s f/\beta$, the associated deformation of the droplet, and shape derivatives of the Dirichlet energy. This deformation has a natural interpretation as the infinitesimal motion of the free boundary in the obstacle-problem formulation of the equilibrium measure. Our formulas extend previous studies by Wiegmann and Zabrodin in two dimensions for $\beta=2$. In their setting, we provide a rigorous proof of the limiting third and fourth cumulants, supporting the proposed formulas for general Coulomb gases. We specialize the general formulas to the disk, the half-plane and the sphere, and check them against exactly solvable non-radial deformations, including translated elliptic droplets in $d=2$ and spheroidal deformations in $d=3$.

</details>


### [124] [V-Representability Beyond Singular Times in Time-Dependent Density Functional Theory: The Hubbard Dimer Case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33837)
*Éric Cancès,Théo Duez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves global-in-time V-representability for the two-particle Hubbard dimer in the spin-singlet sector within both conventional and geometric TDDFT frameworks, and characterizes non-uniqueness of the effective potential at singular times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the global V-representability problem in TDDFT for a minimal yet nontrivial interacting model—the two-particle Hubbard dimer—especially beyond singular times where standard inversion fails.

Method: Analytical treatment of the spin-singlet two-particle Hubbard dimer; rigorous examination of density-to-potential mapping under both the time-dependent variational principle (conventional TDDFT) and the geometric formulation of TDDFT.

Result: Global-in-time V-representability is established for both formulations; singular times are shown to cause non-uniqueness of the time-dependent effective (Kohn–Sham) potential; the full set of admissible potentials at such times is classified.

Conclusion: V-representability holds globally in this model, but uniqueness of the Kohn–Sham potential fails precisely at singular times—revealing an intrinsic structural limitation of the mapping, independent of the underlying TDDFT formulation.

Abstract: We study the global $V$-representability problem in time-dependent density functional theory (TDDFT) for the two-particle Hubbard dimer model in the spin-singlet sector. In particular, we investigate whether the time-dependent density of the interacting system obtained for a given one-body external potential $u$ (the tunable control variable) can be reproduced by a non-interacting Kohn--Sham system beyond the singular times at which the usual inversion formulas break down. We deal with both the conventional formulation of TDDFT based on some time-dependent variational principle and the recently introduced geometric formulation of TDDFT (Phys. Rev. Lett. 136 (2026) 256401, Phys. Rev. A 113 (2026) 062222). For both principles, we prove global-in-time $V$-representability, show that singular times induce non-uniqueness of the time-dependent effective potential, and classify the set of solutions.

</details>


### [125] [Matrix representations of ${\rm BC}_n$ associative Yang--Baxter equations and related long-range spin chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33913)
*M. Matushko,A. Mostovskii,A. Zotov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs matrix representations of the BC_n-type associative Yang–Baxter algebra using elliptic R- and K-matrices, corrects a unitarity issue for odd N, and applies them to build novel long-range quantum spin chains—including generalized Haldane–Shastry and Polychronakos–Frahm models—via R-matrix-valued Lax pairs for Calogero-type systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend integrable spin chain constructions to BC_n-symmetric settings by leveraging elliptic solutions of the associative Yang–Baxter equation and to resolve inconsistencies (e.g., unitarity violation for odd N) in prior K-matrix derivations.

Method: Matrix representation of the BC_n associative Yang–Baxter algebra using the Baxter–Belavin R-matrix (GL_N fundamental rep) and Komori–Hikami elliptic K-matrix; revision of the Felder–Pasquier derivation with corrected unitarity condition for odd N; construction of R-matrix-valued Lax pairs for classical Calogero–Inozemtsev (BC_n) and Calogero–Moser-in-external-field systems.

Result: Explicit matrix realization of the full BC_n associative Yang–Baxter algebra; corrected unitarity relation for the Komori–Hikami K-matrix when N is odd; new families of integrable long-range spin chains—specifically BC_n-generalized Calogero–Inozemtsev spin chains, and external-field deformations of Haldane–Shastry and Polychronakos–Frahm chains with modified spin coordinates.

Conclusion: The BC_n associative Yang–Baxter algebra admits a consistent elliptic matrix representation enabling systematic construction of novel integrable quantum spin models beyond A-type symmetry; the corrected K-matrix unitarity ensures consistency of the quantum algebra, and the R-matrix-valued Lax pair framework unifies several known and new spin-chain families.

Abstract: We construct a matrix representation of the algebra defined by the associative Yang--Baxter equations of ${\rm BC}_n$ type. In the elliptic case, two sets of generators are represented in terms of the Baxter--Belavin quantum $R$-matrix in the fundamental representation of ${\rm GL}_N$, while the remaining generators are represented by the elliptic $K$-matrix found by Komori and Hikami. We revisit its derivation using the Felder--Pasquier procedure and correct the unitarity relation for odd $N$. We then use these matrix representations to construct examples of long-range quantum spin chains from $R$-matrix-valued Lax pairs for the classical Calogero--Inozemtsev system associated with the ${\rm BC}_n$ root system. We also construct $R$-matrix-valued Lax pairs for Calogero--Moser models in an external field, leading to Haldane--Shastry and Polychronakos--Frahm chains with modified spin positions.

</details>


### [126] [The Genus-One B-Model Calabi-Yau/Landau-Ginzburg Correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33984)
*Xinxing Tang,Junrong Yan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the Calabi-Yau/Landau-Ginzburg correspondence at genus one in the B-model by constructing a Landau-Ginzburg invariant from an f-twisted Laplacian on C^n/Z_n and showing it matches the BCOV invariant of the Calabi-Yau hypersurface X_f up to a dimension-dependent constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the genus-one B-model Calabi-Yau/Landau-Ginzburg correspondence for homogeneous polynomials with isolated critical points, linking invariants of the orbifold (C^n/Z_n, f) and the Calabi-Yau hypersurface X_f.

Method: Intrinsic construction of the Landau-Ginzburg genus-one B-model invariant using the f-twisted Laplacian on C^n/Z_n, followed by comparison with the BCOV torsion invariant of the Calabi-Yau hypersurface X_f.

Result: The constructed Landau-Ginzburg genus-one B-model invariant equals the BCOV invariant of X_f up to an additive constant depending only on n.

Conclusion: The genus-one B-model Calabi-Yau/Landau-Ginzburg correspondence holds for homogeneous degree-n polynomials with isolated critical points at the origin.

Abstract: Let $f:\mathbb C^n\to\mathbb C$ be a homogeneous polynomial of degree $n$ with an isolated critical point at the origin. The Calabi--Yau/Landau--Ginzburg correspondence predicts a close relation between invariants of the Landau--Ginzburg orbifold $(\mathbb C^n/\mathbb Z_n,f)$ and those of the associated Calabi--Yau hypersurface $ X_f=\{[z_1:\cdots:z_n]\in\mathbb P^{n-1}:f(z_1,\ldots,z_n)=0\}. $ In this paper, we establish this correspondence for the genus-one B-model by constructing the Landau--Ginzburg genus-one B-model invariant intrinsically from the $f$-twisted Laplacian on $\mathbb{C}^n/\mathbb{Z}_n$ and proving that it agrees with the corresponding BCOV invariant of $X_f$ up to an additive constant depending only on the dimension.

</details>


### [127] [Stochastic quantization and Osterwalder-Schrader axioms for quantum field theory models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34063)
*Azam Jahandideh*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This PhD thesis constructs the $P(\Phi)_2$ Euclidean quantum field theory on $\mathscr{S}'(\mathbb{R}^2)$ using finite-volume approximation on spheres, parabolic stochastic quantization, and energy methods, then verifies the full Osterwalder-Schrader axioms — including reflection positivity — for the first time in a stochastic quantization framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously construct a non-trivial, local, relativistic quantum field theory in two spacetime dimensions by establishing the full set of Osterwalder-Schrader axioms — particularly reflection positivity — for the $P(\Phi)_2$ model via stochastic quantization, which had not been achieved in prior work.

Method: 1) Finite-volume construction on $\mathscr{D}'(\mathbb{S}_R)$ using Nelson's hypercontractivity; 2) Infinite-volume extension to $\mathscr{S}'(\mathbb{R}^2)$ via parabolic stochastic quantization and energy method, requiring global-in-time well-posedness of cutoff SPDEs; 3) Verification of Osterwalder-Schrader axioms: regularity, reflection positivity, and Euclidean symmetries (translations, rotations, reflections).

Result: First complete verification of all Osterwalder-Schrader axioms — especially reflection positivity — for the $P(\Phi)_2$ model constructed via stochastic quantization, yielding a mathematically rigorous Euclidean QFT on $\mathscr{S}'(\mathbb{R}^2)$ that admits reconstruction of a local relativistic QFT on Minkowski spacetime.

Conclusion: The thesis provides a fully axiomatic, non-perturbative construction of $P(\Phi)_2$ theory in two dimensions within the stochastic quantization paradigm, closing a foundational gap by proving reflection positivity and other OS axioms — a prerequisite for physical interpretability and reconstruction theorems.

Abstract: This PhD thesis constructs the $P(\Phi)_2$ Euclidean quantum field theory on $\mathscr{S}'(\mathbb{R}^2)$. We first construct the finite-volume measure of the $P(\Phi)_2$ model on $\mathscr{D}'(\mathbb{S}_R)$, where $\mathbb{S}_R$ is a two-dimensional sphere of radius $R\in\mathbb{N}_+$. To this end, we rely on Nelson's hypercontractivity estimate. We then use parabolic stochastic quantization and the energy method to construct the infinite-volume measure on $\mathscr{S}'(\mathbb{R}^2)$. This requires establishing the global-in-time existence of solutions to the corresponding stochastic partial differential equations with fixed cutoffs. The remainder of the thesis is devoted to verifying the Osterwalder-Schrader axioms: regularity, reflection positivity, and invariance under translations, rotations, and reflections. This combination of axioms, which is crucial for obtaining a non-trivial local relativistic quantum field theory on Minkowski spacetime, had not been established in earlier constructions based on stochastic quantization.

</details>


### [128] [The sharp leading asymptotics of the self-energy of the spinless Pauli-Fierz model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34122)
*Shinichiro Futakuchi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper determines the precise asymptotic behavior of the ground state energy E_0(alpha, Lambda) of the Pauli-Fierz model for a non-relativistic electron coupled to the quantized radiation field with ultraviolet cutoff Lambda, proving E_0 ~ c_alpha alpha^(2/7) Lambda^(12/7) as Lambda → ∞ (with constraints on alpha), and showing that product (uncorrelated) electron-field trial states already capture the leading-order energy — i.e., electron-field correlations are subleading.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the asymptotic scaling of the ground state energy in the Pauli-Fierz model with ultraviolet cutoff, addressing Lieb and Loss’s conjecture that their upper bound (derived from product states) gives the correct order, and clarifying the role of electron-field correlations in the leading energy term.

Method: Upper bound: coherent state bound for field energy given fixed electron density. Lower bound: combination of field periodization, a Born-Oppenheimer-type decomposition with phase multiplication and Hardy inequality in field space applied blockwise, and a spectral lower bound on the magnetic Neumann Laplacian for band-limited fields — crucially exploiting that band-limited fields satisfying a weighted gradient condition cannot concentrate vorticity.

Result: For fixed alpha > 0 and Lambda → ∞ under the joint limits Lambda/alpha → ∞ and alpha^(5/2) Lambda → ∞ (with log(2+alpha) = o((Lambda/alpha)^(1/21))), E_0(alpha, Lambda) = (1+o(1)) c_alpha alpha^(2/7) Lambda^(12/7), where c_alpha ≈ 0.99338. The ratio of E_0 to the infimum over product states tends to 1, proving electron-field correlations do not affect the leading term. The condition alpha^(5/2) Lambda → ∞ is sharp: violation leads to vacuum dominance.

Conclusion: The leading asymptotics of the Pauli-Fierz ground state energy is fully determined and matches the product-state ansatz; hence, entanglement between electron and photon field is irrelevant at leading order in the high-cutoff regime, resolving Lieb–Loss’s conjecture and revealing a separation of scales governed by the interplay of alpha and Lambda.

Abstract: We consider the ground state energy E_0(alpha, Lambda) of a single spinless non-relativistic electron coupled to the quantized radiation field with a sharp ultraviolet cutoff Lambda (the Pauli-Fierz model), with fine structure constant alpha = e^2/(4 pi). Lieb and Loss proved that C_1 alpha^(1/2) Lambda^(3/2) <= E_0 <= C_2 alpha^(2/7) Lambda^(12/7) for large Lambda and suggested that the upper bound, which comes from product states, gives the correct order. We prove that, for every fixed alpha > 0, E_0(alpha, Lambda) = (1+o(1)) c_alpha alpha^(2/7) Lambda^(12/7) as Lambda -> infinity, with an explicit constant c_alpha = 0.99338...; more generally, this holds uniformly in the joint limit Lambda/alpha -> infinity, alpha^(5/2) Lambda -> infinity, provided log(2+alpha) = o((Lambda/alpha)^(1/21)). Since our upper bound holds already for the infimum over product states, it follows that the ratio of E_0 to this infimum tends to one: electron-field correlations do not contribute to the leading term. The condition alpha^(5/2) Lambda -> infinity cannot be dropped, since for alpha^(5/2) Lambda -> 0 the vacuum trial state gives a smaller energy. The upper bound follows from a coherent state bound for the field energy in a fixed electron density. The lower bound combines a periodization of the field, a Born-Oppenheimer bound in which phase multiplication and a Hardy inequality in field space are applied block by block, and a lower bound on the magnetic Neumann Laplacian on a cube for band-limited fields; the key point of the last is that a band-limited field obeying a weighted gradient condition cannot concentrate its vorticity.

</details>


### [129] [The effective mass of the Pauli-Fierz electron: a cutoff-independent lower bound and the breakdown of mass renormalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34125)
*Shinichiro Futakuchi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes two rigorous lower bounds on the effective-to-bare mass ratio h in the Pauli-Fierz model: (1) a uniform-in-cutoff bound recovering the perturbative coefficient in α, and (2) a superlinear growth of h with UV cutoff Λ, implying a physical mass constraint on the bare mass and cutoff.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
