Motivation: To rigorously justify the expected physical constraint that a fixed physical electron mass can only be reproduced by the Pauli-Fierz model for UV cutoffs below a critical value depending on α — a claim previously conjectured but unproven by Lieb and Loss (2002).

Method: Uses variational trial states: (i) a coherent field translation of the ground state (for the first bound), exploiting minimality to cancel linear terms; (ii) a phase-modified ground state dependent on field configuration (for the second bound), combined with novel geometric tools — a field-based estimator for electron position (via shallow level-set center), divergence-free external subspace decoupling, and rarity analysis of shallow depressions near localization edge — building on sharp self-energy asymptotics from a companion paper.

Result: Two proven lower bounds: (1) h ≥ 1 + 0.3441α/(1 + 0.2985√α + 0.1252α) for all α > 0, λ ≥ 1; (2) for each fixed α, h ≥ λ^(8/7−ε) for large λ, implying ι_PF(α) := inf_λ>0 h/λ > 0 and thus Λ ≤ m_phys/ι_PF(α) for consistent physical mass reproduction.

Conclusion: The paper provides the first rigorous proof that the Pauli-Fierz effective mass diverges superlinearly with UV cutoff, confirming the non-perturbative necessity of a finite cutoff to match a given physical mass — resolving a long-standing open problem posed by Lieb and Loss.

Abstract: We consider the Pauli-Fierz model of one spinless non-relativistic electron coupled to the quantized transverse electromagnetic field, without external potential, with a sharp ultraviolet cutoff Lambda and bare mass m. Let h = m_eff/m be the ratio of the effective mass, defined by the curvature of the fiber ground state energy at total momentum zero, to the bare mass; by scaling, h depends only on the fine structure constant alpha = e^2/(4 pi) and on lambda = Lambda/m. We prove two lower bounds. First, h >= 1 + 0.3441 alpha/(1 + 0.2985 sqrt(alpha) + 0.1252 alpha) for all alpha > 0 and all lambda >= 1; the bound does not depend on the cutoff, and with the band extended to the cutoff its first order coefficient in alpha is the perturbative one of Hiroshima and Spohn. The proof uses a coherent translation of the field applied to the ground state as a trial state; the term linear in the translation vanishes by minimality of the ground state. Second, for every fixed alpha, h grows faster than linearly: for every epsilon > 0, h >= lambda^(8/7 - epsilon) for all large lambda (8/7 is the limit of the method). Consequently iota_PF(alpha) := inf_{lambda > 0} h/lambda > 0, and for a fixed physical mass m_phys a bare mass reproducing it exists only if Lambda <= m_phys/iota_PF(alpha). This was expected, but not proved, by Lieb and Loss (2002). The proof of the second bound starts from the sharp self-energy asymptotics of a companion paper and uses a trial state obtained from the ground state by a phase depending on the field configuration. The new ingredients are an estimator for the position of the electron given by the local centre of a shallow level set of the photon cloud, the decoupling of a localized part of the field from the electron through a divergence-free external subspace, and a proof that shallow depressions near the edge of the localization window are rare.

</details>


### [130] [Spectral localizer for unbounded topological Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34138)
*Hermann Schulz-Baldes*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Extends the spectral localizer framework from bounded to unbounded gapped Hamiltonians (e.g., Schrödinger and regularized Dirac operators) using coercive phase space localization and wavelet-based discretization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize index pairings via the spectral localizer from bounded Fredholm modules to physically relevant unbounded gapped Hamiltonians, particularly second-order differential operators.

Method: Leverages coercive phase space localization and wavelet-based discretization to construct a finite-dimensional spectral localizer for unbounded operators; builds on prior joint work with Loring on K-theoretic index pairings.

Result: Demonstrates computability of index pairings for unbounded gapped Hamiltonians via the half-signature of a finite-dimensional spectral localizer matrix.

Conclusion: The spectral localizer framework is robustly extendable to unbounded operators, enabling topological index computations for continuum models in mathematical physics using numerically feasible, discretized constructions.

Abstract: In a series of joint papers with Loring, it was shown how index parings of $K$-theory classes with Fredholm modules can be computed as the half-signature of a suitably constructed finite-dimensional matrix, called the spectral localizer. After offering a novel perspective on earlier results, it is shown how the framework can be extended to unbounded gapped Hamiltonians given as second-order linear differential operators (in particular, matrix-valued Schrödinger and regularized Dirac operators). Important insights are the coercive phase space localization and discretizations by means of wavelets.

</details>


### [131] [Random-walk scaling without Bernoulli statistics: transport, persistence and resonances in the triangle map](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34179)
*Mirko Degli Esposti*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the triangle map—a piecewise-parabolic, area-preserving toral map with zero Lyapunov exponent—via symbolic dynamics (±1 sequences) and transport statistics (partial sums Sₙ). It establishes spectral, coboundary, and combinatorial properties of the symbol; proves anomalous diffusion (with parameter-dependent diffusion coefficient D ∈ [0.2, 14]×Bernoulli), first-passage exponent 1/2, and slow variance growth at β=0 due to resonant zero-sum words; identifies a crossover timescale β⁻¹ᐟ² and computes an exact logarithmic variance correction in asymmetric deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand transport, ergodicity, and statistical behavior in a paradigmatic zero-entropy, area-preserving dynamical system—the triangle map—which exhibits subtle deviations from Bernoulli randomness despite lacking exponential instability.

Method: Symbolic dynamics on a bipartition of the torus; spectral analysis of the Koopman operator in H¹; coboundary theory; exact arithmetic on polygonal cylinder sets; Green-Kubo formalism; combinatorial analysis of zero-sum words; asymptotic statistics of partial sums Sₙ and first-passage times; perturbation analysis near β = 0.

Result: The symbol is not an H¹-coboundary; runs are uniformly bounded; generic parameters yield normal diffusion with highly variable D (0.2–14×Bernoulli); first-passage exponent is 1/2 with non-Bernoulli amplitude; at β = 0, D = 0 but Var(Sₙ) grows subdiffusively due to resonant trapping by zero-sum words obeying a 1/ℓ law; crossover time scales as β⁻¹ᐟ²; asymmetric deformation yields a logarithmic variance term with exact coefficient; worst-case Birkhoff averages decay as in uniquely ergodic systems; Fourier mode correlations decay as n⁻⁰·².

Conclusion: The triangle map displays rich, nontrivial statistical mechanics despite vanishing Lyapunov exponent: it is neither mixing nor Bernoulli-like, yet exhibits Gaussian diffusion generically; its anomalies stem from algebraic resonances encoded in zero-sum words, suggesting deep links between symbolic combinatorics, cohomology, and transport. Conjectures are posed on unique ergodicity, mixing, CLT validity, and cohomological rigidity.

Abstract: The triangle map is an area-preserving, piecewise-parabolic map of the torus with vanishing Lyapunov exponent. We study the symbolic process $X_n=\pm1$ given by the two halves of the torus, whose partial sums $S_n$ are the transport of the lifted momentum, with an integrator free of accumulated rounding errors and exact computations on polygonal cylinder sets. We prove that the Koopman operator has no non-constant $H^1$ eigenfunctions, that the symbol is not an $H^1$ coboundary, and that runs of equal symbols are uniformly bounded. For generic parameters the walk is normally diffusive and Gaussian, but its diffusion coefficient is not that of a coin: away from the edges of the parameter square it varies between $0.2$ and $14$ times the Bernoulli value, and for a benchmark pair, with $D=3.09\pm0.01$, the excess is produced by the order of the bounded inter-event intervals, not by their distribution. The first-passage exponent is $1/2$ with a non-Bernoulli amplitude, and worst-case Birkhoff averages decay as for a uniquely ergodic system. For $\beta=0$ the Green-Kubo sum cancels, $D=0$, yet $\mathrm{Var}(S_n)$ keeps growing slowly, sign changes are rare and the correlations of Fourier modes decay as $n^{-0.2}$. We identify the zero-sum words, which become pure twists at $\beta=0$, as resonances that trap orbits with a computable $1/\ell$ law, set the crossover time $\beta^{-1/2}$ at small $\beta$, and, in an asymmetric deformation of the symbol, produce a logarithmic term in the variance whose coefficient we compute exactly. We formulate conjectures on unique ergodicity, mixing, the central limit theorem and the cohomology of the symbol.

</details>


### [132] [Semantic burstiness across principal directions of textual variation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34188)
*Mirko Degli Esposti,Marcelo A. Montemurro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a method to quantify semantic burstiness in texts by projecting pre-trained word embeddings onto PCA-derived semantic directions, revealing that long-range correlations in language arise from ordered recurrence of semantically related content—not just global rank-based correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how long-range correlations in written texts relate to semantic organization, beyond local syntax or simple word-frequency statistics.

Method: Token-wise mapping of texts into pre-trained word embeddings; PCA on embedding trajectories to extract text-specific semantic directions; thresholding projected series to define inter-event times; computing burstiness spectrum {B_k} via coefficients of variation; comparing against word-shuffled and fractional-Gaussian-noise surrogates using DFA, autocorrelation, and statistical tests.

Result: Original texts consistently exhibit higher burstiness than both controls across all 28 texts and first 20 PCA components; controls are statistically indistinguishable; autocorrelation and DFA confirm semantic projections carry structure beyond global rank-level correlations.

Conclusion: Long-range semantic correlations reflect ordered recurrence of semantically related material—not emergent properties of global rank statistics—supporting a cognitively grounded, hierarchical model of textual semantics.

Abstract: Long-range correlations in written texts extend over scales far beyond local syntax, but their relation to semantic organisation remains incompletely understood. Previous work has linked these correlations to the bursty recurrence of topically relevant words. Here we generalise that construction from individual lexical items to distributed semantic observables. We map a text token by token onto a trajectory of pre-trained word embeddings, use principal component analysis (PCA) to identify text-specific directions of semantic variation, and threshold the projected series to define inter-event times; their coefficients of variation form a component-resolved burstiness spectrum $\{B_k\}$. Across 28 literary and scientific texts and the first 20 components, the originals remain systematically above the geometric inter-event-time baseline and above two complementary controls: a word-shuffled null, which preserves the embedding covariance, the PCA eigensystem and the marginal projected distributions while destroying temporal order, and a fractional-Gaussian-noise surrogate, which preserves the Zipfian frequency structure and matches the detrended fluctuation analysis (DFA) exponent of the original rank sequence. With events defined by the upper 5% tail of each projection, the original exceeds both controls in every text-component pair, while the two controls are statistically indistinguishable from each other. Autocorrelation and detrended fluctuation analysis of the semantic projections separate the originals from both controls in the same way. Global rank-level long-range correlations are therefore insufficient to generate the bursty recurrence observed along semantic directions. The results instead support an interpretation in terms of the ordered recurrence of semantically related material.

</details>


### [133] [Boundary-condition independence of infinite-chain ground states in the $S=1$ $J$-$D$ antiferromagnetic chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34330)
*Hal Tasaki*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves convergence and equivalence of two finite-volume ground state constructions for the S=1 J-D antiferromagnetic chain near D=0, establishing a symmetric, gapped, locally unique infinite-chain ground state whose properties depend continuously on D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish the existence and properties of a symmetric, gapped ground state for the S=1 J-D model near D=0—serving as foundational input for analyzing a topological phase transition in related work.

Method: Extends analytic machinery from OpenAI's Haldane gap proofs; employs inductive estimates and uniform convergence arguments on periodic (even-length) and open (odd-length with symmetric boundary fields) finite chains; proves full-sequence convergence (no subsequences) and coincidence of limits, uniformly in D near zero.

Result: Both finite-volume ground state sequences converge uniformly in D to the same infinite-chain limit—a symmetric, locally unique, gapped ground state with continuous dependence of local expectations on D.

Conclusion: The two physically motivated finite-volume approximations yield identical, well-behaved infinite-volume ground states near D=0, validating their use in subsequent analysis of topological order and phase transitions.

Abstract: Extending the analytic machinery and using the numerical inputs from OpenAI's proofs of the Haldane gap, we study infinite-chain ground states of the $S=1$ $J$-$D$ antiferromagnetic model $H=\sum_j\{\boldsymbol S_j\cdot\boldsymbol S_{j+1}+D(S_j^{\mathrm z})^2\}$ for sufficiently small $|D|$. We consider two finite-volume settings: periodic chains with an even number of sites, and open chains with an odd number of sites and the same positive magnetic field in the $\mathrm z$ direction at both endpoints. We prove that the ground states in each setting have an infinite-chain limit along the full sequence, without passing to a subsequence, and that the two limiting states coincide. Convergence is uniform in $D$ on a fixed neighborhood of zero. The common limit is a symmetric locally-unique gapped ground state, and its local expectation values depend continuously on $D$. These conclusions concern the two specified constructions, not uniqueness among all infinite-chain ground states. They provide the input for the study of a topological phase transition in a forthcoming revision of our companion paper. The author formulated the problem and proposed the basic strategy. ChatGPT Astra made substantial contributions to the inductive estimates and to completing the proof.

</details>


### [134] [Rigorous Lower Bounds for Atomic and Ionic Ground-State Energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34480)
*Michael Hofacker*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new iterative method to compute rigorous lower bounds for ground-state energies of atoms/ions, improving the best-known constant in the asymptotic lower bound and validating its performance against prior results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve existing rigorous lower bounds on atomic ground-state energies, especially the prefactor in the N^{1/3}Z^2 scaling law, approaching the Thomas-Fermi asymptotic constant.

Method: A novel iterative scheme combining an initial energy estimate with a sharp upper bound on the maximal number of bound electrons N_c(Z); uses functional-analytic and variational techniques rooted in many-body quantum mechanics.

Result: Derives explicit lower bound E(N,Z) ≥ −0.8710 N^{1/3} Z^2, improving previous constant 1.1447; computes concrete lower bounds for E(N,N) for 2 ≤ N ≤ 86; proves uniqueness of expectation values of kinetic, potential, and interaction energies in the ground-state space.

Conclusion: The method significantly tightens rigorous lower bounds, bridges gap toward Thomas-Fermi asymptotics, and provides both quantitative improvements and structural insights (e.g., uniqueness of energy component expectations) for atomic ground states.

Abstract: We establish a new iterative method for computing rigorous lower bounds for the ground-state energies of all atoms and ions. The accuracy of the resulting bounds depends on two ingredients: the quality of the initial energy estimate and the quality of the used upper bound for the maximum number of bound electrons, $N_c(Z)$, as a function of the nuclear charge $Z>0$. In particular, this new method allows us to establish the explicit lower bound $E(N,Z) \geq -0.8710\,N^{1/3}Z^2$ for the ground-state energy $E(N,Z)$ of the atom/ion with $N \in \mathbb{N}$ electrons and nuclear charge $Z \in (0,N]$, thereby considerably improving the best known constant $A=(3/2)^{1/3} \approx 1.1447$ in the estimate $E(N,Z) \geq -A\,N^{1/3}Z^2$ for $0 < Z \leq N$ and coming much closer to the best possible constant given by the Thomas-Fermi asymptotics $\lim_{N \to \infty} |E(N,N)|N^{-7/3}= -E_{\text{TF}}(1)\approx 0.7687$ derived in Adv. Math., 23(1) by Lieb and Simon. To demonstrate the potential and the limits of our method, we also compute explicit lower bounds for the ground-state energies $E(N,N)$ of all atoms with $2 \leq N \leq 86$ and we compare them with the bounds obtained in J. Chem. Phys., 62(8) by Hertel, Lieb, Thirring and the bounds obtained by Seco in his dissertation. Finally, we also provide a uniqueness result for the expectation values of the kinetic, potential and interaction energies on the ground-state space.

</details>


### [135] [Gaussian $β$-Markov Path Spaces without a Spectral Gap and the Araki--Woods Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34783)
*Yoshitsugu Sekine*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Extends the Gaussian β-Markov path space construction for free Bose fields to one-particle Hamiltonians without spectral gap, preserving equivalence to the Araki–Woods representation; enables applications in constructive QFT at positive temperature and equilibrium statistical mechanics (e.g., BEC absence in non-number-conserving quasiparticle systems).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Gaussian β-Markov path space framework—previously restricted to gapped one-particle Hamiltonians—to gapless cases, enabling rigorous treatment of equilibrium quantum statistical mechanics and constructive thermal QFT, particularly for systems like non-conserved quasiparticles where Bose–Einstein condensation may be absent.

Method: Mathematical extension of Dereziński–Gérard’s construction using operator-algebraic and functional-analytic techniques; establishes equivalence to the Araki–Woods representation for gapless one-particle Hamiltonians via careful regularization and domain analysis of thermal KMS states.

Result: A rigorously defined Gaussian β-Markov path space for free Bose fields with gapless one-particle Hamiltonians, proven equivalent to the Araki–Woods representation; provides a framework for thermal equilibrium states in settings where particle number is not conserved.

Conclusion: The extended construction removes the spectral gap restriction, broadening applicability to physically relevant equilibrium and thermal QFT problems—including the analysis of Bose–Einstein condensation in open or dissipative quasiparticle systems.

Abstract: Dereziński and Gérard constructed the Gaussian $\beta$-Markov path space of a free Bose field in a form equivalent to the Araki--Woods representation, which suits nonequilibrium statistical mechanics with a quantum-field environment, but only for one-particle Hamiltonians with a spectral gap. We extend this construction, together with the equivalence to the Araki--Woods representation, to one-particle Hamiltonians without a spectral gap. The extension is intended for constructive quantum field theory at positive temperature and equilibrium statistical mechanics, for instance in the problem of the absence of Bose--Einstein condensation of quasiparticles whose number is not conserved.

</details>


### [136] [On the types of quasiperiodic potentials and level surfaces of quasiperiodic functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34989)
*A.Ya. Maltsev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares two classes of quasiperiodic functions—those from restrictions of smooth periodic functions and those arising in quasicrystals—using the Novikov problem to analyze their level-set geometry, revealing shared features and key differences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify conceptual and structural distinctions and commonalities between mathematically constructed quasiperiodic functions (via restriction) and physically motivated ones (in quasicrystals), which are often conflated despite differing origins and properties.

Method: Geometric analysis of level sets of quasiperiodic functions using the Novikov problem; comparison of topological and analytical properties across both function classes.

Result: Both classes share certain global geometric features (e.g., stability of foliation structure under perturbations), but differ critically in spectral properties, regularity of level sets, and realizability as physical potentials due to constraints from cut-and-project construction versus arbitrary smooth restriction.

Conclusion: The distinction is not merely technical: physical quasiperiodic potentials are constrained by underlying lattice geometry and projection rules, whereas restricted periodic functions form a broader, smoother class lacking such physical realizability conditions.

Abstract: We examine the similarities and differences between two types of quasiperiodic functions: those obtained by restricting smooth periodic functions in $\mathbb{R}^N$ to lower-dimensional subspaces $\mathbb{R}^n \subset \mathbb{R}^N$, and those that arise as physical fields or potentials in quasicrystalline structures. Our analysis reveals that functions in these two classes share a number of common features while also exhibiting significant differences. As a key tool for investigating both classes of functions, we employ the Novikov problem - specifically, the problem of describing the global geometry of the level sets of quasiperiodic functions.

</details>


### [137] [General Relativity for Mathematicians - Part I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35194)
*Filippo Fila-Robattino,Manuel Tecchiolli*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Lecture notes covering foundational topics in mathematical physics, including variational principles, special relativity, differential geometry (manifolds, connections, curvature), geodesics, and derivation of Einstein's equations from the Einstein-Hilbert action; aimed at mathematically rigorous exposition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a mathematically rigorous treatment of classical mechanics and general relativity for a mathematical audience, bridging physical intuition with formal definitions, proofs, and computations.

Method: Pedagogical exposition combining physical motivation with precise mathematical definitions, detailed proofs, and explicit calculations; structured around core theoretical physics topics grounded in differential geometry and variational calculus.

Result: A self-contained set of lecture notes suitable for graduate-level instruction, enabling rigorous understanding of Einstein's field equations via the Einstein-Hilbert action and related geometric concepts.

Conclusion: The notes successfully unify physical insight and mathematical rigor, offering a coherent pathway from classical mechanics through differential geometry to general relativity, tailored for students with strong mathematical background.

Abstract: These notes cover the first part of a course of the same title taught by Filippo Fila-Robattino and Manuel Tecchiolli at the University of Zurich in the Winter Semester of 2025. Topics include variational principles in classical mechanics, special relativity, smooth manifolds and tensor calculus, connections and curvature, geodesic motion, and the derivation of Einstein's equations from the Einstein-Hilbert action. The treatment is aimed at a mathematical audience, combining physical motivation with precise definitions, detailed proofs, and explicit calculations.

</details>


### [138] [Measurement-dependent reduction of open dynamics in the quantum phase estimation algorithm: the visible subspace and the damped Fejér kernel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35332)
*Marina O. Gordeichuk,Oleg M. Kiselev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the quantum phase estimation (QPE) algorithm under open quantum dynamics, identifying the minimal observable space needed to predict the full output distribution; it derives exact structural results using Krylov reduction, provides dimension bounds (M to M²−M+1), and introduces approximate reduction with quadratic error control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the minimal observable subspace governing QPE output distributions under general Markovian (GKLS) open dynamics—beyond ideal closed-system assumptions—enabling robust analysis of noise-affected phase estimation.

Method: Applies Krylov subspace theory to the adjoint Liouvillian generator; constructs cyclic-sum observables from the state matrix; derives exact basis via spectral/residue class decomposition modulo register dimension M; introduces finite-time Gramian-based approximate reduction with a posteriori residual error estimation.

Result: Exact dimension formula and constructive basis for the visible observable space under diagonal adjoint generators; dimension bounded between M and M²−M+1; damped Fejér kernel solution for a solvable GKLS model; demonstration that higher harmonics remain distinguishable in QPE distributions even under identical Ramsey contrast; quadratic-order error bound for approximate reduction with compatible initial states.

Conclusion: The QPE output distribution under open dynamics is fully governed by a structured, often low-dimensional, observable subspace—enabling efficient simulation, noise characterization, and error-resilient design; the framework unifies exact spectral analysis with practical approximate reduction tools.

Abstract: For the quantum phase estimation (QPE) algorithm we study the minimal space of observables that is sufficient to predict the entire output distribution under open dynamics of the counting register. Building on the general Krylov theory of reduction, we compute the structure of this space explicitly for the full Fourier measurement. The outcome probabilities are determined by cyclic sums of the state matrix. If the adjoint generator is diagonal on the matrix units, the spectral classes inside the residue classes of the index difference modulo the register dimension $M$ give a constructive basis, an exact dimension formula, and attainable bounds: the dimension of the visible space lies between $M$ and $M^2-M+1$. The class includes independent qubit dephasing, and an analytically solvable reference model of Gorini--Kossakowski--Lindblad--Sudarshan (GKLS) type yields a damped Fejér kernel; two random-unitary channels with the same Ramsey contrast show that higher harmonics are distinguishable in the QPE distribution. For approximate reduction we use a finite-time Gramian and an a posteriori residual estimate, with a quadratic error order for a reducing unperturbed subspace and a compatible initial state. Numerical calculations check the consistency of the representations and the behaviour of the error under non-Toeplitz perturbations.

</details>


### [139] [Existence of a weak solution to the Navier-Stokes equations enjoying the energy equality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35517)
*Paolo Maremonti,Alfonsina Tartaglione*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the existence of a weak solution to the Navier-Stokes equations that satisfies the energy equality—not just the standard energy inequality—by taking limits of mollified solutions (Leray's approach); however, it does not establish that all Leray–Hopf weak solutions satisfy energy equality due to lack of uniqueness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing open question of whether a weak solution to the Navier-Stokes equations can satisfy the energy equality (rather than only the weaker energy inequality) in the absence of regularity or uniqueness assumptions.

Method: Constructs a weak solution as the limit of smooth solutions to a mollified (regularized) version of the Navier-Stokes system, following Leray’s classical regularization approach; carefully controls the convergence to ensure passage to the energy equality in the limit.

Result: Existence of at least one weak solution satisfying the energy equality is established; however, it remains unknown whether *every* Leray–Hopf weak solution satisfies the energy equality, due to non-uniqueness.

Conclusion: The energy equality is attainable for some weak solutions of the 3D incompressible Navier-Stokes equations, but its universality among Leray–Hopf solutions cannot be guaranteed without a uniqueness result.

Abstract: To date, a Leray's weak solution to the Navier-Stokes equations enjoys an energy inequality. We prove the existence of a weak solution that enjoys the energy equality. The existence of the weak solution is achieved as limit of solutions to the mollified Navier-Stokes system (Leray's approach). Since no uniqueness result is known for weak solutions we are not able to claim that any weak solution in the sense of Leray-Hopf enjoys the energy equality.

</details>


### [140] [Finite-step projective dynamics of Schrödinger cocycles: non-degeneracy, regularity, and rigidity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35583)
*Mira Shamis*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes the 'no deterministic images' (NDI) condition for Schrödinger cocycles, proving its validity under broad classes of potentials—including nonconstant measurable, ergodic with infinite essential range, finite-state locally constant, bounded Markov/hidden Markov (under an L² gap), and real trigonometric (e.g., almost Mathieu). It derives Hölder regularity for solution-direction distributions and conditional stationary measures, classifies persistence for finite-state cases, provides sharp counterexamples, and links atomicity of Oseledets direction limits to Lyapunov exponent vanishing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand spectral and dynamical properties of Schrödinger operators via cocycle dynamics, particularly how randomness in potentials prevents deterministic behavior in projective transfer maps—captured by the NDI condition—and to quantify regularity (e.g., Hölder continuity) of associated invariant measures and solution directions.

Method: Functional-analytic and probabilistic techniques: spectral theory of transfer operators, mixing and ergodic theory, large deviation estimates, Hölder regularity analysis under L² spectral gaps, explicit construction of counterexamples, and analytic perturbation arguments extended to step-dependent settings.

Result: Two-step NDI holds universally for nonconstant measurable potentials over mixing systems and ergodic potentials with infinite essential range; classification of persistence for finite-state potentials; Hölder bounds on solution-direction distributions and conditional stationary measures for bounded Markov/hidden Markov potentials under a one-step L² gap; NDI and uniform-in-step Hölder bounds for all k ≥ 2 for real trigonometric potentials; non-atomicity of Oseledets direction distributions and convergence to unstable distribution for irrational frequencies and positive Lyapunov exponent; atomic subsequential limit implies zero Lyapunov exponent.

Conclusion: The NDI condition is robust across diverse potential classes, and its validity—combined with quantitative Hölder regularity—provides new criteria for positivity and vanishing of the Lyapunov exponent, unifying and extending prior results on Schrödinger cocycles.

Abstract: We study the no deterministic images condition (NDI) for Schrödinger cocycles, which excludes a common image of a fixed probability measure under almost all projective transfer transformations. We prove two-step NDI at every energy for nonconstant measurable potentials over mixing systems and ergodic potentials with infinite essential range, and classify persistence for finite-state locally constant potentials. For bounded Markov and hidden Markov potentials on general state spaces under a one-step $L^2$ norm gap condition on the Markov operator, we prove Hölder bounds for solution-direction distributions above an exponential cutoff and Hölder conditional stationary measures. Counterexamples show the essential sharpness of both assumptions. For nonconstant real trigonometric potentials, including the almost Mathieu operator, we prove NDI and Hölder bounds for distributions of solution directions at all steps $k\ge2$ and all frequencies (rational or irrational). The exponent depends on the step, while the constant is uniform in steps, frequency and energy on compact intervals. For every irrational frequency and every energy with positive Lyapunov exponent, we prove non-atomicity of both Oseledets direction distributions and convergence to the unstable distribution from every phase-independent initial probability. This gives a new criterion for a zero Lyapunov exponent: a subsequential limit with an atom for one fixed initial direction suffices. All these results extend to an analytic class, with step-dependent regularity bounds.

</details>


### [141] [Curvature-induced fracture mode coupling in doubly curved shells](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35597)
*Riccardo Brambilla,Siddhant Kumar,Bianca Giovanardi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a semi-analytical framework based on Reissner shallow-shell theory to analyze fracture behavior of arbitrarily oriented through-thickness cracks in doubly curved shells (spheres, ellipsoids, cylinders, hyperboloids, minimal surfaces) under general loading; it reveals curvature-induced coupling among opening (Mode I), sliding (Mode II), and tearing (Mode III) fracture modes—unlike flat plates—and identifies geometry-dependent coupling strength (strongest in hyperboloids, weakest in ellipsoids), including shear-induced crack closure in hyperboloids as a potential damage-resistance mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical shell fracture theories are limited to cracks aligned with principal curvature directions and simple loadings; the fracture behavior of arbitrarily oriented cracks in doubly curved shells under general loading remains poorly understood.

Method: Semi-analytical framework built on Reissner shallow-shell theory, computing stress intensity factors (SIFs), energy release rates (G), and crack-lip displacements for isotropic shells with positive (spheres, ellipsoids), zero (cylinders), and negative (hyperboloids, minimal surfaces) Gaussian curvature.

Result: Shell curvature couples Mode I, II, and III fracture responses—single loading components activate multiple modes; coupling strength depends on crack orientation and local curvature components (normal, parallel, torsional); ellipsoids show weak coupling, hyperboloids strongest, cylinders most orientation-sensitive; in-plane shear induces crack closure in hyperboloids.

Conclusion: Local curvature fundamentally governs fracture-mode coupling in shells; this framework provides a mechanistic basis for designing shellular (meta-)structures with tailored curvature distributions to control fracture behavior and enhance damage resistance.

Abstract: Classical shell fracture theories predominantly consider cracks aligned with principal curvature directions in cylindrical and spherical shells under tensile mode or pressure loading, leaving the fracture behavior of arbitrarily oriented cracks in doubly curved shells under general loading poorly understood. Here, we introduce a semi-analytical framework based on Reissner shallow-shell theory to investigate isotropic shells with positive, zero, and negative Gaussian curvature. The formulation computes stress intensity factors, energy release rates, and crack-lip displacements for arbitrarily inclined through-thickness cracks in spheres, ellipsoids, cylinders, hyperboloids, and minimal surfaces under general mechanical loading. We show that shell curvature couples opening, sliding, and tearing, allowing a single loading component to activate multiple fracture modes, unlike in flat plates. This coupling depends strongly on crack orientation and the normal, parallel, and torsional components of the shell curvature. Ellipsoids exhibit weak coupling, hyperboloids generally exhibit the strongest coupling, and cylinders display the most complex orientation-dependent responses. Moreover, in-plane shear can induce crack closure in hyperboloidal shells, suggesting a potential geometric mechanism for enhanced damage resistance. These findings elucidate the role of local curvature in fracture-mode coupling and provide a mechanistic foundation for the analysis of shellular (meta-)structures with engineered curvature distributions.

</details>


### [142] [Simplifying 2-Bundle Descriptions of Invariants and Local Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35669)
*Ferreira Bastien*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that 2-bundle formalism is not necessary for describing string coupling to a two-form gauge potential: surface amplitudes, quantum partition functions, and classical field dynamics can be recovered via simpler, finite, local, or cochain-based reductions—e.g., Fourier transformation, invertible substitutions, or direct cochain evaluation—bypassing the need for principal 2-bundles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate that the geometric complexity of principal 2-bundles—often used to describe strings coupled to non-globally-defined two-form gauge potentials—is unnecessary for recovering physical predictions (amplitudes, partition functions, classical dynamics) and topological invariants.

Method: Employs Fourier transformation for 3D finite gauge theories with cohomological actions; uses invertible local substitutions to reduce 2-connection actions (encoded by finite-dimensional crossed modules) to ordinary gauge/tensor dynamics; applies direct cochain evaluation in a 4D quadratic example where ordinary gauge-theory reduction fails.

Result: Partition functions and classical dynamics are explicitly recovered without 2-bundles: (i) 3D cohomological theories reduce to sums of ordinary finite-group gauge theory partition functions; (ii) a class of 2-connection actions reduces locally and finitely to standard gauge + tensor fields; (iii) a 4D quadratic case admits exact cochain evaluation despite absence of ordinary gauge reduction.

Conclusion: Principal 2-bundles, while geometrically insightful, are not physically indispensable—the essential observables and dynamics admit equivalent, simpler descriptions using cocycles, bundles, operators, modules, or cochain methods, eliminating redundant field variables and global gluing data.

Abstract: A string couples to a two-form gauge potential whose local descriptions need not fit together as a single global form. Principal 2-bundles provide a geometric description of this gluing. However, we recover surface amplitudes and quantum partition functions, as well as local classical field dynamics, without the 2-bundle formalism. For global phases, topological and index invariants, and twisted-sheaf moduli, established equivalences give alternative descriptions through cocycles, bundles, operators, or modules, with the required global compatibility data. For three-dimensional finite gauge theories with cohomological actions, Fourier transformation expresses the partition function as a sum of partition functions of ordinary finite-group gauge theories. An invertible local substitution reduces invariant classical actions for a class of 2-bundle connections represented by finite-dimensional crossed modules to ordinary gauge and tensor dynamics. These finite and local reductions explicitly eliminate field variables. A four-dimensional quadratic example instead admits a direct cochain evaluation even when an ordinary gauge-theory reduction fails. These results therefore do not require the additional 2-bundle presentation.

</details>


### [143] [Entropic hypocoercivity via the Polchinski flow: an application to the lattice sine-Gordon model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35759)
*Bowen Li,Jianfeng Lu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a multiscale criterion for exponential decay of relative entropy in underdamped Langevin dynamics for lattice field theories, using a phase-space Polchinski flow and Gaussian coarse-graining; it applies to smooth bounded perturbations of Gaussian fields and yields uniform entropy decay for the 2D sine–Gordon model with optimal rate O(m).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To prove exponential convergence to equilibrium (via relative entropy decay) for stochastic quantization of lattice field theories—specifically underdamped Langevin dynamics—without relying on global convexity or log-Sobolev inequalities, especially in regimes where standard functional inequalities fail (e.g., critical or near-critical models like sine–Gordon).

Method: Introduces a phase-space extension of the Polchinski renormalization group flow that commutes with free kinetic evolution; constructs scale-dependent Gaussian coarse-grainings; measures momentum dissipation transfer to position across scales; controls cross terms via entropy-bounded correctors; tracks force defect evolution to bound kinetic estimate errors.

Result: A quantitative multiscale criterion: if the smoothed interaction Hessian’s operator norm along the Polchinski flow has small L² norm in scale, then relative entropy decays exponentially at rate depending only on that norm and friction; applied to massive 2D lattice sine–Gordon with 0 < β < 4π and small coupling, yields uniform (in lattice spacing and volume) exponential entropy decay for all finite-entropy initial laws, with optimal rate ∼ m when friction ∝ m.

Conclusion: Exponential entropy decay for underdamped Langevin dynamics in lattice field theory is governed by a renormalized, scale-integrated measure of interaction curvature; the framework unifies kinetic and potential contributions via multiscale phase-space analysis and extends applicability beyond convexity-based methods.

Abstract: We develop a multiscale criterion for exponential decay of relative entropy in the canonical stochastic quantization of lattice field theories, the underdamped Langevin dynamics of the field and its momentum. For smooth bounded perturbations of Gaussian fields, the criterion requires the uniform operator-norm bound on the smoothed interaction Hessian along the Polchinski flow of Bauerschmidt and Bodineau to have a small $L^2$ norm in the scale variable. The decay constants depend only on this $L^2$ norm and the friction. The proof measures the transfer of momentum dissipation to position on the same scales that decompose entropy. A phase-space extension of the Polchinski flow gives Gaussian coarse-graining that commutes with free kinetic evolution. Integrating position--momentum cross terms over these scales yields a corrector bounded by entropy. A force defect accounts for changes in the averaged interaction force away from equilibrium; its evolution in scale controls the errors in the kinetic estimate. For the massive two-dimensional lattice sine--Gordon model with $0<\beta<4\pi$ and small dimensionless local coupling, the criterion gives entropy decay for all finite-entropy initial laws, uniformly in the lattice spacing and the volume. For friction proportional to the mass $m$, the rate has optimal order $m$.

</details>


### [144] [Hidden Singularity and Fractal Dimensions for Extended Harper Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35822)
*Simon Becker,Svetlana Jitomirskaya,Igor Krasovsky*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes improved dimensional bounds (Hausdorff and box dimensions) for the spectra of the extended Harper model in the zero-measure regime, using a novel family of unitary transformations to reduce to singular Jacobi operators and adapting the BJK method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain sharp dimensional estimates (Hausdorff and box dimensions) for the spectrum of the extended Harper model—particularly in the zero-measure regime—where classical spectral measures vanish and fine fractal geometry becomes essential.

Method: Introduces a family of unitary transformations generalizing the chiral transform (BJK), spectrally reducing each operator in the zero-measure regime to a singular Jacobi operator; then applies and extends the BJK covering method to construct efficient interval covers with controlled length and number, leveraging denominators $q_n$ of rational approximants to the irrational frequency $\alpha$.

Result: For all irrational frequencies, the spectrum has finite $1/2$-dimensional Hausdorff measure and lower box dimension $\leq 1/2$; on the isotropic critical boundary, both improve to $1/3$. Covers consist of $O(q_n)$ intervals of total length $O(q_n^{-1})$, improving to $O(q_n^{-2})$ on the boundary; packing estimates yield examples where box-counting dimension does not exist.

Conclusion: The extended Harper model’s spectrum exhibits quantifiably smaller fractal complexity in the zero-measure regime than previously known, with dimensional bounds tightly linked to Diophantine approximation properties of the frequency and critically sharpened at the isotropic boundary—demonstrating the power of spectral equivalence via generalized chiral transforms.

Abstract: We prove that, for every irrational frequency, the spectra of the extended Harper family throughout the zero-measure regime have finite $1/2$-dimensional Hausdorff measure and lower box dimension at most $1/2$. Both numbers improve to $1/3$ on the isotropic critical boundary. The proof is based on a family of unitary transformations, that specializes to the chiral transform of [BJK] for the case of the critical almost Mathieu. This family allows to make every operator in this regime spectrally equivalent to a singular Jacobi operator. Using these singular representatives, we apply the method of [BJK] to construct covers by $O(q_n)$ intervals of total length $O(q_n^{-1})$, improving to $O(q_n^{-2})$ on the isotropic boundary, where $q_n$ is the denominator of a canonical approximant to $\alpha$. The covers give upper box bounds and, with packing estimates, examples where the box-counting dimension fails to exist.

</details>


### [145] [Quantum dynamics on the sparse Erdős-Rényi graph](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35867)
*Johannes Alt,Raphael Ducatez,Antti Knowles*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the long-time quantum dynamics of a free particle on an Erdős-Rényi random graph, showing that the wavefunction starting from a localized vertex becomes approximately radial and characterizing its precise asymptotic form; it dynamically reveals coexistence of localized (bound) and delocalized (dispersive) states in the regime √(log N) ≪ d ≪ N^{o(1)}, complementing prior spectral results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the large-time quantum evolution on sparse random graphs and provide a dynamical interpretation of the recently established spectral coexistence of localized and delocalized eigenstates.

Method: Rigorous asymptotic analysis of the Schrödinger evolution generated by the adjacency matrix of G(N, d/N); exploits radial approximation, spectral localization/delocalization dichotomy, and careful time-scale separation under the condition √(log N) ≪ d ≪ N^{o(1)}. 

Result: The wavefunction asymptotically splits into a localized mode (centered at vertices with degree near 2d, corresponding to bound states) and a dispersive wave (associated with bulk eigenvalues); precise radial asymptotics are derived for times 1 ≪ t ≪ T_N with explicit T_N ≫ 1.

Conclusion: Dynamical localization emerges naturally from graph structure—specifically low-degree vertices—and coexists with dispersion, offering a time-evolution counterpart to known spectral phase coexistence in sparse Erdős-Rényi graphs.

Abstract: We analyse the large-time asymptotics of the Schrödinger time evolution of a free quantum particle on the Erdős-Rényi graph $\mathbb{G}(N,d/N)$, where the quantum Hamiltonian is given by the adjacency matrix of $\mathbb{G}$. Starting from a state localized at a vertex, the wave function is approximately radial, and we characterize precisely its asymptotic form. Our results in particular cover vertices whose degree is close to $2d$, hence probing the emergence of bound states from a dynamical perspective. This provides a dynamical counterpart of the coexistence of localized and delocalized states established in [arXiv:1905.03243,arXiv:2005.14180,arXiv:2106.12519,arXiv:2109.03227,arXiv:2305.16294], with the former giving rise to a localized mode and the latter to a dispersive wave. Our results hold in the regime $\sqrt{\log N} \ll d \ll N^{o(1)}$, which is the full region in which such dynamical phenomena may exist, and for times $1 \ll t \ll T_N$ for some explicit $T_N \gg 1$.

</details>
